## 1. Rethinking Video Autoencoders for Interactive World Models

**中文标题**: Rethinking Video Autoencoders for Interactive World Models

**作者**: Adrien Ramanana Rahary, Yutong Bai, Nicolas Dufour, Patrick Pérez, David Picard

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.30129v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30129v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30129v1)

**项目主页**: https://kyutai.org/blog/2026-10-21-cadre

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video world models predict future frames from past frames and actions. They serve as simulators in which agents train, plan or are evaluated, and that humans can control interactively. They generate in the latent space of a video autoencoder, whose compression sets their cost per frame and how long each action waits to take effect. Yet world models often reuse the autoencoders of text-to-video generators, built for offline generation with no agent waiting and, without their training data and code, not retrainable at another compression. We therefore ask how, where and how much a world model should compress. We build CADRE, an open family of causal diffusion representation autoencoders spanning multiple spatio-temporal compressions. Its members differ only in a small bottleneck on frozen features and share a diffusion decoder self-distilled to one or a few steps. We train the same world model on each autoencoder in two domains, manipulation and navigation, with two environments each. CADRE forms most of the latency-quality frontier, especially with a moving camera. Our study also leads to four findings. At strong compression, a diffusion decoder is sharper than perceptual regression. In robot manipulation, temporal compression is more effective in the autoencoder than in the world model. Manipulation and navigation call for opposite compressions: temporal compression costs little when the camera is still, and spatial compression when it moves. Surprisingly, with a still camera, coarser spatial tokens hurt motion far more than image quality. Code, models and a live demo are at https://kyutai.org/blog/2026-10-21-cadre

</details>

<details>

Surprisingly, with a still camera, coarser spatial tokens hurt motion far more than image quality. Code, models and a live demo are at https://kyutai.org/blog/2026-10-21-cadre

</details>

## 2. Attn-Forcing: Learning Full-Context Attention Proxies for Streaming Video Generation

**中文标题**: Attn-Forcing: Learning Full-Context Attention Proxies for Streaming Video Generation

**作者**: Junyao Gao, Sibo Liu, Jiaxing Li, Yanan Sun, Hanxin Zhu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.30036v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30036v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30036v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Increasing the attention context length significantly improves streaming video generation quality, while incurring prohibitive memory and computational costs as the context grows. Existing methods attempt to capture this contextual information through attention selection or compression, but remain limited by bounded representations and lack explicit attention-level supervision to retain historical contributions to current queries. To address this, we introduce Attn-Forcing, a novel framework that preserves full-context attention contributions for streaming video generation under bounded memory and computation. We achieve this by introducing attention proxies that continuously approximate the attention responses induced by the historical context. Specifically, we implement the proxies as learnable visual latents and employ a Proxy Update Module to incorporate visual context from historical latents. Moreover, we propose an Attention Matching Loss to explicitly constrain the attention behavior of the learned proxies using full-context attention supervision. Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate that Attn-Forcing consistently improves streaming video generation quality and achieves state-of-the-art performance over existing baseline methods.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Increasing the attention context length significantly improves streaming video generation quality, while incurring prohibitive memory and computational costs as the context grows. Existing methods attempt to capture this contextual information through attention selection or compression, but remain limited by bounded representations and lack explicit attention-level supervision to retain historical contributions to current queries. To address this, we introduce Attn-Forcing, a novel framework that preserves full-context attention contributions for streaming video generation under bounded memory and computation. We achieve this by introducing attention proxies that continuously approximate the attention responses induced by the historical context. Specifically, we implement the proxies as learnable visual latents and employ a Proxy Update Module to incorporate visual context from historical latents. Moreover, we propose an Attention Matching Loss to explicitly constrain the attention behavior of the learned proxies using full-context attention supervision. Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate that Attn-Forcing consistently improves streaming video generation quality and achieves state-of-the-art performance over existing baseline methods.

</details>

## 3. RealMe: Immersive Volumetric Video from Minimal Captures

**中文标题**: RealMe: Immersive Volumetric Video from Minimal Captures

**作者**: Chenxu Zhou, Zhiyuan Yu, Yudong Jin, Yinji Shentu, Yu Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.30023v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30023v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30023v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

This paper addresses the problem of creating immersive volumetric videos from sparse and low-resolution capture resources. Conventional dense multi-view reconstruction methods can produce high-quality volumetric videos, but their reliance on costly capture setups makes them impractical in real-world scenarios. Recent approaches use generative models to relax capture requirements, but still suffer from limited resolution and 4D inconsistency. To overcome these constraints, we propose RealMe, a unified framework built around a high-resolution video diffusion model that enhances limited captures into high-resolution (2K), 4D-consistent videos for volumetric video reconstruction. Specifically, RealMe constructs auxiliary data from capture videos and encodes them into latents along with layout-aware positional embeddings. The video diffusion model adapts factorized spatio-temporal attention to the multi-view capture layout, denoises these latents across viewpoints and time, and decodes them into enhanced videos to refine the 4D representation. This adaptation helps the model capture temporal dynamics and multi-view relationships, leading to more 4D-consistent generation. To reduce the data gap between generated videos and real captures, we apply a latent frequency loss that provides frequency-domain supervision in latent space. Extensive experiments on the public DNA-Rendering benchmark and our self-captured RealVV dataset demonstrate that RealMe is able to create immersive volumetric videos and outperforms state-of-the-art methods in visual fidelity and 4D consistency.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

This paper addresses the problem of creating immersive volumetric videos from sparse and low-resolution capture resources. Conventional dense multi-view reconstruction methods can produce high-quality volumetric videos, but their reliance on costly capture setups makes them impractical in real-world scenarios. Recent approaches use generative models to relax capture requirements, but still suffer from limited resolution and 4D inconsistency. To overcome these constraints, we propose RealMe, a unified framework built around a high-resolution video diffusion model that enhances limited captures into high-resolution (2K), 4D-consistent videos for volumetric video reconstruction. Specifically, RealMe constructs auxiliary data from capture videos and encodes them into latents along with layout-aware positional embeddings. The video diffusion model adapts factorized spatio-temporal attention to the multi-view capture layout, denoises these latents across viewpoints and time, and decodes them into enhanced videos to refine the 4D representation. This adaptation helps the model capture temporal dynamics and multi-view relationships, leading to more 4D-consistent generation. To reduce the data gap between generated videos and real captures, we apply a latent frequency loss that provides frequency-domain supervision in latent space. Extensive experiments on the public DNA-Rendering benchmark and our self-captured RealVV dataset demonstrate that RealMe is able to create immersive volumetric videos and outperforms state-of-the-art methods in visual fidelity and 4D consistency.

</details>

## 4. Looking Ahead: Modeling Future Evolution in Autoregressive Video Generation

**中文标题**: Looking Ahead: Modeling Future Evolution in Autoregressive Video Generation

**作者**: Ruopeng Gao, Yukun Huang, Jiarui Hu, Shuai Wang, Wenbo Hu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29989v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29989v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29989v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autoregressive video diffusion models generate videos chunk by chunk, yet producing plausible continuations does not guarantee that they capture how scenes evolve. Under teacher forcing, strong temporal redundancy may allow local visual continuity to serve as a shortcut for current-chunk denoising, reducing the pressure to model visual evolution. We introduce Future Chunk Prediction (FCP), an auxiliary objective jointly optimized with teacher-forced denoising that predicts temporally separated chunks without access to the intervening visual context. Drawing supervision directly from training videos, FCP requires no additional pretrained visual encoder and leaves autoregressive inference unchanged. Our method achieves superior physical plausibility over standard teacher-forced autoregressive training with a lower overall training compute budget, highlighting the effectiveness and efficiency of future-chunk supervision for modeling visual evolution. Intriguingly, our visualizations suggest that FCP fosters a more structured representation space, which may be valuable for building autoregressive world models.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Autoregressive video diffusion models generate videos chunk by chunk, yet producing plausible continuations does not guarantee that they capture how scenes evolve. Under teacher forcing, strong temporal redundancy may allow local visual continuity to serve as a shortcut for current-chunk denoising, reducing the pressure to model visual evolution. We introduce Future Chunk Prediction (FCP), an auxiliary objective jointly optimized with teacher-forced denoising that predicts temporally separated chunks without access to the intervening visual context. Drawing supervision directly from training videos, FCP requires no additional pretrained visual encoder and leaves autoregressive inference unchanged. Our method achieves superior physical plausibility over standard teacher-forced autoregressive training with a lower overall training compute budget, highlighting the effectiveness and efficiency of future-chunk supervision for modeling visual evolution. Intriguingly, our visualizations suggest that FCP fosters a more structured representation space, which may be valuable for building autoregressive world models.

</details>

## 5. DewHand: Direct Metric World-Space Hand Motion Reconstruction from Egocentric Videos

**中文标题**: DewHand: Direct Metric World-Space Hand Motion Reconstruction from Egocentric Videos

**作者**: Naru Suzuki, Satoshi Ikehata, Takehiko Ohkawa, Yoichi Sato, Shuhei Kurita

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29936v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29936v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29936v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recovering metric world-space hand motion from egocentric video supports understanding human interactions and transferring them to embodied agents. Three challenges complicate this task: multi-stage pipelines propagate errors from independently estimated hand and camera motion; monocular observations leave physical scale ambiguous; and occlusion and out-of-view hands admit multiple plausible motion trajectories. We present DewHand, a unified model for direct joint reconstruction of world-space hand and camera motion. To avoid compounding errors from multi-stage estimation, DewHand jointly predicts hand and camera motion in a shared world coordinate system within a single-stage pipeline. To resolve metric-scale ambiguity, it combines scene-level geometric features from VGGT-$Ω$ with metric-space supervision, while hand-specific features preserve fine-grained articulation. To handle occlusion and out-of-view hands, we formulate reconstruction as conditional motion generation with flow matching. Under the same architecture, this generative formulation outperforms direct regression in both accuracy and temporal smoothness with only two sampling steps. Experiments on HOT3D and ARCTIC demonstrate improved world-space accuracy and temporal consistency over state-of-the-art methods. DewHand reduces rigid-aligned world-space error relative to StableHand from 32.9 to 28.4 mm on HOT3D and from 76.2 to 36.2 mm on ARCTIC. It also recovers camera scale within 10% on two thirds of HOT3D clips, versus about a quarter for SLAM. Moreover, in zero-shot evaluations on HoloAssist, H2O, and TACO, captured with different devices, DewHand achieves the lowest rigid-aligned world-space error (WA-MPJPE) on each dataset.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Moreover, in zero-shot evaluations on HoloAssist, H2O, and TACO, captured with different devices, DewHand achieves the lowest rigid-aligned world-space error (WA-MPJPE) on each dataset.

</details>

## 6. CausalAlign: Causal Alignment of Video Generation Models for Consistent Ego-Motion

**中文标题**: CausalAlign: Causal Alignment of Video Generation Models for Consistent Ego-Motion

**作者**: Kai Zhu, Yintianrun Zhang, Shuwei Jiang, Ruolin Zuo, Yixin Chen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29915v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29915v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29915v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Diffusion-based video generation models (VGMs) can produce visually plausible videos, yet their camera ego-motion often drifts over long horizons. Camera ego-motion determines how the viewpoint changes between frames and is essential for maintaining consistent geometry and trajectories. To address this problem, we propose CausalAlign, a causal alignment framework with two components. First, we diagnose how precise ego motion is represented, used, and retained within VGMs using linear probes and causal interventions, revealing a rise-and-fall pattern during denoising. Second, we strengthen ego-motion state in VGM by aligning the representations at the identified layers to a 3D foundation model through delta-token space. This space encodes the changes between consecutive frames, allowing the alignment to focus on ego-motion dynamics rather than static scene content. With Wan2.2 fine-tuned on DL3DV and RealEstate10K, CausalAlign can largely improve motion accuracy, geometric consistency, and temporal smoothness, while preserving visual quality. For example, it improves geometric consistency by more than 20% relative to the base model. Moreover, with the fine-tuned VGM, CausalAlign improves the success rate of world action models (WAMs) by 10 points on mobile manipulation tasks of RoboCasa365 benchmark. Overall, CausalAlign improves ego-motion consistency in diffusion-based VGMs and yields noticeable gains in embodied models.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Diffusion-based video generation models (VGMs) can produce visually plausible videos, yet their camera ego-motion often drifts over long horizons. Camera ego-motion determines how the viewpoint changes between frames and is essential for maintaining consistent geometry and trajectories. Overall, CausalAlign improves ego-motion consistency in diffusion-based VGMs and yields noticeable gains in embodied models.

</details>

## 7. GeoMem-VLA: Internalizing 3D Scene Understanding into Temporal Memory for Vision-Language-Action Models

**中文标题**: GeoMem-VLA: Internalizing 3D Scene Understanding into Temporal Memory for Vision-Language-Action Models

**作者**: Qinfan Zhang, Yifan Luo, Haoyuan Shi, Guanlin Liu, Han Dong et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29866v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29866v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29866v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Geometric structure is essential for robotic manipulation and understanding the 3D world. Most VLA models rely on 2D representations of current observations, while geometry-aware approaches often require additional 3D information. However, geometry inferred independently from individual observations is often discarded rather than accumulated into a persistent task representation. Our key insight is that action generation needs a compact, task-relevant geometric abstraction that can be continuously updated, rather than a complete, renderable 3D reconstruction at every step. We introduce GeoMem-VLA, a VLA framework that internalizes explicit 3D supervision into dedicated geometric tokens and maintains them as temporal memory for action generation, without directly aligning the VLM's native visual embeddings with the teacher feature space. During training, a frozen geometry foundation model supervises learnable GeoPlan tokens through a geometric flow-matching objective over the current scene point map and future end-effector tracks. These tokens are integrated with the memory of observation representations, where historical information is retrieved, adaptively fused with current observations, and consolidated to retain event frames as the task progresses. At inference, the policy retrieves historical representations and fuses them with the current working representation to predict actions, without invoking the 3D teacher or explicitly reconstructing the scene. After benchmark-specific downstream adaptation, GeoMem-VLA achieves 98.9% average success on LIBERO and 91.10% / 91.24% on RoboTwin's clean/randomized settings. Across five real-world tasks, GeoMem-VLA achieves 78.0% average success. These results suggest that internalizing and remembering 3D scene understanding is a promising approach to robot control.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Most VLA models rely on 2D representations of current observations, while geometry-aware approaches often require additional 3D information. However, geometry inferred independently from individual observations is often discarded rather than accumulated into a persistent task representation. </details>

## 8. UniRAP: Towards Unified Part-level Physical Affordance Reasoning and Actionable Perception

**中文标题**: UniRAP: Towards Unified Part-level Physical Affordance Reasoning and Actionable Perception

**作者**: Linfei Li, Ruining Hu, Lin Zhang, Zhong Wang, Fengyi Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29768v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29768v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29768v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-language perception has achieved impressive progress in aligning natural language with visual observations, yet grounding high-level semantics into part-level physical interaction remains challenging. To address this gap, we propose UniRAP, a unified model for inferring part-level physical affordances and mapping language instructions to actionable geometric representations. UniRAP formulates this problem as a conditional multimodal generation task, integrating visual inputs, textual instructions, and optional prompts into a shared spatiotemporal representation space through a unified interface token mechanism. To predict executable contact geometry, we introduce a Unified Affordance Decoder (UAD), which jointly performs object detection, part-level affordance segmentation, and 4-DoF interaction pose estimation by leveraging intermediate segmentation features. In addition, we propose a curriculum-based transfer training strategy that progressively adapts the model from general visual parsing to interaction-aware perception, improving data efficiency under limited textual annotations. Experiments show that UniRAP achieves state-of-the-art performance on referring expression segmentation, affordance grounding, and interaction pose estimation, while maintaining strong spatiotemporal consistency in dynamic video scenarios. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of UniRAP as a unified perception framework for language-guided physical manipulation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Vision-language perception has achieved impressive progress in aligning natural language with visual observations, yet grounding high-level semantics into part-level physical interaction remains challenging. To address this gap, we propose UniRAP, a unified model for inferring part-level physical affordances and mapping language instructions to actionable geometric representations. UniRAP formulates this problem as a conditional multimodal generation task, integrating visual inputs, textual instructions, and optional prompts into a shared spatiotemporal representation space through a unified interface token mechanism. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of UniRAP as a unified perception framework for language-guided physical manipulation.

</details>

## 9. MS2CAST: A Video Anomaly Detection Framework via Multi-Scale Spatial Cross-Attention and Efficient Spatiotemporal Networks

**中文标题**: MS2CAST: A Video Anomaly Detection Framework via Multi-Scale Spatial Cross-Attention and Efficient Spatiotemporal Networks

**作者**: Ali Safaei, Mohammad Kazemi

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29649v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29649v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29649v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video anomaly detection (VAD) is a challenging task for surveillance systems due to the scarcity and diversity of anomalous events. While semi-supervised VAD approaches have advanced the field, many rely on computationally intensive optical flow networks to capture motion dynamics. Convolutional neural networks (CNNs) are also widely used for spatial encoding, but their effective receptive field is highly local. To address these limitations, we introduce MS2CAST, an end-to-end semi-supervised predictive VAD framework. For spatial encoding, we employ a multi-scale cross-attention encoder that fuses the hierarchical feature representations from a vision transformer (MViTv2-S) and a CNN (ConvNeXt-T) to exploit their distinct receptive fields. To model spatiotemporal dynamics without optical flow estimation, we integrate two interchangeable spatiotemporal modules in the latent space. One module (SwinLSTM) incorporates a Swin Transformer, while the other (VMRNN) integrates Vision Mamba blocks. Our objective function combines L1 loss, Gradient Difference Loss (GDL), and Multi-Scale Structural Similarity Index Measure (MS-SSIM) to ensure pixel-wise accuracy, sharpness, and structural integrity. Evaluations across the UCSD Ped2, CUHK Avenue, and ShanghaiTech datasets demonstrate frame-level Receiver Operating Characteristic (ROC) Area Under the Curve (AUC) scores that are competitive with top-tier techniques while maintaining a peak GPU memory usage of under 1 GB.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video anomaly detection (VAD) is a challenging task for surveillance systems due to the scarcity and diversity of anomalous events. While semi-supervised VAD approaches have advanced the field, many rely on computationally intensive optical flow networks to capture motion dynamics. Evaluations across the UCSD Ped2, CUHK Avenue, and ShanghaiTech datasets demonstrate frame-level Receiver Operating Characteristic (ROC) Area Under the Curve (AUC) scores that are competitive with top-tier techniques while maintaining a peak GPU memory usage of under 1 GB.

</details>

## 10. CineEvolve: An Agentic Self-Evolution Framework for Cinematic Multi-Shot Video Generation

**中文标题**: CineEvolve: An Agentic Self-Evolution Framework for Cinematic Multi-Shot Video Generation

**作者**: Jingzhe Zhang, Peixuan Zhang, Genying Li, Bing Ma, Shuchen Weng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29590v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29590v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29590v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/JingZhe-8/CineEvolve.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Cinematic multi-shot video generation requires both narrative planning and iterative refinement. Existing methods either lack narrative reasoning or rely on feed-forward workflows without policy updates. In this paper, we propose CineEvolve, an agentic self-evolution framework for cinematic multi-shot video generation that unifies a Director and a Critic within a single vision-language policy. Emulating real-world filmmaking that couples iterative direction with critical feedback, we train CineEvolve via alternating reinforcement learning, where the Director surfaces flaws to sharpen the Critic, and the updated Critic guides the Director's targeted revisions. To stabilize co-evolution, we introduce Evolutionary GRPO (E-GRPO), leveraging historical anchoring and revision safeguarding to mitigate advantage inflation. Additionally, we contribute CineTraj, a 40K cinematic trajectory dataset spanning creation, critique, and revision. Extensive experiments demonstrate that CineEvolve outperforms existing approaches. Our code will be available at https://github.com/JingZhe-8/CineEvolve.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Cinematic multi-shot video generation requires both narrative planning and iterative refinement. Existing methods either lack narrative reasoning or rely on feed-forward workflows without policy updates. In this paper, we propose CineEvolve, an agentic self-evolution framework for cinematic multi-shot video generation that unifies a Director and a Critic within a single vision-language policy. Emulating real-world filmmaking that couples iterative direction with critical feedback, we train CineEvolve via alternating reinforcement learning, where the Director surfaces flaws to sharpen the Critic, and the updated Critic guides the Director's targeted revisions. To stabilize co-evolution, we introduce Evolutionary GRPO (E-GRPO), leveraging historical anchoring and revision safeguarding to mitigate advantage inflation. Additionally, we contribute CineTraj, a 40K cinematic trajectory dataset spanning creation, critique, and revision. Extensive experiments demonstrate that CineEvolve outperforms existing approaches. Our code will be available at https://github.com/JingZhe-8/CineEvolve.

</details>

---

## 11. Generalized CycleGRPO for Fine-Grained Multimodal Understanding and Grounding

**中文标题**: Generalized CycleGRPO for Fine-Grained Multimodal Understanding and Grounding

**作者**: Xin Zhang, Haochen Wang, Beibei Lin, Zhaoxiang Zhang, Robby T. Tan et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29552v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29552v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29552v1)

**项目主页**: https://devinxzhang.github.io/Generalized-CycleGRPO-Page/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Fine-grained multimodal intelligence requires models to both understand a designated region and localize it from language. Existing methods learn these directions with separate objectives and task-specific annotations. We present Generalized CycleGRPO, the first unified reinforcement learning framework coupling region understanding and localization across spatial, temporal, and spatio-temporal domains. Given a region, a multimodal large language model generates candidate descriptions and then localizes each description to reconstruct the input region. Reconstruction overlap provides a verifiable reward for captioning, while individual localization rewards improve the inverse mapping. A common region-space formulation extends this cycle from image masks and boxes to video intervals and timestamped spatial evidence, without reference captions or an external language-model judge during reinforcement learning. In videos, the cycle encourages descriptions that distinguish a target event from similar recurrences. We introduce DOVE (Distinctive Occurrence-level Video-caption Evaluation), a human-verified contrastive benchmark for this temporal distinctiveness. CycleGRPO improves SAMTok's overall gIoU from 57.5% to 68.2% on GroundingSuite and average gIoU from 71.2% to 78.7% on GRES. For temporal tasks, it achieves 51.9% tIoU on Charades and 33.3% Temporal Evidence Coverage on DOVE, compared with 45.6% and 14.8% for the Qwen3-VL initialization. These results establish region-text cycle consistency as a general self-evaluation principle for multimodal understanding and localization. Models, code, and DOVE are available at https://devinxzhang.github.io/Generalized-CycleGRPO-Page/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Fine-grained multimodal intelligence requires models to both understand a designated region and localize it from language. We introduce DOVE (Distinctive Occurrence-level Video-caption Evaluation), a human-verified contrastive benchmark for this temporal distinctiveness. CycleGRPO improves SAMTok's overall gIoU from 57.5% to 68.2% on GroundingSuite and average gIoU from 71.2% to 78.7% on GRES. For temporal tasks, it achieves 51.9% tIoU on Charades and 33.3% Temporal Evidence Coverage on DOVE, compared with 45.6% and 14.8% for the Qwen3-VL initialization. These results establish region-text cycle consistency as a general self-evaluation principle for multimodal understanding and localization. Models, code, and DOVE are available at https://devinxzhang.github.io/Generalized-CycleGRPO-Page/.

</details>

## 12. Supervision-Adaptive Curriculum and Guarded Anti-Curriculum Learning for Dense Prediction

**中文标题**: Supervision-Adaptive Curriculum and Guarded Anti-Curriculum Learning for Dense Prediction

**作者**: Chunming He, Dingming Zhang, Fengyang Xiao, Zhiwen Cao, Sina Farsiu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29519v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29519v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29519v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Curriculum learning orders samples by difficulty, but difficulty alone does not establish supervision reliability for dense prediction. Easy samples can expose shortcuts, hard samples can be informative, and noisy or ambiguous samples can mislead training. Building on our conference method CurriSeg, we present PaceShift, a stabilize-then-challenge paradigm combining supervision-adaptive curriculum selection with guarded spectral post-training. A learning-dynamics view motivates separating supervision reliability from temporal update value and applying spectral challenges after stabilization. The first phase estimates reliability from available supervision, augmentation consistency, model disagreement, and prediction certainty. Temporal statistics guide sample weighting, while pixel-uncertainty weights reduce the early influence of uncertain regions. The second phase ranks predictions from multiple spectral views using supervision agreement, robustness, reference consistency, and penalties. A pairwise preference objective measured against the frozen Phase-I reference, together with an anchor, refines the prediction. The framework supports full, weak, semi-supervised, and model-derived supervision. Experiments across context-entangled content segmentation (CECS), broader dense-prediction tasks, and foundation-model and cross-modal adaptations show accuracy gains. Targeted diagnostics indicate improved pooled calibration and corruption robustness in the evaluated settings. PaceShift preserves the deployed architecture and adds no inference computation. Code will be publicly released.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 13. Sparsity Should Be ALLOTted by Output: Training-Free Allocation of Block-Sparse Attention for Video Diffusion Transformers

**中文标题**: Sparsity Should Be ALLOTted by Output: Training-Free Allocation of Block-Sparse Attention for Video Diffusion Transformers

**作者**: Tianze Xia, Xiaoling Zhou, Ruya Lan, Yiqiao Liao, Chengru Song et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29441v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29441v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29441v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Full 3D attention is costly in video Diffusion Transformers. Block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing only selected attention blocks. However, existing methods either overlook the potential to optimize sparsity allocation or rely on heuristic rules or local metrics to guide it. In this work, we observe that sparsifying attention in different layers and at different denoising steps affects final-video fidelity to substantially different degrees. Yet the identities of the most sensitive layer-step pairs remain nearly unchanged across inputs. Building on these findings, we propose Allocating Layer-step Latency by Output Tests (ALLOT), a training-free sparsity allocation method. Treating each layer-step pair as a cell, ALLOT estimates each cell's contribution to final-video error through (i) cell probing and (ii) trajectory calibration, then jointly optimizes cell densities under a measured-latency budget. It preserves existing selection strategies and kernels, replacing only the allocation component. At equal or shorter wall-clock time, ALLOT outperforms every compared allocation strategy with XAttention, with an 11.8 dB PSNR gain on HunyuanVideo. Extensive experiments across four additional published pipelines on Wan2.1, Wan2.2, and HunyuanVideo show that ALLOT outperforms or ties the default in all nine settings.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Full 3D attention is costly in video Diffusion Transformers. Block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing only selected attention blocks. However, existing methods either overlook the potential to optimize sparsity allocation or rely on heuristic rules or local metrics to guide it. Yet the identities of the most sensitive layer-step pairs remain nearly unchanged across inputs. Treating each layer-step pair as a cell, ALLOT estimates each cell's contribution to final-video error through (i) cell probing and (ii) trajectory calibration, then jointly optimizes cell densities under a measured-latency budget. It preserves existing selection strategies and kernels, replacing only the allocation component. At equal or shorter wall-clock time, ALLOT outperforms every compared allocation strategy with XAttention, with an 11.8 dB PSNR gain on HunyuanVideo. Extensive experiments across four additional published pipelines on Wan2.1, Wan2.2, and HunyuanVideo show that ALLOT outperforms or ties the default in all nine settings.

</details>

## 14. OACap: Caption-Guided Active Observation for Detailed Video Captioning

**中文标题**: OACap: Caption-Guided Active Observation for Detailed Video Captioning

**作者**: Hao Shi, Hong Yan, Xiaoyang Zhang, Qing Cui, Chao Huang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29327v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29327v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29327v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.CL, cs.MA, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Unlike question answering, where a query specifies a perceptual target, open-ended detailed captioning must determine what evidence to acquire and how to integrate it into a coherent global description. We propose Observation-Adaptive Video Captioner (OACap), a caption-centered agent that turns unsupported or missing information into targeted observation requests, uses spatiotemporal tools to acquire evidence, and iteratively verifies and revises the caption. OACap captions consistently improve performance across general video-understanding benchmarks using a fixed text-only reader. To better diagnose the information they preserve, we introduce Factual-Semantic Captioning Bench (FSCap-Bench). Its Core-Support Factuality Score (CSFS) combines claim precision with importance-aware fact coverage and responds as expected to four controlled perturbation types on 93.0-98.3% of valid caption pairs. Its Cross-Modal Semantic Round-Trip Score (CSRS) measures semantic preservation through a video-caption-video-caption round trip by matching atomic facts from source and recovered captions. Together, they connect downstream textual-memory utility with factual and semantic preservation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Unlike question answering, where a query specifies a perceptual target, open-ended detailed captioning must determine what evidence to acquire and how to integrate it into a coherent global description. We propose Observation-Adaptive Video Captioner (OACap), a caption-centered agent that turns unsupported or missing information into targeted observation requests, uses spatiotemporal tools to acquire evidence, and iteratively verifies and revises the caption. OACap captions consistently improve performance across general video-understanding benchmarks using a fixed text-only reader. To better diagnose the information they preserve, we introduce Factual-Semantic Captioning Bench (FSCap-Bench). Its Core-Support Factuality Score (CSFS) combines claim precision with importance-aware fact coverage and responds as expected to four controlled perturbation types on 93.0-98.3% of valid caption pairs. Its Cross-Modal Semantic Round-Trip Score (CSRS) measures semantic preservation through a video-caption-video-caption round trip by matching atomic facts from source and recovered captions. Together, they connect downstream textual-memory utility with factual and semantic preservation.

</details>

## 15. Clinical radar heart sounds with echocardiographic phenotypes of ventricular dysfunction and valvular disease

**中文标题**: Clinical radar heart sounds with echocardiographic phenotypes of ventricular dysfunction and valvular disease

**作者**: Jinye Li, Qingchao Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29267v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29267v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29267v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Radar heart sounds capture cardiac mechanical activity without contact, while echocardiography characterizes ventricular and valvular function. Paired measurements connect these signals to structural heart disease, yet such data remain sparsely represented in public resources. Here we present 537 radar heart-sound recordings paired with one echocardiographic report for each of 162 patients. The cohort covers three overlapping phenotypes: left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF) below 50%, regional wall-motion abnormality and at least moderate valvular regurgitation, present in 38, 43 and 39 patients, respectively. Original complex signals, processed radar heart sounds and temporal quality segmentation support signal-to-phenotype analysis. Technical validation retains 95.93% of signal duration and confirms reproducible waveform reconstruction. Patient-level five-fold evaluation achieves a mean outer-test AUROC of $0.770\pm0.138$ for reduced LVEF, identifying mechanical information associated with ventricular systolic dysfunction. This resource supports research linking non-contact cardiac mechanical signals to echocardiographic structural and functional phenotypes.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Radar heart sounds capture cardiac mechanical activity without contact, while echocardiography characterizes ventricular and valvular function. Paired measurements connect these signals to structural heart disease, yet such data remain sparsely represented in public resources. The cohort covers three overlapping phenotypes: left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF) below 50%, regional wall-motion abnormality and at least moderate valvular regurgitation, present in 38, 43 and 39 patients, respectively. Original complex signals, processed radar heart sounds and temporal quality segmentation support signal-to-phenotype analysis. Technical validation retains 95.93% of signal duration and confirms reproducible waveform reconstruction. Patient-level five-fold evaluation achieves a mean outer-test AUROC of $0.770\pm0.138$ for reduced LVEF, identifying mechanical information associated with ventricular systolic dysfunction. This resource supports research linking non-contact cardiac mechanical signals to echocardiographic structural and functional phenotypes.

</details>

## 16. ER-Chord: Spatially Consistent One-Step Image Editing via Equal-Energy Reliability Redistribution

**中文标题**: ER-Chord: Spatially Consistent One-Step Image Editing via Equal-Energy Reliability Redistribution

**作者**: Peize Li, Hanzhen Zhao, Xingguo Xu, Yilong Fan, Zhen Tao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29248v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29248v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29248v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/Peizeli1/ER-Chord.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

One-step generative models enable fast text-guided image editing, yet they often introduce unintended changes to regions that should remain unchanged. Temporal aggregation stabilizes editing fields across noise levels, but leaves their spatial allocation insensitive to local variations in reliability. Consequently, temporal stability alone does not ensure spatially consistent editing. To address this lim- itation, we propose ER-Chord, a training-free and inversion-free framework for reliability-guided spatial redistribution. Our key insight is that agreement between editing fields at neighboring noise levels provides a local reliability signal for allocating editing strength. We formulate this allocation as an equal-energy op- timization that redistributes control energy according to local reliability while preserving the original editing directions and total control energy. This formu- lation admits a unique global solution that can be efficiently computed using existing field evaluations, requiring no additional denoiser calls. Experiments on two benchmarks, PIE-Bench and TEdBench, demonstrate improvements over state-of-the-art one-step editing baselines, achieving superior source preservation alongside consistent gains across multiple generative backbones.The project page is at https://github.com/Peizeli1/ER-Chord.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Consequently, temporal stability alone does not ensure spatially consistent editing. To address this lim- itation, we propose ER-Chord, a training-free and inversion-free framework for reliability-guided spatial redistribution. Our key insight is that agreement between editing fields at neighboring noise levels provides a local reliability signal for allocating editing strength. We formulate this allocation as an equal-energy op- timization that redistributes control energy according to local reliability while preserving the original editing directions and total control energy. This formu- lation admits a unique global solution that can be efficiently computed using existing field evaluations, requiring no additional denoiser calls. Experiments on two benchmarks, PIE-Bench and TEdBench, demonstrate improvements over state-of-the-art one-step editing baselines, achieving superior source preservation alongside consistent gains across multiple generative backbones.The project page is at https://github.com/Peizeli1/ER-Chord.

</details>

## 17. Cache Locally, Recompute Selectively for Streaming Video Understanding

**中文标题**: Cache Locally, Recompute Selectively for Streaming Video Understanding

**作者**: Junhyeok Lee, Sangyoon Lee, Jaeho Lee

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29229v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29229v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29229v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Streaming video understanding (SVU) requires video-LLMs to process incoming video continuously while answering user queries with low latency. Recent methods reduce query-time cost by prefilling frames into KV caches during idle time and reusing the cached KVs at query time. To bound per-frame computation over unbounded video streams, these methods restrict the context available during prefill. This creates a fundamental context mismatch: the restricted context may omit information needed to properly contextualize video tokens, yet, still contain information irrelevant to the eventual query, which is unknown during prefill. We propose FrameBlend, a simple yet effective plug-and-play method to address this mismatch through selective KV recomputation, without requiring any additional training. At query time, FrameBlend re-prefills selected video tokens using query-relevant context and replaces their cached KVs with recomputed ones. Crucially, this recomputation enables shorter attention windows during idle-time prefill, substantially reducing prefill cost while improving accuracy. Experiments across three SVU frameworks and five streaming and offline benchmarks demonstrate accuracy gains of up to 3.9 pp. On RVS-Ego, FrameBlend reduces per-frame prefill FLOPs by over 20% and total processing time by 14-59% for retrieval-based frameworks, including the cost of query-time recomputation. These results demonstrate that a simple change in how contextualization is distributed between idle time and query time can improve the accuracy-efficiency trade-off for SVU.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Streaming video understanding (SVU) requires video-LLMs to process incoming video continuously while answering user queries with low latency. Recent methods reduce query-time cost by prefilling frames into KV caches during idle time and reusing the cached KVs at query time. To bound per-frame computation over unbounded video streams, these methods restrict the context available during prefill. This creates a fundamental context mismatch: the restricted context may omit information needed to properly contextualize video tokens, yet, still contain information irrelevant to the eventual query, which is unknown during prefill. We propose FrameBlend, a simple yet effective plug-and-play method to address this mismatch through selective KV recomputation, without requiring any additional training. At query time, FrameBlend re-prefills selected video tokens using query-relevant context and replaces their cached KVs with recomputed ones. Crucially, this recomputation enables shorter attention windows during idle-time prefill, substantially reducing prefill cost while improving accuracy. Experiments across three SVU frameworks and five streaming and offline benchmarks demonstrate accuracy gains of up to 3.9 pp. On RVS-Ego, FrameBlend reduces per-frame prefill FLOPs by over 20% and total processing time by 14-59% for retrieval-based frameworks, including the cost of query-time recomputation. These results demonstrate that a simple change in how contextualization is distributed between idle time and query time can improve the accuracy-efficiency trade-off for SVU.

</details>

## 18. DetM-Tracker: Deterministic and Memory-Efficient VIS for Autonomous Industrial Inspection

**中文标题**: DetM-Tracker: Deterministic and Memory-Efficient VIS for Autonomous Industrial Inspection

**作者**: You-Rui Lin, Jen-Chieh Wu, Tzu-Ti Wei, Jen-Jee Chen, Yu-Chee Tseng

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29212v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29212v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29212v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autonomous industrial inspection requires strictly deterministic real-time inference under tight memory and computational constraints. However, existing Video Instance Segmentation (VIS) models suffer from prohibitive scaling: dense temporal memory grows with spatial resolution and sequence length, while fixed-capacity decoders fail to exploit the inherent sparsity of industrial scenes, creating a practical memory wall on resource-constrained edge platforms. We present DetM-Tracker, a streamlined VIS framework for autonomous industrial inspection, where target objects often exhibit highly similar appearance under constrained "micro" fields of view. DetM-Tracker eliminates architectural redundancy through three synergistic components: (1) Compact Scalar Memory, which condenses high-dimensional instance features into four-scalar descriptors, reducing temporal memory complexity from $O(T \cdot N \cdot R)$ to $O(T \cdot \hat{N})$. This mechanism effectively compresses frame-level representations into a bank of active instances, where $R$ denotes resolution, $N$ and $\hat{N}$ represent global and window-specific instance counts, respectively and $T$ the temporal window size; and (2) Dynamic Instance Channels, which adaptively allocate query capacity according to the actual number of visible objects; and (3) Entropy-Gated Skip Connection, which concentrates computational budget on high-uncertainty spatial regions to refine object boundaries in a single forward pass without auxiliary refinement heads. Our approach effectively bridges the gap between modern VIS models and the practical demands of industrial edge deployment, reducing inference latency by approximately 20$\times$ and VRAM consumption by 6$\times$ compared to state-of-the-art dense-feature baselines.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Autonomous industrial inspection requires strictly deterministic real-time inference under tight memory and computational constraints. However, existing Video Instance Segmentation (VIS) models suffer from prohibitive scaling: dense temporal memory grows with spatial resolution and sequence length, while fixed-capacity decoders fail to exploit the inherent sparsity of industrial scenes, creating a practical memory wall on resource-constrained edge platforms. We present DetM-Tracker, a streamlined VIS framework for autonomous industrial inspection, where target objects often exhibit highly similar appearance under constrained "micro" fields of view. DetM-Tracker eliminates architectural redundancy through three synergistic components: (1) Compact Scalar Memory, which condenses high-dimensional instance features into four-scalar descriptors, reducing temporal memory complexity from $O(T \cdot N \cdot R)$ to $O(T \cdot \hat{N})$. This mechanism effectively compresses frame-level representations into a bank of active instances, where $R$ denotes resolution, $N$ and $\hat{N}$ represent global and window-specific instance counts, respectively and $T$ the temporal window size; and (2) Dynamic Instance Channels, which adaptively allocate query capacity according to the actual number of visible objects; and (3) Entropy-Gated Skip Connection, which concentrates computational budget on high-uncertainty spatial regions to refine object boundaries in a single forward pass without auxiliary refinement heads. Our approach effectively bridges the gap between modern VIS models and the practical demands of industrial edge deployment, reducing inference latency by approximately 20$\times$ and VRAM consumption by 6$\times$ compared to state-of-the-art dense-feature baselines.

</details>

## 19. NeuralSRender: G-Buffer Guided Video Diffusion for Sim-to-Real Rendering

**中文标题**: NeuralSRender: G-Buffer Guided Video Diffusion for Sim-to-Real Rendering

**作者**: Yipeng Lu, Youqi Liao, Zhenyu Wang, Yuning Peng, Zhen Dong

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29200v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29200v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29200v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/WHU-USI3DV/NeuralSRender.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

The scarcity of high-quality annotated visual data remains a critical bottleneck for autonomous-driving perception. Simulators provide training data at scale, but the limited realism of synthetic videos compromises perception performance in real-world applications. To address this limitation, we propose NeuralSRender, a sim-to-real video translation framework that enhances the photorealism of synthetic videos while maintaining geometric consistency with the source scenes. Built on a pretrained video diffusion transformer (DiT), NeuralSRender uses a lightweight projector to inject geometric and material priors from G-buffers, and LoRA enables parameter-efficient adaptation. The model is trained in two stages: it first learns G-buffer conditioning from synthetic videos with accurate G-buffers, then adapts to real-world appearance using real videos with estimated G-buffers. During the second stage, an additional LoRA module is trained while the projector and first-stage LoRA remain frozen to reduce interference from estimation noise. NeuralSRender introduces only 1.45\% parameter overhead and can be trained on consumer-grade GPUs with 24 GB of memory. To support training and evaluation, we introduce GenReal, a large-scale driving video dataset comprising synthetic and real-world videos with corresponding G-buffers. Experiments demonstrate improved photorealism with competitive geometric consistency. Training perception models on our translated videos instead of raw simulator renders improves detection $\mathrm{AP}_{50}$ on nuImages from 35.1 to 40.6 and segmentation mIoU on Cityscapes from 32.4 to 40.8. Our code, data, and model weights are available at: https://github.com/WHU-USI3DV/NeuralSRender.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The scarcity of high-quality annotated visual data remains a critical bottleneck for autonomous-driving perception. During the second stage, an additional LoRA module is trained while the projector and first-stage LoRA remain frozen to reduce interference from estimation noise. NeuralSRender introduces only 1.45\% parameter overhead and can be trained on consumer-grade GPUs with 24 GB of memory. To support training and evaluation, we introduce GenReal, a large-scale driving video dataset comprising synthetic and real-world videos with corresponding G-buffers. Experiments demonstrate improved photorealism with competitive geometric consistency. Training perception models on our translated videos instead of raw simulator renders improves detection $\mathrm{AP}_{50}$ on nuImages from 35.1 to 40.6 and segmentation mIoU on Cityscapes from 32.4 to 40.8. Our code, data, and model weights are available at: https://github.com/WHU-USI3DV/NeuralSRender.

</details>

## 20. Multi-Scale Latent Temporal Compression Attention and Video-Text Cross Attention Fusion for Video Action Detection

**中文标题**: Multi-Scale Latent Temporal Compression Attention and Video-Text Cross Attention Fusion for Video Action Detection

**作者**: Yicheng Qiu, Keiji Yanai

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29176v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29176v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29176v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In video action detection, modeling the temporal dependencies in continuous video streams is crucial. Previous methods based on deep neural networks can perform frame-level video recognition tasks. However, these methods struggle to model dynamic temporal patterns, facing challenges such as redundant frame information, difficulty balancing global and local features, and insufficient fusion of visual and textual features. To address these issues, we propose a video temporal attention module based on the Multi-Scale Latent Temporal Compression Attention, which aggregates continuous temporal sequences into feature representations across diverse temporal scales while efficiently reducing redundancy. We also introduce a Video-Text Cross Attention Fusion module that leverages cross-attention at both latent and standard feature levels, achieving fine-grained fusion between video and textual features to enhance semantic alignment and feature interaction. Experimental results across multiple video recognition benchmarks demonstrate that our proposed methods achieve superior performance compared to existing methods, validating the effectiveness and generalization capability.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

In video action detection, modeling the temporal dependencies in continuous video streams is crucial. Previous methods based on deep neural networks can perform frame-level video recognition tasks. However, these methods struggle to model dynamic temporal patterns, facing challenges such as redundant frame information, difficulty balancing global and local features, and insufficient fusion of visual and textual features. To address these issues, we propose a video temporal attention module based on the Multi-Scale Latent Temporal Compression Attention, which aggregates continuous temporal sequences into feature representations across diverse temporal scales while efficiently reducing redundancy. We also introduce a Video-Text Cross Attention Fusion module that leverages cross-attention at both latent and standard feature levels, achieving fine-grained fusion between video and textual features to enhance semantic alignment and feature interaction. Experimental results across multiple video recognition benchmarks demonstrate that our proposed methods achieve superior performance compared to existing methods, validating the effectiveness and generalization capability.

</details>

## 21. SICAF: Time-Varying Focus Bottleneck for Self-Supervised Event-Based Optical Flow with Spiking Neural Network

**中文标题**: SICAF: Time-Varying Focus Bottleneck for Self-Supervised Event-Based Optical Flow with Spiking Neural Network

**作者**: Shuangming Yang, Qing He, Shangqi Guo, Badong Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29158v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29158v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29158v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Event-based optical flow estimation achieves high temporal resolution and dynamic range by exploiting asynchronous events, while self-supervised learning provides an effective solution to the absence of ground-truth labels. Spiking neural networks (SNNs), as brain-inspired models, process information through asynchronous spikes, naturally aligning with optical flow events. However, the temporal irregularity across event streams hinders the compact extraction of spatiotemporal features, while the absence of explicit labels leads to spurious correlations, thereby increasing redundancy within latent representations. In this paper, we propose a novel 'Compactly Compress, Time-varying Focus' training strategy. We present SICAF (Spikes Information Compression And Focus) framework that formulates the learning process as a constrained optimization with temporal modulation. It encourages self-supervised SNNs to extract compact latent representations while preventing over-compression that would discard temporal saliency. To better enable feature selectivity across time steps, we design an estimator with learnable temporal attention, achieving context awareness with cross-time dependencies. Experimental results on several datasets demonstrate that SICAF achieves state-of-the-art performance in self-supervised SNNs, with a 8.14% improvement in AEE on the MVSEC dataset and a 13.49% increase in robustness under white-box adversarial attacks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Event-based optical flow estimation achieves high temporal resolution and dynamic range by exploiting asynchronous events, while self-supervised learning provides an effective solution to the absence of ground-truth labels. Spiking neural networks (SNNs), as brain-inspired models, process information through asynchronous spikes, naturally aligning with optical flow events. However, the temporal irregularity across event streams hinders the compact extraction of spatiotemporal features, while the absence of explicit labels leads to spurious correlations, thereby increasing redundancy within latent representations. In this paper, we propose a novel 'Compactly Compress, Time-varying Focus' training strategy. We present SICAF (Spikes Information Compression And Focus) framework that formulates the learning process as a constrained optimization with temporal modulation. It encourages self-supervised SNNs to extract compact latent representations while preventing over-compression that would discard temporal saliency. To better enable feature selectivity across time steps, we design an estimator with learnable temporal attention, achieving context awareness with cross-time dependencies. Experimental results on several datasets demonstrate that SICAF achieves state-of-the-art performance in self-supervised SNNs, with a 8.14% improvement in AEE on the MVSEC dataset and a 13.49% increase in robustness under white-box adversarial attacks.

</details>

## 22. PhyMo: Learning Physical Dynamics with Accurate and Continuous Motion from Multi-View Videos

**中文标题**: PhyMo: Learning Physical Dynamics with Accurate and Continuous Motion from Multi-View Videos

**作者**: Shangjia Liu, Jinxi Li, Siyuan Zhou, Bo Yang, Bing Wang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29107v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29107v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29107v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recovering dynamic 3D scene geometry, appearance, and the physical motion states that govern scene evolution from multi-view videos is important yet challenging. A key difficulty is that appearance can be deceptive. Under rendering supervision alone, a model may fit the observed images by changing color or opacity to compensate for inaccurate deformation, rather than recovering the true evolving geometry and motion. This issue is especially severe in scenes with fast or locally complex motion, where inaccurate trajectories can still produce plausible images over the observed frames, but lead to unreliable reconstruction and discontinuous future prediction. In this paper, we propose PhyMo, a framework for learning evolving dynamics from multi-view videos. We introduce a Motion-Aware Trajectory Representation that drives the model to recover accurate motion from deformation, rather than relying on appearance shortcuts to explain observed motion. On top of this, we propose a Continuous Motion Evolution module with bidirectional kinematic continuity constraint to promote temporally consistent evolution of displacement, velocity, and acceleration. Together, these designs yield geometrically faithful interpolation and continuous motion extrapolation. Experiments on four dynamic scene benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recovering dynamic 3D scene geometry, appearance, and the physical motion states that govern scene evolution from multi-view videos is important yet challenging. A key difficulty is that appearance can be deceptive. Under rendering supervision alone, a model may fit the observed images by changing color or opacity to compensate for inaccurate deformation, rather than recovering the true evolving geometry and motion. This issue is especially severe in scenes with fast or locally complex motion, where inaccurate trajectories can still produce plausible images over the observed frames, but lead to unreliable reconstruction and discontinuous future prediction. In this paper, we propose PhyMo, a framework for learning evolving dynamics from multi-view videos. We introduce a Motion-Aware Trajectory Representation that drives the model to recover accurate motion from deformation, rather than relying on appearance shortcuts to explain observed motion. On top of this, we propose a Continuous Motion Evolution module with bidirectional kinematic continuity constraint to promote temporally consistent evolution of displacement, velocity, and acceleration. Together, these designs yield geometrically faithful interpolation and continuous motion extrapolation. Experiments on four dynamic scene benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance.

</details>

## 23. OccJEPA: Occlusion as Masking for Amodal Video Segmentation

**中文标题**: OccJEPA: Occlusion as Masking for Amodal Video Segmentation

**作者**: Genpei Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29061v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29061v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29061v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/zgp110/occjepa.

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Amodal video segmentation asks for the complete mask of an object in every frame, including the parts hidden behind other objects. The best current methods generate these masks with fine-tuned video diffusion models, which need many denoising steps and an extra depth network, and give a different answer for every sample. Self-supervised video world models of the JEPA family are trained on a closely related task: predicting the representation of masked space-time regions from the visible ones. We therefore treat occlusion as masking. The tokens that the object's visible mask does not cover are dropped, the pre-trained V-JEPA 2.1 predictor fills them in, and a light head decodes the observed and predicted features into amodal masks. The resulting model, OccJEPA (Occlusion-as-masking JEPA), is deterministic and 33.5x faster than the diffusion-based state of the art, and more accurate on SAIL-VOS, on TAO-Amodal, including a held-out test split (+4.00 AP50) and visible masks tracked by SAM2, and on Kubric. Controlled experiments trace the gain to the predictor's pre-trained weights rather than to its capacity, for world models from 87M to 1.85B parameters. Code is available at https://github.com/zgp110/occjepa.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Amodal video segmentation asks for the complete mask of an object in every frame, including the parts hidden behind other objects. The best current methods generate these masks with fine-tuned video diffusion models, which need many denoising steps and an extra depth network, and give a different answer for every sample. Self-supervised video world models of the JEPA family are trained on a closely related task: predicting the representation of masked space-time regions from the visible ones. We therefore treat occlusion as masking. The tokens that the object's visible mask does not cover are dropped, the pre-trained V-JEPA 2.1 predictor fills them in, and a light head decodes the observed and predicted features into amodal masks. The resulting model, OccJEPA (Occlusion-as-masking JEPA), is deterministic and 33.5x faster than the diffusion-based state of the art, and more accurate on SAIL-VOS, on TAO-Amodal, including a held-out test split (+4.00 AP50) and visible masks tracked by SAM2, and on Kubric. Controlled experiments trace the gain to the predictor's pre-trained weights rather than to its capacity, for world models from 87M to 1.85B parameters. Code is available at https://github.com/zgp110/occjepa.

</details>

## 24. Weakly Supervised Fish Segmentation through Point-Supervised Tracking and Motion-Informed Language

**中文标题**: Weakly Supervised Fish Segmentation through Point-Supervised Tracking and Motion-Informed Language

**作者**: Vedanth Kallakuri, Katherine A. Skinner

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29047v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29047v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29047v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Understanding the properties of fish populations is increasingly important. Analyzing fish length, width, and mass is integral to maintaining healthy stock in the aquaculture industry, while counting wild fish populations supports marine conservation and coastal reef restoration efforts. Deep learning-based segmentation models offer a promising means of obtaining such information at scale. Most of these models, however, require costly and time-consuming per-pixel segmentation labels for training, which can take up to two minutes per fish to annotate manually. To address this inefficiency, we propose a weakly supervised segmentation pipeline that requires only point-labeled data (one click per fish) to produce accurate segmentation masks. Our pipeline consists of four stages. First, a point-supervised tracker predicts point annotations for each frame. These points are then converted into motion-informed language expressions, which guide a language-conditioned segmentation model (LMPM++) to capture temporal consistency and produce candidate segmentation masks. Finally, optical flow and background subtraction are applied as post-processing filters to suppress noise from incorrect segmentations. Experiments on the DeepFish dataset show improvement in foreground mean intersection over union (mIoU) as compared to baseline models.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Understanding the properties of fish populations is increasingly important. Analyzing fish length, width, and mass is integral to maintaining healthy stock in the aquaculture industry, while counting wild fish populations supports marine conservation and coastal reef restoration efforts. Deep learning-based segmentation models offer a promising means of obtaining such information at scale. Most of these models, however, require costly and time-consuming per-pixel segmentation labels for training, which can take up to two minutes per fish to annotate manually. To address this inefficiency, we propose a weakly supervised segmentation pipeline that requires only point-labeled data (one click per fish) to produce accurate segmentation masks. Our pipeline consists of four stages. First, a point-supervised tracker predicts point annotations for each frame. These points are then converted into motion-informed language expressions, which guide a language-conditioned segmentation model (LMPM++) to capture temporal consistency and produce candidate segmentation masks. Finally, optical flow and background subtraction are applied as post-processing filters to suppress noise from incorrect segmentations. Experiments on the DeepFish dataset show improvement in foreground mean intersection over union (mIoU) as compared to baseline models.

</details>

## 25. Charts as Epistemic Actions for Experience-Driven Agentic Data Analysis

**中文标题**: Charts as Epistemic Actions for Experience-Driven Agentic Data Analysis

**作者**: Xinzhe Wang, Xudong Weng, Zang Li, Shaolei Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.30072v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30072v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30072v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Data agents built on ReAct-style loops now execute complex analytical workflows over structured data, yet their intermediate reasoning still runs almost entirely through text. Human analysts instead move between numbers, tables, and charts, reading visual evidence to expose trends and anomalies that text summaries drop. We ask whether charts can serve as internal epistemic actions inside a data agent's reasoning loop, and how to teach an agent to use them. We first build \probe{}, a suite of 200 controlled tasks under four matched interface conditions, and find a persistent gap between what agents can read and what they choose to use. Agents score as well from self-generated charts as from numeric output, yet plotting access alone does not translate into consistent use. Task slices show that charts lead on temporal, pattern, and anomaly reasoning, while text leads on precise reading and conditional verification. We then propose \method{}, which distils two families of experience from conditioned trajectory rollouts. One family records how to select and verify analytical operations, and the other records when to invoke text, charts, or both. A trajectory judge scores outcomes and labels intermediate steps, and a validation gate admits only experience that improves held-out performance. On three held-out benchmarks with four LLM backbones, \method{} raises analytical accuracy and visual evidence quality over matched baselines, and backbones that never drew charts begin using them, all without touching model parameters.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Data agents built on ReAct-style loops now execute complex analytical workflows over structured data, yet their intermediate reasoning still runs almost entirely through text. Human analysts instead move between numbers, tables, and charts, reading visual evidence to expose trends and anomalies that text summaries drop. We ask whether charts can serve as internal epistemic actions inside a data agent's reasoning loop, and how to teach an agent to use them. We first build \probe{}, a suite of 200 controlled tasks under four matched interface conditions, and find a persistent gap between what agents can read and what they choose to use. Agents score as well from self-generated charts as from numeric output, yet plotting access alone does not translate into consistent use. Task slices show that charts lead on temporal, pattern, and anomaly reasoning, while text leads on precise reading and conditional verification. We then propose \method{}, which distils two families of experience from conditioned trajectory rollouts. One family records how to select and verify analytical operations, and the other records when to invoke text, charts, or both. A trajectory judge scores outcomes and labels intermediate steps, and a validation gate admits only experience that improves held-out performance. On three held-out benchmarks with four LLM backbones, \method{} raises analytical accuracy and visual evidence quality over matched baselines, and backbones that never drew charts begin using them, all without touching model parameters.

</details>

## 26. SP-HLM: Sparse-Pose-Driven Motion Generation and Whole-Body Control for Humanoid Loco-Manipulation

**中文标题**: SP-HLM: Sparse-Pose-Driven Motion Generation and Whole-Body Control for Humanoid Loco-Manipulation

**作者**: Zhaohui Yang, Quanbao Lin, Chengqi Shi, Zhenglang Weng, Jicheng Lin et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.30044v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30044v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30044v1)

**项目主页**: https://sp-hlm.github.io/

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Humanoid loco-manipulation requires an effective interface between high-level task policies and whole-body control. Existing interfaces typically rely on a single action representation for both task intent and whole-body coordination. This may burden the high-level policy or leave one aspect underspecified. We present SP-HLM, a framework built on a sparse-pose-driven interface. The interface combines five task-critical body poses with a learned latent motion prior to represent spatial intent and whole-body coordination in complementary forms. SP-VLA, a vision-language-action model, first predicts task-relevant sparse poses and finger joint positions from multimodal observations and language instructions using asynchronous action-chunk inference. Given these sparse targets, SP-MGM, a motion generation model trained through two-stage distillation, reconstructs coordinated full-body motion in real time. A pretrained motion encoder maps this motion to a compact latent representation. SP-GMT, a general motion tracker, then integrates the explicit sparse targets with this implicit motion prior for whole-body control. Experiments in simulation and on a real humanoid robot demonstrate improved motion tracking, whole-body coordination, and loco-manipulation success over existing approaches. Additional results and videos are available on our \href{https://sp-hlm.github.io/}{\textcolor{blue}{project page}}.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Humanoid loco-manipulation requires an effective interface between high-level task policies and whole-body control. Existing interfaces typically rely on a single action representation for both task intent and whole-body coordination. This may burden the high-level policy or leave one aspect underspecified. We present SP-HLM, a framework built on a sparse-pose-driven interface. The interface combines five task-critical body poses with a learned latent motion prior to represent spatial intent and whole-body coordination in complementary forms. SP-VLA, a vision-language-action model, first predicts task-relevant sparse poses and finger joint positions from multimodal observations and language instructions using asynchronous action-chunk inference. Given these sparse targets, SP-MGM, a motion generation model trained through two-stage distillation, reconstructs coordinated full-body motion in real time. A pretrained motion encoder maps this motion to a compact latent representation. SP-GMT, a general motion tracker, then integrates the explicit sparse targets with this implicit motion prior for whole-body control. Experiments in simulation and on a real humanoid robot demonstrate improved motion tracking, whole-body coordination, and loco-manipulation success over existing approaches. Additional results and videos are available on our \href{https://sp-hlm.github.io/}{\textcolor{blue}{project page}}.

</details>
