## 27. GuideEvolve: Self-Evolving Agent Framework with Expert Guidance and 3Ts Mechanisms

**中文标题**: GuideEvolve: Self-Evolving Agent Framework with Expert Guidance and 3Ts Mechanisms

**作者**: Meihong Jia, Yasemin Timar, Jonathan Taws

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.30017v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30017v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30017v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

This study introduces GuideEvolve, a self-evolving agent framework incorporating expert guidance and three autonomous regulatory mechanisms, collectively termed the 3Ts: Think Fast and Slow, Throwback, and Termination by choice. The framework addresses critical limitations in extant self-evolving agent paradigms through dual innovations: (1) a task-specific expert-evaluation-driven evolutionary pipeline, and (2) the integration of 3Ts mechanisms to facilitate cognitive optimization, performance recovery via temporal rollback, and adaptive termination protocols. Empirical validation across the MGSM and MMLU benchmarks demonstrates that GuideEvolve attains performance metrics comparable to current state-of-the-art models (89.8% accuracy versus Gödel Agent's 90.6% on MGSM; 89.1% versus 87.9% on MMLU), while exhibiting notably reduced variance (25-59%) and enhanced robustness. Furthermore, the framework achieves a 100% evolution success rate, superior to the baseline's 82%, diminishes required optimization iterations by approximately 50% (from 30 iterations in baseline to 15.1 and 13.4 across tasks), and provides improved stability for production deployment.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

This study introduces GuideEvolve, a self-evolving agent framework incorporating expert guidance and three autonomous regulatory mechanisms, collectively termed the 3Ts: Think Fast and Slow, Throwback, and Termination by choice. The framework addresses critical limitations in extant self-evolving agent paradigms through dual innovations: (1) a task-specific expert-evaluation-driven evolutionary pipeline, and (2) the integration of 3Ts mechanisms to facilitate cognitive optimization, performance recovery via temporal rollback, and adaptive termination protocols. Empirical validation across the MGSM and MMLU benchmarks demonstrates that GuideEvolve attains performance metrics comparable to current state-of-the-art models (89.8% accuracy versus Gödel Agent's 90.6% on MGSM; 89.1% versus 87.9% on MMLU), while exhibiting notably reduced variance (25-59%) and enhanced robustness. Furthermore, the framework achieves a 100% evolution success rate, superior to the baseline's 82%, diminishes required optimization iterations by approximately 50% (from 30 iterations in baseline to 15.1 and 13.4 across tasks), and provides improved stability for production deployment.

</details>

## 28. Every Alarm Has a Cause: An Open Generator of Telecom Alarm Storms with Exact Root Causes, Bound to NORIA-O, RFC 8632 and TMF642

**中文标题**: Every Alarm Has a Cause: An Open Generator of Telecom Alarm Storms with Exact Root Causes, Bound to NORIA-O, RFC 8632 and TMF642

**作者**: Madhulatha Mandarapu, Sandeep Kunkunuru

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.30004v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30004v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30004v1)

**类别**: cs.NI, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Alarm root-cause analysis is studied on data that cannot be shared. Operator logs are confidential, so what the field publishes is a causal graph between alarm types rather than per-incident causes, a fixed scenario set, gated, or never released. Two recent benchmarks carry per-incident root causes at scale; neither states a download location or a licence.   We give a generator rather than a dataset. It runs on real backbone topologies from the Internet Topology Zoo and records the exact root cause and the propagation path of every alarm -- a by-product of generation, not a labelling pass. Every emitted field binds to a term in NORIA-O, RFC 8632, RFC 8345 or TMF642, and an unknown term fails the run. We ship fixed configurations, splits, a scorer and nine baselines.   A one-line rule -- rank the device that raised the earliest critical alarm -- takes top-1 at 0.650 against 0.575 for our own temporal method, and wins 13 of 15 runs over three backbones and five seeds. An LLM given the same view reaches 0.600, the same model allowed to query the graph 0.625, and the rule wins on real operator alarms too, at 0.773: a property of alarm streams, not of our generator. One fault class separates the table: when a site loses power its PoP reports nothing, so every method that ranks alarmed devices scores 0.000 or near it, while the two that walk the topology to a silent candidate take 1.000 and 0.833.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Alarm root-cause analysis is studied on data that cannot be shared. Operator logs are confidential, so what the field publishes is a causal graph between alarm types rather than per-incident causes, a fixed scenario set, gated, or never released. Two recent benchmarks carry per-incident root causes at scale; neither states a download location or a licence.   We give a generator rather than a dataset. It runs on real backbone topologies from the Internet Topology Zoo and records the exact root cause and the propagation path of every alarm -- a by-product of generation, not a labelling pass. Every emitted field binds to a term in NORIA-O, RFC 8632, RFC 8345 or TMF642, and an unknown term fails the run. We ship fixed configurations, splits, a scorer and nine baselines.   A one-line rule -- rank the device that raised the earliest critical alarm -- takes top-1 at 0.650 against 0.575 for our own temporal method, and wins 13 of 15 runs over three backbones and five seeds. An LLM given the same view reaches 0.600, the same model allowed to query the graph 0.625, and the rule wins on real operator alarms too, at 0.773: a property of alarm streams, not of our generator. One fault class separates the table: when a site loses power its PoP reports nothing, so every method that ranks alarmed devices scores 0.000 or near it, while the two that walk the topology to a silent candidate take 1.000 and 0.833.

</details>

## 29. Bio-Inspired Auditory Processing: Coupling Parallel Resonate-and-Fire Dynamics with Ternary Recurrent Networks for Speech Recognition

**中文标题**: Bio-Inspired Auditory Processing: Coupling Parallel Resonate-and-Fire Dynamics with Ternary Recurrent Networks for Speech Recognition

**作者**: Pavlos Poulos, Katelyn Sadorf, Themos Stafylakis, Costas A. Anastassiou

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29720v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29720v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29720v1)

**类别**: cs.SD, cs.AI, cs.NE, eess.AS, q-bio.NC

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) promise energy-efficient inference through sparse, event-driven computation, yet remain constrained on continuous, context-dependent tasks such as automatic speech recognition (ASR). A key bottleneck is the Leaky Integrate-and-Fire (LIF) neuron, whose membrane decay erases information across pauses and phonetic boundaries while it must both extract features and retain long-range context. Inspired by cortical separation of nonlinear filtering and somatic integration, we introduce the Spiking Ternary GRU (stGRU): a ternary-weight gated recurrent unit performs temporal filtering, while downstream Resonate-and-Fire (RF) neurons generate the binary spike stream driving the next block's event pathway. This makes binary event transmission a native training constraint rather than a separate deployment-time activation quantization step; together with ternary weights, learned projections avoid dense floating-point matrix MACs. Trained end-to-end on LibriSpeech-960, Small, Medium, and Large stGRUs reach 8.09%, 7.04%, and $6.44 \pm 0.17\%$ WER on test-clean without an external language model. To our knowledge, these are the first matrix-MAC-free spiking networks without a learned ANN sequence decoder to achieve single-digit WER on LibriSpeech. The architecture also achieves 10.88% test CER on Aishell-1 (9.28% on dev) when trained from scratch. Internally, trained networks develop a depth-graded hierarchy: shallow layers act as fast acoustic filters, while deep layers form slower semantic integrators whose latent representations segregate into part-of-speech manifolds and word-level spike events. Deep-layer firing rates follow lognormal distributions with a sparsity gradient, mirroring hierarchical processing observed in human cortex in vivo. Our biologically grounded architecture combines competitive ASR with favorable modeled compute and memory cost.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) promise energy-efficient inference through sparse, event-driven computation, yet remain constrained on continuous, context-dependent tasks such as automatic speech recognition (ASR). A key bottleneck is the Leaky Integrate-and-Fire (LIF) neuron, whose membrane decay erases information across pauses and phonetic boundaries while it must both extract features and retain long-range context. Inspired by cortical separation of nonlinear filtering and somatic integration, we introduce the Spiking Ternary GRU (stGRU): a ternary-weight gated recurrent unit performs temporal filtering, while downstream Resonate-and-Fire (RF) neurons generate the binary spike stream driving the next block's event pathway. This makes binary event transmission a native training constraint rather than a separate deployment-time activation quantization step; together with ternary weights, learned projections avoid dense floating-point matrix MACs. Trained end-to-end on LibriSpeech-960, Small, Medium, and Large stGRUs reach 8.09%, 7.04%, and $6.44 \pm 0.17\%$ WER on test-clean without an external language model. To our knowledge, these are the first matrix-MAC-free spiking networks without a learned ANN sequence decoder to achieve single-digit WER on LibriSpeech. The architecture also achieves 10.88% test CER on Aishell-1 (9.28% on dev) when trained from scratch. Internally, trained networks develop a depth-graded hierarchy: shallow layers act as fast acoustic filters, while deep layers form slower semantic integrators whose latent representations segregate into part-of-speech manifolds and word-level spike events. Deep-layer firing rates follow lognormal distributions with a sparsity gradient, mirroring hierarchical processing observed in human cortex in vivo. Our biologically grounded architecture combines competitive ASR with favorable modeled compute and memory cost.

</details>

## 30. Block Diffusion for Longitudinal EHR: Accuracy, Efficiency, and Representation Trade-offs

**中文标题**: Block Diffusion for Longitudinal EHR: Accuracy, Efficiency, and Representation Trade-offs

**作者**: Ashvin Gupta, Joshua Placidi, Brendan Delaney, Alessandra Russo

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29533v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29533v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29533v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autoregressive (AR) models have proved effective for zero-shot prediction from longitudinal electronic health records, but impose strictly sequential generation on trajectories containing temporally clustered and interdependent clinical events. We investigate block-based masked diffusion (BD) as an alternative on MIMIC-IV under matched tokenisation, context length, model capacity, and evaluation conditions. Across block sizes, BD exhibits a clear quality--efficiency trade-off, with small-block configurations retaining much of the predictive performance of AR while enabling substantially faster inference. A representative $B{=}8$ configuration achieves approximately $1.43\times$ faster evaluation. Although generative rollout remains below AR in most settings, frozen linear probes on BD and AR representations achieve near-identical performance across clinical prediction tasks, indicating that BD learns comparably informative clinical representations. Increasing the denoising budget yields diminishing returns, while alternative decoding strategies provide limited gains. Overall, our results show that block diffusion is a viable and computationally attractive approach to longitudinal EHR modelling, with further improvements in iterative decoding offering a promising route toward closing the remaining generative performance gap.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Autoregressive (AR) models have proved effective for zero-shot prediction from longitudinal electronic health records, but impose strictly sequential generation on trajectories containing temporally clustered and interdependent clinical events. We investigate block-based masked diffusion (BD) as an alternative on MIMIC-IV under matched tokenisation, context length, model capacity, and evaluation conditions. Across block sizes, BD exhibits a clear quality--efficiency trade-off, with small-block configurations retaining much of the predictive performance of AR while enabling substantially faster inference. A representative $B{=}8$ configuration achieves approximately $1.43\times$ faster evaluation. Although generative rollout remains below AR in most settings, frozen linear probes on BD and AR representations achieve near-identical performance across clinical prediction tasks, indicating that BD learns comparably informative clinical representations. Increasing the denoising budget yields diminishing returns, while alternative decoding strategies provide limited gains. Overall, our results show that block diffusion is a viable and computationally attractive approach to longitudinal EHR modelling, with further improvements in iterative decoding offering a promising route toward closing the remaining generative performance gap.

</details>

## 31. Keeping Time vs. Understanding Time: Neither Alone Explains Temporal Reasoning in LLMs

**中文标题**: Keeping Time vs. Understanding Time: Neither Alone Explains Temporal Reasoning in LLMs

**作者**: Auss Abbood, Fernando Martínez-Plumed, José Hernández-Orallo, Nigel Collier

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29507v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29507v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29507v1)

**类别**: cs.CL, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Temporal reasoning is widely assumed to decompose into two modes: temporal arithmetic (date and time calculations), and temporal semantics (understanding temporal and causal relations). This dichotomy shapes how benchmarks are designed, how models are trained, and how progress is measured, yet its empirical validity has never been directly tested. We provide two lines of evidence that challenge it. First, we test whether tasks labelled as arithmetic or semantic by the benchmarks support transferable gains within their purported mode. Finetuning on these subsets yields predominantly dataset-specific gains ($+6.8$ to $+23.1$ percentage points), while transfer to a different dataset in the same mode ranges from $-3.2$ to $+1.1$ percentage points. Across the two benchmark families used in the transfer analysis, this pattern provides little evidence of coherent, transferable temporal abilities. Second, using cognitive-demand-based model evaluation, we examine which capabilities actually drive performance on these benchmarks. We find that quantitative and logical reasoning, the assumed proxies for temporal arithmetic and temporal semantics, are not the primary drivers; knowledge is. Together, these findings show that current temporal reasoning benchmarks do not cleanly realise the arithmetic-semantics distinction and instead largely reward models for remembering how time typically works.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Temporal reasoning is widely assumed to decompose into two modes: temporal arithmetic (date and time calculations), and temporal semantics (understanding temporal and causal relations). This dichotomy shapes how benchmarks are designed, how models are trained, and how progress is measured, yet its empirical validity has never been directly tested. We provide two lines of evidence that challenge it. First, we test whether tasks labelled as arithmetic or semantic by the benchmarks support transferable gains within their purported mode. Finetuning on these subsets yields predominantly dataset-specific gains ($+6.8$ to $+23.1$ percentage points), while transfer to a different dataset in the same mode ranges from $-3.2$ to $+1.1$ percentage points. Across the two benchmark families used in the transfer analysis, this pattern provides little evidence of coherent, transferable temporal abilities. Second, using cognitive-demand-based model evaluation, we examine which capabilities actually drive performance on these benchmarks. We find that quantitative and logical reasoning, the assumed proxies for temporal arithmetic and temporal semantics, are not the primary drivers; knowledge is. Together, these findings show that current temporal reasoning benchmarks do not cleanly realise the arithmetic-semantics distinction and instead largely reward models for remembering how time typically works.

</details>

## 32. ChronoSynth: Constraint-Aware Question Synthesis over Temporal Knowledge Graphs

**中文标题**: ChronoSynth: Constraint-Aware Question Synthesis over Temporal Knowledge Graphs

**作者**: Feng Liang, Weixin Zeng, Runhao Zhao, Mingjun Zhou, Shaoshi Yang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29339v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29339v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29339v1)

**类别**: cs.CL, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Knowledge graph question generation (KGQG) generates natural-language questions from structured graph evidence and a target answer. When the input graph is temporal, a valid question must also preserve the answer field, time condition, reference event, and temporal order that determine the answer set. Existing KGQG methods and prompting baselines can produce fluent questions that alter these conditions, causing temporal semantic drift. This paper studies temporal KGQG as a constraint-preserving graph-to-question problem and proposes ChronoSynth, a memory-augmented and constraint-aware generation framework. ChronoSynth first constructs a Temporal Intent State to make the intended question semantics explicit. It then retrieves compatible typed question patterns from an indexed pattern memory, adapts them to the input graph, and validates the generated question. Validation feedback guides revisions when a supported inconsistency is detected. Experiments on CRONQUESTIONS and MultiTQ demonstrate state-of-the-art generation performance, with ablations supporting the contributions of its components.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Knowledge graph question generation (KGQG) generates natural-language questions from structured graph evidence and a target answer. When the input graph is temporal, a valid question must also preserve the answer field, time condition, reference event, and temporal order that determine the answer set. Existing KGQG methods and prompting baselines can produce fluent questions that alter these conditions, causing temporal semantic drift. This paper studies temporal KGQG as a constraint-preserving graph-to-question problem and proposes ChronoSynth, a memory-augmented and constraint-aware generation framework. ChronoSynth first constructs a Temporal Intent State to make the intended question semantics explicit. It then retrieves compatible typed question patterns from an indexed pattern memory, adapts them to the input graph, and validates the generated question. Validation feedback guides revisions when a supported inconsistency is detected. Experiments on CRONQUESTIONS and MultiTQ demonstrate state-of-the-art generation performance, with ablations supporting the contributions of its components.

</details>

## 33. YingLan: A Multimodal Ocean Foundation Model for Unified Representation Learning from Heterogeneous Observations

**中文标题**: YingLan: A Multimodal Ocean Foundation Model for Unified Representation Learning from Heterogeneous Observations

**作者**: Sen Jia, Siqi Wei, Junyu Wang, Zijun Lv, Pan Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29313v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29313v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29313v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multimodal ocean modeling remains challenging due to substantial heterogeneity in data structures, physical semantics, and spatiotemporal characteristics across satellite imagery, gridded oceanographic fields, and buoy observations. Existing approaches largely focus on task-specific ocean modeling, while learning transferable representations from heterogeneous observations remains an open problem. To address this challenge, we propose YingLan, a multimodal ocean foundation model that integrates physics-attribute-conditioned dynamic encoding, Spatiotemporal Increment Coupling (STIC), and ocean-state evolution-aware pretraining. Specifically, the dynamic encoding mechanism generates adaptive input projections conditioned on physical semantics and observation characteristics, enabling flexible representation of heterogeneous ocean variables. STIC models multiscale spatiotemporal dependencies through cross-scale interactions of latent temporal increments. The pretraining strategy further combines masked observation reconstruction with temporal evolution objectives to facilitate the learning of coherent ocean-state representations across different prediction horizons. YingLan is implemented using MindSpore and trained on Huawei Ascend 910B NPUs, demonstrating its compatibility with domestic AI computing infrastructure. In a seven-day sea surface salinity (SSS) forecasting task conducted in the Shenzhen coastal waters, YingLan achieves an average RMSE of 0.687508 PSU, corresponding to relative error reductions of 0.58%, 5.31%, and 12.42% compared with LangYa, XiHe, and GloNet2, respectively. These results provide initial evidence of the model's effectiveness in regional ocean forecasting, while broader cross-task and cross-regional evaluations remain necessary to assess its generalization capabilities.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing approaches largely focus on task-specific ocean modeling, while learning transferable representations from heterogeneous observations remains an open problem. To address this challenge, we propose YingLan, a multimodal ocean foundation model that integrates physics-attribute-conditioned dynamic encoding, Spatiotemporal Increment Coupling (STIC), and ocean-state evolution-aware pretraining. Specifically, the dynamic encoding mechanism generates adaptive input projections conditioned on physical semantics and observation characteristics, enabling flexible representation of heterogeneous ocean variables. STIC models multiscale spatiotemporal dependencies through cross-scale interactions of latent temporal increments. The pretraining strategy further combines masked observation reconstruction with temporal evolution objectives to facilitate the learning of coherent ocean-state representations across different prediction horizons. YingLan is implemented using MindSpore and trained on Huawei Ascend 910B NPUs, demonstrating its compatibility with domestic AI computing infrastructure. In a seven-day sea surface salinity (SSS) forecasting task conducted in the Shenzhen coastal waters, YingLan achieves an average RMSE of 0.687508 PSU, corresponding to relative error reductions of 0.58%, 5.31%, and 12.42% compared with LangYa, XiHe, and GloNet2, respectively. These results provide initial evidence of the model's effectiveness in regional ocean forecasting, while broader cross-task and cross-regional evaluations remain necessary to assess its generalization capabilities.

</details>

## 34. MOSAIC: An Omni-Modal Benchmark for Long-Term Memory and Cross-Modal Reasoning in Conversational Agents

**中文标题**: MOSAIC: An Omni-Modal Benchmark for Long-Term Memory and Cross-Modal Reasoning in Conversational Agents

**作者**: Hong Ting Tsang, Boyi Zhang, Haoyu Huang, Jiaxin Bai, Yufei Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29249v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29249v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29249v1)

**类别**: cs.CL, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Omni-modal assistants must remember not only what users say, but also what they show and convey. Existing long-term memory benchmarks are mostly text-and-image or report a single end-to-end score, so they cannot locate whether evidence is lost when it is represented, retrieved, delivered, or read. We introduce MOSAIC, a benchmark of 923 questions over persona-grounded histories of 11-24 sessions spanning text, image, video, and audio. MOSAIC constructs targeted spatial, temporal, and prosodic signals rather than decorative media, with the strongest causal validation for spatial information and selector-level validation for annotated prosody, and pairs them with a matched diagnostic protocol that freezes the question, reader, prompt, and budget while intervening on representation, retrieval, and delivery. Matched decomposition shows that representation loss is modality-dependent, residual reading dominates even under gold evidence, and abstention remains weak on these controls: strong answerable scores coexist with failures to refuse. MOSAIC thus turns omni-modal memory evaluation from a leaderboard into a diagnosis of write, retrieve, deliver, and read failures.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Omni-modal assistants must remember not only what users say, but also what they show and convey. Existing long-term memory benchmarks are mostly text-and-image or report a single end-to-end score, so they cannot locate whether evidence is lost when it is represented, retrieved, delivered, or read. We introduce MOSAIC, a benchmark of 923 questions over persona-grounded histories of 11-24 sessions spanning text, image, video, and audio. MOSAIC constructs targeted spatial, temporal, and prosodic signals rather than decorative media, with the strongest causal validation for spatial information and selector-level validation for annotated prosody, and pairs them with a matched diagnostic protocol that freezes the question, reader, prompt, and budget while intervening on representation, retrieval, and delivery. Matched decomposition shows that representation loss is modality-dependent, residual reading dominates even under gold evidence, and abstention remains weak on these controls: strong answerable scores coexist with failures to refuse. MOSAIC thus turns omni-modal memory evaluation from a leaderboard into a diagnosis of write, retrieve, deliver, and read failures.

</details>

## 35. Dual-Channel Contrastive Encoding of Sperm Whale Coda

**中文标题**: Dual-Channel Contrastive Encoding of Sperm Whale Coda

**作者**: João Victor Quintanilha, Emma Virnelli, Shuo Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29101v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29101v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29101v1)

**项目主页**: https://dcce-research-site.vercel.app.

**类别**: cs.SD, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Sperm whales (Physeter macrocephalus) communicate through short click sequences called codas, whose structure includes the rhythmic pattern of inter-click intervals (ICI) and the spectral composition of the clicks. In this paper, we propose the Dual-Channel Contrastive Encoder (DCCE), which explicitly encodes rhythm and spectral information with separate encoders before fusing them. We evaluate DCCE on three coda-understanding tasks: social unit, coda type, and individual identification. We compare DCCE against strong baselines from literature under a common linear-probe protocol and analyze the role of each channel and training objective through ablations. On 1,383 codas, shared partitions and validation-only layer selection support three random splits and three recording-date-disjoint folds. DCCE with unit pairing and type supervision achieves mean random-split macro-F1 of 0.901/0.865/0.597 for unit/type/identity. Raw ICI remains strongest for type (0.987), and ICI plus temporal mel statistics is a strong identity reference (0.724). An auxiliary identity objective improves spectral-branch identity prediction but does not improve the mean fused-identity score in this configuration. Date-grouped evaluation and four year holdouts expose generalization limits. The results support task-dependent combinations of timing and spectral information, without establishing independent biological channels or general architectural superiority. Code, notebooks, and complete results are available at https://dcce-research-site.vercel.app.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Sperm whales (Physeter macrocephalus) communicate through short click sequences called codas, whose structure includes the rhythmic pattern of inter-click intervals (ICI) and the spectral composition of the clicks. In this paper, we propose the Dual-Channel Contrastive Encoder (DCCE), which explicitly encodes rhythm and spectral information with separate encoders before fusing them. We evaluate DCCE on three coda-understanding tasks: social unit, coda type, and individual identification. We compare DCCE against strong baselines from literature under a common linear-probe protocol and analyze the role of each channel and training objective through ablations. On 1,383 codas, shared partitions and validation-only layer selection support three random splits and three recording-date-disjoint folds. DCCE with unit pairing and type supervision achieves mean random-split macro-F1 of 0.901/0.865/0.597 for unit/type/identity. Raw ICI remains strongest for type (0.987), and ICI plus temporal mel statistics is a strong identity reference (0.724). An auxiliary identity objective improves spectral-branch identity prediction but does not improve the mean fused-identity score in this configuration. Date-grouped evaluation and four year holdouts expose generalization limits. The results support task-dependent combinations of timing and spectral information, without establishing independent biological channels or general architectural superiority. Code, notebooks, and complete results are available at https://dcce-research-site.vercel.app.

</details>

## 36. GRAIL: From Saliency to Semantics in Time-Series Explanation

**中文标题**: GRAIL: From Saliency to Semantics in Time-Series Explanation

**作者**: Enver Menadjiev, Yiqiu Shen, Jaesik Choi, Julia Stoyanovich

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29036v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29036v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29036v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Explaining a time-series model requires both locating the evidence and extracting meaningful knowledge from it. Yet a single timestep is not a semantic unit: the meaningful patterns live across many samples, not in any one of them. Per-timestep saliency is therefore noisy, high-dimensional, and unstable. Existing methods address this by introducing fixed-size windows and treating them as independent players in a cooperative game. Evaluating a coalition of such players requires defining a ``baseline'' region, which prior work tackles with fixed counterfactuals (e.g., a flat line, noise, or a spectral surrogate). This choice frequently dominates the resulting explanation, obscuring the model's true decisions.   To address these issues, we propose GRAIL, a region-based time-series explanation framework that uses domain-specific intervals, such as the P-wave and the QRS complex of an electrocardiogram (ECG) beat, as players. We describe each region with human-readable features and generate counterfactual baselines with an on-manifold conditional generator. Such a choice of players also admits non-temporal features: static covariates enter as singleton players attributed with the temporal regions on one scale, so a patient's tabular evidence (e.g., age, sex) is weighed directly against the signal. We attribute the predictions to the features via Owen values with common-random-number variance reduction. Experimental results on both synthetic and real data show that the proposed framework produces faithful and robust explanations, evaluated under matched on- and off-manifold operators, offering meaningful knowledge to domain experts.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Explaining a time-series model requires both locating the evidence and extracting meaningful knowledge from it. Yet a single timestep is not a semantic unit: the meaningful patterns live across many samples, not in any one of them. Per-timestep saliency is therefore noisy, high-dimensional, and unstable. Existing methods address this by introducing fixed-size windows and treating them as independent players in a cooperative game. Evaluating a coalition of such players requires defining a ``baseline'' region, which prior work tackles with fixed counterfactuals (e.g., a flat line, noise, or a spectral surrogate). This choice frequently dominates the resulting explanation, obscuring the model's true decisions.   To address these issues, we propose GRAIL, a region-based time-series explanation framework that uses domain-specific intervals, such as the P-wave and the QRS complex of an electrocardiogram (ECG) beat, as players. We describe each region with human-readable features and generate counterfactual baselines with an on-manifold conditional generator. Such a choice of players also admits non-temporal features: static covariates enter as singleton players attributed with the temporal regions on one scale, so a patient's tabular evidence (e.g., age, sex) is weighed directly against the signal. We attribute the predictions to the features via Owen values with common-random-number variance reduction. Experimental results on both synthetic and real data show that the proposed framework produces faithful and robust explanations, evaluated under matched on- and off-manifold operators, offering meaningful knowledge to domain experts.

</details>

## 37. Spacetime Splines for Adaptive Robot Motions

**中文标题**: Spacetime Splines for Adaptive Robot Motions

**作者**: Boxuan Zhang, Ante Marić, Sylvain Calinon

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29839v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29839v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29839v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Movement primitives commonly represent robot mo- tions through basis functions indexed by time or phase. Inspired by spacetime representations employed in physics, we propose a spline-based formulation in which spatial geometry and temporal progression are represented within a common set of continuous variables. The proposed method jointly estimates a shared Bezier curve, while allowing spatial and temporal model complexity to be adapted independently (e.g., demonstration-specific phase modulation). As use case, we consider an alternating optimization process that simultaneously estimates path intervals observed in partial demonstrations. Simulation experiments demonstrate accurate joint recovery, refinement through modifications in model complexity and continuity, and improved robustness to variance in the demonstration set. Finally, we demonstrate online retiming and synchronization capabilities in a patient-turning task assisted by a robot, evaluated with a physical manikin.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Movement primitives commonly represent robot mo- tions through basis functions indexed by time or phase. Inspired by spacetime representations employed in physics, we propose a spline-based formulation in which spatial geometry and temporal progression are represented within a common set of continuous variables. The proposed method jointly estimates a shared Bezier curve, while allowing spatial and temporal model complexity to be adapted independently (e.g., demonstration-specific phase modulation). As use case, we consider an alternating optimization process that simultaneously estimates path intervals observed in partial demonstrations. Simulation experiments demonstrate accurate joint recovery, refinement through modifications in model complexity and continuity, and improved robustness to variance in the demonstration set. Finally, we demonstrate online retiming and synchronization capabilities in a patient-turning task assisted by a robot, evaluated with a physical manikin.

</details>

## 38. From Acceptance and Avoidance to Affective Viscosity: Designing Social Agents Intermediating in Human Relationships

**中文标题**: From Acceptance and Avoidance to Affective Viscosity: Designing Social Agents Intermediating in Human Relationships

**作者**: Tomoko Yonezawa

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29674v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29674v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29674v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.HC

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

As social agents become embedded in human relationships, they are expected not only to provide smooth assistance but also to handle rejection, discomfort, jealousy, persistence, and other forms of social tension. This paper connects two research directions: acceptance and avoidance in response to an agent's gaze, approach, and contact, and affective viscosity as a model of how negative affect accumulates, persists, is suppressed, and is expressed over time. We argue that avoidance should not be treated merely as a failure of interaction, but as a relational signal that indicates boundaries between humans and agents. Building on this view, affective viscosity provides a design perspective for agents that maintain, suppress, or express negative states depending on social relationships and interaction histories. We discuss several ongoing design cases, including triadic jealousy, discomfort suppression, patience timing, and spatiotemporal stickiness, and consider how these cases lead to broader questions of blame and accountable intervention. Rather than designing agents as simple scapegoats or artificial villains, we ask how agents can intermediate in problematic social situations while making their intervention traceable, limited, contestable, and repairable.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

As social agents become embedded in human relationships, they are expected not only to provide smooth assistance but also to handle rejection, discomfort, jealousy, persistence, and other forms of social tension. This paper connects two research directions: acceptance and avoidance in response to an agent's gaze, approach, and contact, and affective viscosity as a model of how negative affect accumulates, persists, is suppressed, and is expressed over time. We argue that avoidance should not be treated merely as a failure of interaction, but as a relational signal that indicates boundaries between humans and agents. Building on this view, affective viscosity provides a design perspective for agents that maintain, suppress, or express negative states depending on social relationships and interaction histories. We discuss several ongoing design cases, including triadic jealousy, discomfort suppression, patience timing, and spatiotemporal stickiness, and consider how these cases lead to broader questions of blame and accountable intervention. Rather than designing agents as simple scapegoats or artificial villains, we ask how agents can intermediate in problematic social situations while making their intervention traceable, limited, contestable, and repairable.

</details>

## 39. Mixture of Flow Matching and Particle Filter for Proprioception-Based Online Contact Estimation

**中文标题**: Mixture of Flow Matching and Particle Filter for Proprioception-Based Online Contact Estimation

**作者**: Seo Wook Han, Min Jun Kim

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29568v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29568v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29568v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

In this article, we propose ContactFlowFilter, a novel approach for contact estimation that determines the number of active contacts, their locations, and the associated force vectors. We consider a robot manipulator equipped with joint torque sensors and a base force/torque sensor; such a proprioceptive-only setup presents a challenging inverse problem characterized by (i) multi-modality from non-unique solutions, (ii) temporal dependencies in state evolution, and (iii) discrete contact events involving contact addition/removal. Existing approaches, such as particle filters (PFs) and learning-based methods, often struggle to address these challenges simultaneously. To overcome this, ContactFlowFilter integrates a generative model into the PF framework. The recursive structure of the PF captures temporal dependencies, while the flow matching model serves as an auxiliary proposal distribution to track multi-modal beliefs and detect contact events. Extensive validation in both simulation and real-world experiments confirms the effectiveness of the proposed method. In real-world settings, ContactFlowFilter achieves the lowest dissimilarity from the target distribution, with errors of only 0.36 cm and 0.88 cm for single- and dual-contact scenarios, respectively, while operating at 0.69 ms and 2.69 ms.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We consider a robot manipulator equipped with joint torque sensors and a base force/torque sensor; such a proprioceptive-only setup presents a challenging inverse problem characterized by (i) multi-modality from non-unique solutions, (ii) temporal dependencies in state evolution, and (iii) discrete contact events involving contact addition/removal. Existing approaches, such as particle filters (PFs) and learning-based methods, often struggle to address these challenges simultaneously. To overcome this, ContactFlowFilter integrates a generative model into the PF framework. The recursive structure of the PF captures temporal dependencies, while the flow matching model serves as an auxiliary proposal distribution to track multi-modal beliefs and detect contact events. Extensive validation in both simulation and real-world experiments confirms the effectiveness of the proposed method. In real-world settings, ContactFlowFilter achieves the lowest dissimilarity from the target distribution, with errors of only 0.36 cm and 0.88 cm for single- and dual-contact scenarios, respectively, while operating at 0.69 ms and 2.69 ms.

</details>

## 40. A2I-Calib++: A Kinematics-Driven Anti-noise Active Calibration Framework for Whole-Body Multi-IMU Systems on Legged Robots

**中文标题**: A2I-Calib++: A Kinematics-Driven Anti-noise Active Calibration Framework for Whole-Body Multi-IMU Systems on Legged Robots

**作者**: Fangyu Jiang, Chaoran Xiong, Kehui Ma, Zhen Sun, Zeyu Zhang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29438v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29438v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29438v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recently, multi-node inertial measurement unit (multi-IMU) systems have shown promise for robust odometry in legged robots. Multi-IMU fusion can improve legged robot odometry but requires accurate spatiotemporal calibration among the sensors. Existing multi-IMU calibration methods, designed for aerial or handheld platforms, face challenges on legged robots because routine locomotion may provide insufficient excitation, while foot slips, impacts, and vibrations introduce measurement disturbances. To address these challenges, we propose A2I-Calib++, a kinematics-driven anti-noise active calibration framework for body-mounted IMUs covered by the robot kinematic model. It estimates temporal offsets, extrinsic rotations and translations, and inertial biases through temporal correlation search followed by analytic spatial and bias estimation, without artificial calibration targets or complex parameter tuning for the target IMU. A2I-Calib++ comprises three core modules: 1) a Noise-Isotropy-Inspired Trajectory supported by a finite-horizon conditioning proposition that generates sufficient excitation to limit noise sensitivity; 2) a constraint-aware Adversarial Motion Priors controller that combines the anti-noise excitation with a rolling-contact reward for contact-aware execution; and 3) a contact-constrained virtual IMU that fuses pre-calibrated calf IMUs and joint kinematics under a rolling-contact model to construct the calibration reference. Furthermore, evaluations on both simulation and real-world quadruped platforms demonstrate notably reduced noise sensitivity and simulation calibration errors compared to conventional methods, together with repeatable real-world calibration and improved proprioceptive odometry accuracy for legged robots.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recently, multi-node inertial measurement unit (multi-IMU) systems have shown promise for robust odometry in legged robots. Multi-IMU fusion can improve legged robot odometry but requires accurate spatiotemporal calibration among the sensors. Existing multi-IMU calibration methods, designed for aerial or handheld platforms, face challenges on legged robots because routine locomotion may provide insufficient excitation, while foot slips, impacts, and vibrations introduce measurement disturbances. To address these challenges, we propose A2I-Calib++, a kinematics-driven anti-noise active calibration framework for body-mounted IMUs covered by the robot kinematic model. It estimates temporal offsets, extrinsic rotations and translations, and inertial biases through temporal correlation search followed by analytic spatial and bias estimation, without artificial calibration targets or complex parameter tuning for the target IMU. A2I-Calib++ comprises three core modules: 1) a Noise-Isotropy-Inspired Trajectory supported by a finite-horizon conditioning proposition that generates sufficient excitation to limit noise sensitivity; 2) a constraint-aware Adversarial Motion Priors controller that combines the anti-noise excitation with a rolling-contact reward for contact-aware execution; and 3) a contact-constrained virtual IMU that fuses pre-calibrated calf IMUs and joint kinematics under a rolling-contact model to construct the calibration reference. Furthermore, evaluations on both simulation and real-world quadruped platforms demonstrate notably reduced noise sensitivity and simulation calibration errors compared to conventional methods, together with repeatable real-world calibration and improved proprioceptive odometry accuracy for legged robots.

</details>

---

## 41. Relational Geometry Forcing for World Action Models

**中文标题**: Relational Geometry Forcing for World Action Models

**作者**: Yuan Xu, Yixiang Chen, Qisen Ma, Jiabing Yang, Peiyan Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29282v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29282v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29282v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World Action Models (WAMs) have shown strong potential for robot manipulation by jointly learning visual dynamics and executable actions. However, their video and action objectives do not explicitly constrain the 3D geometry that is crucial for precise manipulation. Recently, geometry-aware WAMs introduce depth or 4D prediction branches, whereas representation alignment methods supervise intermediate WAM features with pretrained 3D foundation models. Yet we find that such pointwise alignment benefits the video features but degrades action learning, since dense geometric feature targets overly restrict compact action features. To address this limitation, we propose Relational Geometry Forcing (RGF), which transfers geometric priors from a frozen 3D foundation model by aligning pairwise feature relations instead of pointwise features. Specifically, RGF applies three complementary relational objectives, where intra-frame and cross-view relations transfer scene geometry and multi-view correspondence to the video features, while action-temporal relations couple action dynamics to geometric scene evolution. Applied only during training, RGF requires no explicit geometry branch or extra inference module. Extensive experiments in simulation and the real world demonstrate the effectiveness of Relational Geometry Forcing, showing consistent gains across diverse manipulation scenarios and WAM paradigms.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World Action Models (WAMs) have shown strong potential for robot manipulation by jointly learning visual dynamics and executable actions. However, their video and action objectives do not explicitly constrain the 3D geometry that is crucial for precise manipulation. Recently, geometry-aware WAMs introduce depth or 4D prediction branches, whereas representation alignment methods supervise intermediate WAM features with pretrained 3D foundation models. Yet we find that such pointwise alignment benefits the video features but degrades action learning, since dense geometric feature targets overly restrict compact action features. To address this limitation, we propose Relational Geometry Forcing (RGF), which transfers geometric priors from a frozen 3D foundation model by aligning pairwise feature relations instead of pointwise features. Specifically, RGF applies three complementary relational objectives, where intra-frame and cross-view relations transfer scene geometry and multi-view correspondence to the video features, while action-temporal relations couple action dynamics to geometric scene evolution. Applied only during training, RGF requires no explicit geometry branch or extra inference module. Extensive experiments in simulation and the real world demonstrate the effectiveness of Relational Geometry Forcing, showing consistent gains across diverse manipulation scenarios and WAM paradigms.

</details>

## 42. VX-One: Typed, Calibrated Decision Heads for Real-Time Perimeter Triage - An empirical study of unknown-attack honesty, cascade gray zones, and calibration under drift

**中文标题**: VX-One: Typed, Calibrated Decision Heads for Real-Time Perimeter Triage - An empirical study of unknown-attack honesty, cascade gray zones, and calibration under drift

**作者**: Juan S. Santillana

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.30090v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30090v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.30090v1)

**类别**: cs.CR, cs.CL, cs.LG, cs.NI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Perimeter triage faces a latency/semantics trade-off: signature engines are fast and format-safe but blind to unseen attacks, while generative models are semantic but slow and produce unparseable output. In late 2026 a class of commercial System One decision models (TypeSafe Jev; Microsoft-Decision-1) began selling typed decision primitives - binary gates, closed-set choices, ordinal scores - as product APIs, with no academic treatment. We present VX-One: a small multi-head network (100K-10M parameters) whose outputs are these primitives - a calibrated gate, a choice over a closed candidate set that always includes an explicit unknown option, and an ordinal score, trained multi-task, temperature-calibrated per head (~0.7-1.7 ms p50 on CPU). We evaluate it on UNSW-NB15, CIC-IDS2017, EMBER2024 against calibrated XGBoost, Random-Forest, MLP, Kitsune, and measure a two-phase cascade (Suricata with 52,674 Emerging-Threats rules -> VX-One) over five days of CIC-IDS2017. Findings: (i) VX-One wins detection (AUROC/FPR@95%TPR) on UNSW-NB15 and under temporal drift on CIC-IDS2017 (0.711 vs 0.637), but XGBoost wins multi-class attribution on all three benchmarks; (ii) leave-one-family-out gate detects never-seen families on 9/9 UNSW folds yet drops below chance on CIC recon family, and abstention threshold transfers poorly across benchmarks; (iii) on payload-less reconstructions signature engines resolve ~0.1% of flows - gray zone ~99.9% of traffic, recovered by Phase 2 (combined recall >=0.978); (iv) calibration collapses under temporal drift (ECE 0.04->0.40) for every method while ranking partially survives. We provide a measured account of where typed calibrated decisions help perimeter triage, and where they fail.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Perimeter triage faces a latency/semantics trade-off: signature engines are fast and format-safe but blind to unseen attacks, while generative models are semantic but slow and produce unparseable output. We evaluate it on UNSW-NB15, CIC-IDS2017, EMBER2024 against calibrated XGBoost, Random-Forest, MLP, Kitsune, and measure a two-phase cascade (Suricata with 52,674 Emerging-Threats rules -> VX-One) over five days of CIC-IDS2017. Findings: (i) VX-One wins detection (AUROC/FPR@95%TPR) on UNSW-NB15 and under temporal drift on CIC-IDS2017 (0.711 vs 0.637), but XGBoost wins multi-class attribution on all three benchmarks; (ii) leave-one-family-out gate detects never-seen families on 9/9 UNSW folds yet drops below chance on CIC recon family, and abstention threshold transfers poorly across benchmarks; (iii) on payload-less reconstructions signature engines resolve ~0.1% of flows - gray zone ~99.9% of traffic, recovered by Phase 2 (combined recall >=0.978); (iv) calibration collapses under temporal drift (ECE 0.04->0.40) for every method while ranking partially survives. We provide a measured account of where typed calibrated decisions help perimeter triage, and where they fail.

</details>

## 43. SCDM: Scalable Causal Discovery in Nonlinear Temporal Systems with Meta-Learning

**中文标题**: SCDM: Scalable Causal Discovery in Nonlinear Temporal Systems with Meta-Learning

**作者**: Jingbo Wang, Kegeng Tang, Zihao Wang, Shaogang Ren

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29992v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29992v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29992v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Causal discovery from nonlinear multivariate time series is challenging in high-dimensional systems, where the number of candidate directed relations grows quadratically with the number of variables. Existing methods are often limited by target-wise model fitting, repeated conditional testing, or expensive graph-search procedures. We propose SCDM, a shared-parameter meta-learning framework for scalable temporal causal discovery. SCDM treats each target variable as a task while learning a shared temporal predictor and a shared causal-strength matrix across tasks. Each meta-episode updates only a subset of target-variable tasks, allowing the model to accumulate structural evidence across the full system without exhaustive target-wise optimization. After training, a post-training graph readout converts the learned predictor into continuous directed causal scores for threshold-independent evaluation. We also provide a finite-error recovery principle showing that, under fully observed delayed-system assumptions and a positive risk-gap condition, thresholding SCDM scores recovers the graph when statistical, meta-optimization, and readout errors are below the population margin. Experiments on controlled synthetic, neuroimaging-inspired, high-dimensional, and real-world temporal benchmarks demonstrate competitive causal recovery and improved scalability in large temporal systems.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Causal discovery from nonlinear multivariate time series is challenging in high-dimensional systems, where the number of candidate directed relations grows quadratically with the number of variables. Existing methods are often limited by target-wise model fitting, repeated conditional testing, or expensive graph-search procedures. We propose SCDM, a shared-parameter meta-learning framework for scalable temporal causal discovery. SCDM treats each target variable as a task while learning a shared temporal predictor and a shared causal-strength matrix across tasks. Each meta-episode updates only a subset of target-variable tasks, allowing the model to accumulate structural evidence across the full system without exhaustive target-wise optimization. After training, a post-training graph readout converts the learned predictor into continuous directed causal scores for threshold-independent evaluation. We also provide a finite-error recovery principle showing that, under fully observed delayed-system assumptions and a positive risk-gap condition, thresholding SCDM scores recovers the graph when statistical, meta-optimization, and readout errors are below the population margin. Experiments on controlled synthetic, neuroimaging-inspired, high-dimensional, and real-world temporal benchmarks demonstrate competitive causal recovery and improved scalability in large temporal systems.

</details>

## 44. NeuroCodec: EEG-Guided Latent-Domain Generative Model for High-Fidelity Target Speaker Extraction

**中文标题**: NeuroCodec: EEG-Guided Latent-Domain Generative Model for High-Fidelity Target Speaker Extraction

**作者**: Julian Silva, Jaliya Kodithuwakku, Navindu Sirimanna, Shaveen Herath, Wageesha N. Manamperi et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29815v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29815v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29815v1)

**类别**: eess.AS, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Driven by recent advancements in hearing assistive devices, target speaker extraction (TSE) in multi-talker environments has drawn significant attention. To address this, hearing technologies identify attention using neural signals recorded via electroencephalography (EEG) to guide TSE, enabling neuro-steered auditory attention decoding. However, most existing techniques rely on time-frequency masking, which suffers from low fidelity in spectro-temporally overlapping regions and is often restricted to narrowband audio. In contrast, we introduce NeuroCodec, which, to the best of our knowledge, is the first generative EEG-guided TSE framework operating in the latent space of a neural audio codec. Specifically, NeuroCodec models spatial EEG dependencies via graph convolutions and fuses cross-modal EEG and speech representations through attention mechanisms and Mamba-based temporal modeling. We further introduce a six-phase training curriculum that progressively incorporates latent reconstruction, multi-scale spectral, and adversarial objectives to ensure stable, high-fidelity speech generation. Experiments on the public Cocktail Party and KUL datasets show NeuroCodec outperforms baselines, across several perceptual metrics. A listening experiment with 20 participants validated that NeuroCodec offers greater perceptual accuracy, achieving a mean score of 82.24 compared with 49.63 for M3ANet. Additionally, the proposed method is seen to enable 44.1 kHz reconstruction with spectral content extending up to 20 kHz, further supporting the perceptual results. Finally, we extend NeuroCodec with a causal streaming formulation suited for real-time processing, providing a basis for hearing assistive devices.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

To address this, hearing technologies identify attention using neural signals recorded via electroencephalography (EEG) to guide TSE, enabling neuro-steered auditory attention decoding. However, most existing techniques rely on time-frequency masking, which suffers from low fidelity in spectro-temporally overlapping regions and is often restricted to narrowband audio. In contrast, we introduce NeuroCodec, which, to the best of our knowledge, is the first generative EEG-guided TSE framework operating in the latent space of a neural audio codec. Specifically, NeuroCodec models spatial EEG dependencies via graph convolutions and fuses cross-modal EEG and speech representations through attention mechanisms and Mamba-based temporal modeling. We further introduce a six-phase training curriculum that progressively incorporates latent reconstruction, multi-scale spectral, and adversarial objectives to ensure stable, high-fidelity speech generation. Experiments on the public Cocktail Party and KUL datasets show NeuroCodec outperforms baselines, across several perceptual metrics. A listening experiment with 20 participants validated that NeuroCodec offers greater perceptual accuracy, achieving a mean score of 82.24 compared with 49.63 for M3ANet. Additionally, the proposed method is seen to enable 44.1 kHz reconstruction with spectral content extending up to 20 kHz, further supporting the perceptual results. Finally, we extend NeuroCodec with a causal streaming formulation suited for real-time processing, providing a basis for hearing assistive devices.

</details>

## 45. Leveraging Time-Series Foundation Models for Financial Crime Detection

**中文标题**: Leveraging Time-Series Foundation Models for Financial Crime Detection

**作者**: Danae Natalie Savvidi, H. Çağrı Bilgi, Kubilay Atasu, Arie van Deursen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29667v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29667v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29667v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Financial crime detection requires identifying illicit activity within large transaction networks, where suspicious behaviour is rare and unfolds over time. Existing graph-based methods capture relationships among accounts but typically underuse the temporal behaviour encoded in account transaction histories. We introduce Temporal-Context Edge Enrichment (TCEE), a framework that addresses this gap by summarising each account's transaction history with a frozen time-series foundation model (TSFM), and enriching each transaction's representation with the relevant temporal context from these summaries. These enriched representations can be scored directly for edge classification, or fed into a graph neural network (GNN) as edge features to capture broader structural information. Across six AMLWorld datasets, TCEE improves PR-AUC over the strongest evaluated baseline on five by a relative 10.8% on average, while training 6.0x faster end-to-end. Feeding TCEE's representations into a GNN raises PR-AUC a further 4.8% on the four smaller datasets. On an Ethereum phishing dataset, we show that TSFM-generated account representations fed into a GNN are also effective for node classification, improving PR-AUC by 14.5% over the strongest baseline. As TSFMs continue to advance, our results suggest they can strengthen financial crime detection by capturing temporal signals that complement graph-based approaches.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We introduce Temporal-Context Edge Enrichment (TCEE), a framework that addresses this gap by summarising each account's transaction history with a frozen time-series foundation model (TSFM), and enriching each transaction's representation with the relevant temporal context from these summaries. These enriched representations can be scored directly for edge classification, or fed into a graph neural network (GNN) as edge features to capture broader structural information. Across six AMLWorld datasets, TCEE improves PR-AUC over the strongest evaluated baseline on five by a relative 10.8% on average, while training 6.0x faster end-to-end. Feeding TCEE's representations into a GNN raises PR-AUC a further 4.8% on the four smaller datasets. On an Ethereum phishing dataset, we show that TSFM-generated account representations fed into a GNN are also effective for node classification, improving PR-AUC by 14.5% over the strongest baseline. As TSFMs continue to advance, our results suggest they can strengthen financial crime detection by capturing temporal signals that complement graph-based approaches.

</details>

## 46. BrainCodes: A Lightweight EEG Foundation Model over Discrete Neural Codes

**中文标题**: BrainCodes: A Lightweight EEG Foundation Model over Discrete Neural Codes

**作者**: Kleanthis Avramidis, Ivan Tashev, Dimitra Emmanouilidou

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29320v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29320v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29320v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.NE

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent electroencephalography (EEG) foundation models increasingly emphasize model scale, potentially limiting their deployment on resource-constrained devices and in everyday environments. We argue that transferable EEG features can instead be learned with a compact architecture and introduce BrainCodes, a lightweight foundation model pretrained through self-supervised learning on more than 800 hours of recordings from over 1,000 participants. Our architecture combines a quantization-based neural tokenizer with a spatiotemporal encoder that supports arbitrary electrode configurations. The pretraining objective explicitly encourages representations that capture spatial connectivity through channel positional encoding, and sensitivity to narrowband neural oscillations through an auxiliary training objective. Our model matches or surpasses the state of the art across diverse neurophysiological monitoring tasks, including sleep staging, attention assessment, and cognitive load estimation. BrainCodes transfers effectively to low-resource wearable devices while using up to 97% fewer trainable parameters than comparable architectures. Beyond predictive accuracy, our evaluation framework characterizes the geometry and invariances of learned EEG representations across measurable spatial, temporal, and spectral signal properties.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Recent electroencephalography (EEG) foundation models increasingly emphasize model scale, potentially limiting their deployment on resource-constrained devices and in everyday environments. We argue that transferable EEG features can instead be learned with a compact architecture and introduce BrainCodes, a lightweight foundation model pretrained through self-supervised learning on more than 800 hours of recordings from over 1,000 participants. Our architecture combines a quantization-based neural tokenizer with a spatiotemporal encoder that supports arbitrary electrode configurations. The pretraining objective explicitly encourages representations that capture spatial connectivity through channel positional encoding, and sensitivity to narrowband neural oscillations through an auxiliary training objective. Our model matches or surpasses the state of the art across diverse neurophysiological monitoring tasks, including sleep staging, attention assessment, and cognitive load estimation. BrainCodes transfers effectively to low-resource wearable devices while using up to 97% fewer trainable parameters than comparable architectures. Beyond predictive accuracy, our evaluation framework characterizes the geometry and invariances of learned EEG representations across measurable spatial, temporal, and spectral signal properties.

</details>

## 47. DISRL: Dual-Scale Incremental Semi-Supervised Reinforcement Learning for Dynamic Community Tracking

**中文标题**: DISRL: Dual-Scale Incremental Semi-Supervised Reinforcement Learning for Dynamic Community Tracking

**作者**: Tianming Zhang, Jinxu Wang, Yixuan Zhang, Zhengyi Yang, Chenyu Hou et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29315v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29315v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29315v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Dynamic local community detection tracks the evolving community of designated seed nodes across graph snapshots. However, it faces two critical challenges: temporal supervision uncertainty, where sparse and outdated labels fail to provide seed-specific guidance under topology changes, and unbounded error cascades, where sequential policy transfer accumulates exploration errors over long horizons. To address these challenges, we propose DISRL, a Dual-scale Incremental Semi-supervised Reinforcement Learning framework. DISRL introduces a supervision construction module that dynamically generates seed-specific guidance from sparse known communities, and a dual-scale community exploration module that stabilizes policy evolution by decoupling short-term adaptation within temporal windows from long-term parameter consolidation across windows. Furthermore, we construct a dynamic graph benchmark with diverse community evolution scenarios. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DISRL consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines under various dynamic settings.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Dynamic local community detection tracks the evolving community of designated seed nodes across graph snapshots. However, it faces two critical challenges: temporal supervision uncertainty, where sparse and outdated labels fail to provide seed-specific guidance under topology changes, and unbounded error cascades, where sequential policy transfer accumulates exploration errors over long horizons. To address these challenges, we propose DISRL, a Dual-scale Incremental Semi-supervised Reinforcement Learning framework. DISRL introduces a supervision construction module that dynamically generates seed-specific guidance from sparse known communities, and a dual-scale community exploration module that stabilizes policy evolution by decoupling short-term adaptation within temporal windows from long-term parameter consolidation across windows. Furthermore, we construct a dynamic graph benchmark with diverse community evolution scenarios. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DISRL consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines under various dynamic settings.

</details>

## 48. Design and Tuning of Multi-Agent Systems Using Bayesian Evolutionary Optimization

**中文标题**: Design and Tuning of Multi-Agent Systems Using Bayesian Evolutionary Optimization

**作者**: Dominik Jehle, Tom Zehle

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29276v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29276v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29276v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.MA

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Designing effective multi-agent systems (MAS) remains a challenging and resource-intensive task. We propose a Bayesian Evolutionary Optimization framework for automatically discovering task-specific MAS topologies. The approach combines evolutionary operators to generate diverse workflow graphs with Bayesian optimization to focus cost intensive evaluations on promising candidates. In contrast to LLM-based candidate selection, our approach uses a graph-aware Gaussian Process and Expected Improvement to provide an explicit selection criterion during evolutionary search. We evaluate the method on BigCodeBench for code generation and on a tabular preprocessing benchmark using TabPFN. On BigCodeBench, evolved architectures achieve the highest overall performance among the evaluated agent systems. On temporal tabular datasets, optimized MAS preprocessing improves over the default TabPFN model on several tasks, while remaining competitive on IID datasets. Overall, the results demonstrate the potential of Bayesian selection for guiding evolutionary MAS search under expensive evaluation budgets.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

The approach combines evolutionary operators to generate diverse workflow graphs with Bayesian optimization to focus cost intensive evaluations on promising candidates. On BigCodeBench, evolved architectures achieve the highest overall performance among the evaluated agent systems. On temporal tabular datasets, optimized MAS preprocessing improves over the default TabPFN model on several tasks, while remaining competitive on IID datasets. Overall, the results demonstrate the potential of Bayesian selection for guiding evolutionary MAS search under expensive evaluation budgets.

</details>

## 49. MA-UITD: Offline Multi-Agent Reward Inference for Characterizing Continuous Social Behavior

**中文标题**: MA-UITD: Offline Multi-Agent Reward Inference for Characterizing Continuous Social Behavior

**作者**: Zeyuan Zhang, Yiyun Yang, Yuan Cheng, Herbert Zheng Wu, Yusi Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29237v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29237v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29237v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, cs.MA

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multi-agent inverse reinforcement learning offers a powerful framework not only for imitation learning but also for understanding the neural computations supporting social interactions and decision-making. Inferring rewards from interacting agents is challenging because observed choices reflect both personal preferences and expectations about others. Existing methods often require additional environment interaction, repeated policy optimization as an inner loop, or restrictive assumptions about environment dynamics and action spaces, limiting their applicability to fixed behavioral datasets. We introduce Multi-Agent Unilateral Inverse Temporal Difference Learning (MA-UITD), an offline method for inferring explicit agent-specific rewards from joint demonstrations in continuous-state environments with either discrete or bounded continuous actions. Its core idea is to represent the long-term consequences of each agent's own actions through belief-conditioned successor features that average over other agents' behavior. Shared reward features and agent-specific preference vectors connect these unilateral values to the target reward function, while inverse temporal-difference consistency and noise-contrastive behavioral learning enable inference without known dynamics or an inner policy-optimization loop. Across simulated cooperative, competitive, and general-sum tasks, MA-UITD outperforms baselines in discrete-action settings and infers behaviorally compatible reward profiles in continuous-action settings. When applied to experimentally collected social-foraging trajectories, MA-UITD inferred distinct spatial and interaction-dependent reward structures for leaders and followers, providing a quantitative characterization of latent behavioral objectives that may be relevant for understanding the neural computations underlying social behavior.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Multi-agent inverse reinforcement learning offers a powerful framework not only for imitation learning but also for understanding the neural computations supporting social interactions and decision-making. Inferring rewards from interacting agents is challenging because observed choices reflect both personal preferences and expectations about others. Existing methods often require additional environment interaction, repeated policy optimization as an inner loop, or restrictive assumptions about environment dynamics and action spaces, limiting their applicability to fixed behavioral datasets. Its core idea is to represent the long-term consequences of each agent's own actions through belief-conditioned successor features that average over other agents' behavior. Across simulated cooperative, competitive, and general-sum tasks, MA-UITD outperforms baselines in discrete-action settings and infers behaviorally compatible reward profiles in continuous-action settings. When applied to experimentally collected social-foraging trajectories, MA-UITD inferred distinct spatial and interaction-dependent reward structures for leaders and followers, providing a quantitative characterization of latent behavioral objectives that may be relevant for understanding the neural computations underlying social behavior.

</details>
