## 50. Delay-Guided Attention for Quality Prediction in Multistage Manufacturing

**中文标题**: Delay-Guided Attention for Quality Prediction in Multistage Manufacturing

**作者**: Jiarui Zhang, Zhaoyang Guan, Zhixuan Xia, Naichen Shi

**发布日期**: 2026-10-21

**arXiv ID**: [2610.29220v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29220v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.29220v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Quality prediction in multistage manufacturing is challenging when inter-stage lags vary over time, as measurements taken at the same timestamp may reflect different process states. Existing approaches often preserve production order but fuse timestamp-aligned features or model delayed dependencies implicitly, without identifying the upstream history relevant to each downstream observation. We propose Delay-Guided Attention for Multi-stage Forecasting (DAMF), which explicitly estimates inter-stage lag distributions and uses them to guide cross-stage information transfer for final quality prediction. For each adjacent stage pair, a lag predictor compares the current downstream representation with candidate lagged upstream representations. An occurrence-magnitude factorization separately estimates the probability of a positive lag and its conditional magnitude, yielding a sample- and stage-pair-specific distribution over discrete lag states without requiring plant-specified delays at inference. DelayAlignment incorporates this soft distribution as a temporal prior into differentiable cross-stage attention, while gated residual fusion regulates information propagation across production stages. For interpretation, segment-wise Viterbi decoding converts pointwise lag distributions into temporally coherent lag paths, whereas forecasting retains the soft distributions for alignment. On the real-world LiquidSugar dataset, DAMF improves final quality prediction over the compared baselines. Evaluations on two controlled-delay benchmarks show more accurate and stable lag recovery, and ablation studies support the contributions of lag prediction, delay-guided attention, and structured temporal decoding.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Quality prediction in multistage manufacturing is challenging when inter-stage lags vary over time, as measurements taken at the same timestamp may reflect different process states. Existing approaches often preserve production order but fuse timestamp-aligned features or model delayed dependencies implicitly, without identifying the upstream history relevant to each downstream observation. We propose Delay-Guided Attention for Multi-stage Forecasting (DAMF), which explicitly estimates inter-stage lag distributions and uses them to guide cross-stage information transfer for final quality prediction. For each adjacent stage pair, a lag predictor compares the current downstream representation with candidate lagged upstream representations. An occurrence-magnitude factorization separately estimates the probability of a positive lag and its conditional magnitude, yielding a sample- and stage-pair-specific distribution over discrete lag states without requiring plant-specified delays at inference. DelayAlignment incorporates this soft distribution as a temporal prior into differentiable cross-stage attention, while gated residual fusion regulates information propagation across production stages. For interpretation, segment-wise Viterbi decoding converts pointwise lag distributions into temporally coherent lag paths, whereas forecasting retains the soft distributions for alignment. On the real-world LiquidSugar dataset, DAMF improves final quality prediction over the compared baselines. Evaluations on two controlled-delay benchmarks show more accurate and stable lag recovery, and ablation studies support the contributions of lag prediction, delay-guided attention, and structured temporal decoding.

</details>

---

## 51. RoPT: Robust 6DoF Object Pose Tracking in Video

**中文标题**: RoPT: Robust 6DoF Object Pose Tracking in Video

**作者**: Yongqing Liang, Yuzhong Chen, Sihang Li, Junsuk Kim, Chase Kidder et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28962v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28962v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28962v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Accurate 6-DoF object pose tracking is essential for robotic interaction, yet conventional monocular pipelines frequently suffer from catastrophic divergence under rapid object motion. We present RoPT, a robust tracking framework engineered to overcome this limitation by unifying visual perception, temporal kinematics, and global re-localization into a tightly coupled closed loop. Unlike existing open-loop trackers, RoPT delivers three critical capabilities that fundamentally alter system resilience. First, the visual perception component tracks object masks to robustly handle partial and severe occlusions. Second, a kinematic factor graph regularizes frame-to-frame updates, forecasts forward pose predictions, and drives a parallel multi-hypothesis verification scheme to bypass local minima. Finally, an active re-localization module transforms the pipeline into a closed-loop system, making it failure-aware and capable of deterministic self-recovery. We demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework across challenging benchmarks alongside real-world robotic demonstrations, achieving significantly higher tracking survivability under rapid motion.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Accurate 6-DoF object pose tracking is essential for robotic interaction, yet conventional monocular pipelines frequently suffer from catastrophic divergence under rapid object motion. We present RoPT, a robust tracking framework engineered to overcome this limitation by unifying visual perception, temporal kinematics, and global re-localization into a tightly coupled closed loop. Unlike existing open-loop trackers, RoPT delivers three critical capabilities that fundamentally alter system resilience. First, the visual perception component tracks object masks to robustly handle partial and severe occlusions. Second, a kinematic factor graph regularizes frame-to-frame updates, forecasts forward pose predictions, and drives a parallel multi-hypothesis verification scheme to bypass local minima. Finally, an active re-localization module transforms the pipeline into a closed-loop system, making it failure-aware and capable of deterministic self-recovery. We demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework across challenging benchmarks alongside real-world robotic demonstrations, achieving significantly higher tracking survivability under rapid motion.

</details>

## 52. Pair-Free Coarse-to-Real Video Generation via Real-to-Coarse Image Supervision

**中文标题**: Pair-Free Coarse-to-Real Video Generation via Real-to-Coarse Image Supervision

**作者**: Gonzalo Gómez-Nogales, Daohan Lu, Peiye Zhuang, Tinghui Zhou, Yi Zhou

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28954v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28954v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28954v1)

**项目主页**: https://gonzalognogales.github.io/coarse2real-sharp/.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Coarse-to-real (C2R) video generation converts coarse animation, simulation, or game-rendered videos into photorealistic videos while preserving scene layout, camera motion, and dynamics. Training such models requires aligned (coarse, realistic) video pairs, which are expensive to create and confine prior systems to narrow synthetic domains. We exploit a simple asymmetry: paired videos are hard to obtain, but paired real-to-coarse images are easy to construct with existing editing and 3D reconstruction tools. Using these image pairs, we train the DINO-R2C model to transfer realistic images to coarse images in DINO feature space, and apply it frame-wise to ordinary real videos to synthesize pseudo-coarse videos. The C2R# model then learns to generate each real video from its pseudo-coarse counterpart, free from needing (coarse, realistic) video pairs. At inference, the DINO-R2C model is discarded and actual coarse videos drive the C2R# model directly. Thus, our pair-free training scales with any real-video corpora rather than with paired synthetic videos. We will release the model and project webpage at https://gonzalognogales.github.io/coarse2real-sharp/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Coarse-to-real (C2R) video generation converts coarse animation, simulation, or game-rendered videos into photorealistic videos while preserving scene layout, camera motion, and dynamics. Training such models requires aligned (coarse, realistic) video pairs, which are expensive to create and confine prior systems to narrow synthetic domains. We exploit a simple asymmetry: paired videos are hard to obtain, but paired real-to-coarse images are easy to construct with existing editing and 3D reconstruction tools. Using these image pairs, we train the DINO-R2C model to transfer realistic images to coarse images in DINO feature space, and apply it frame-wise to ordinary real videos to synthesize pseudo-coarse videos. The C2R# model then learns to generate each real video from its pseudo-coarse counterpart, free from needing (coarse, realistic) video pairs. At inference, the DINO-R2C model is discarded and actual coarse videos drive the C2R# model directly. Thus, our pair-free training scales with any real-video corpora rather than with paired synthetic videos. We will release the model and project webpage at https://gonzalognogales.github.io/coarse2real-sharp/.

</details>

## 53. Sketch2Sim: an end-to-end modular framework for hand-drawn circuit analysis

**中文标题**: Sketch2Sim: an end-to-end modular framework for hand-drawn circuit analysis

**作者**: Mohammad Kawsar, Al Amin Islam Ridoy, Md Rakib Hossain, Farzana Akter, AKM Bahalul Haque et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28836v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28836v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28836v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.CV, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

This study proposes a sketch-to-simulation system to solve handwritten circuit diagrams. The system converts raw images into a Simulation Program with Integrated Circuit Emphasis (SPICE)-compatible netlist and automatically calculates the circuit's nodal voltages and currents. Another objective is to propose a holistic framework that benefits students and educators by enhancing the teaching and learning experience of electric circuits and expediting engineering design reviews. This article presents a multistage pipeline that converts raw photographs of handwritten electric circuits into executable SPICE-like netlists, providing a solution. A dataset of 1,000 hand-drawn Resistor-Inductor-Capacitor (RLC) circuits was curated and annotated for components, text regions, and character recognition. The proposed system decomposes a circuit diagram into three stages: (i) dual You Only Look Once (YOLO)-based detectors for components and text regions, (ii) a Convolutional Recurrent Neural Network (CRNN) with Connectionist Temporal Classification (CTC) and Optical Character Recognition (OCR)-based domain-aware symbol value extraction, and (iii) image processing with thresholding, skeletonization, and Density-Based Spatial Clustering of Applications with Noise (DBSCAN) to automatically identify nodes and map them with corresponding components. The proposed system produces the netlist and solves the circuit. The qualitative and quantitative ablation studies on modular stages, as well as end-to-end tests, demonstrate robust performance across various handwriting styles, lighting conditions, and clutter.This study serves as an interactive learning aid and practical tool for professionals, providing a comprehensive user interface (UI), enabling rapid digitization of circuit sketches for study, design, prototyping, or troubleshooting.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

This study proposes a sketch-to-simulation system to solve handwritten circuit diagrams. The proposed system decomposes a circuit diagram into three stages: (i) dual You Only Look Once (YOLO)-based detectors for components and text regions, (ii) a Convolutional Recurrent Neural Network (CRNN) with Connectionist Temporal Classification (CTC) and Optical Character Recognition (OCR)-based domain-aware symbol value extraction, and (iii) image processing with thresholding, skeletonization, and Density-Based Spatial Clustering of Applications with Noise (DBSCAN) to automatically identify nodes and map them with corresponding components. The proposed system produces the netlist and solves the circuit. The qualitative and quantitative ablation studies on modular stages, as well as end-to-end tests, demonstrate robust performance across various handwriting styles, lighting conditions, and clutter.This study serves as an interactive learning aid and practical tool for professionals, providing a comprehensive user interface (UI), enabling rapid digitization of circuit sketches for study, design, prototyping, or troubleshooting.

</details>

## 54. HorizonComposer: Spatiotemporally Consistent Driving Video Editing with Enriched Traffic Semantics

**中文标题**: HorizonComposer: Spatiotemporally Consistent Driving Video Editing with Enriched Traffic Semantics

**作者**: Mauricio Soroco, Yuqiu Liu, Zaid Tasneem, Francesco Pittaluga, Abhishek Aich et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28829v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28829v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28829v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Instruction-guided video generative models offer a scalable solution for stress-testing autonomous driving systems by simulating diverse environmental conditions. However, driving scenes operate within a dense Operational Design Domain (ODD) governed by strict traffic semantics. When applied to global driving-condition edits, general-purpose instruction-guided video editors frequently corrupt these semantics, leading to structural hallucinations and ``temporal shock.'' This work distills and protects traffic semantics during video editing at three levels. First, we construct a hybrid dataset by combining temporally aligned real-world videos with synthetic edits, balancing foundational scene structure with unambiguous appearance signals. Second, during training, we adapt advantage conditioning from offline reinforcement learning to align the model using dense rewards. We extract dense, pixel-level rewards from the hybrid data to serve as a localized signal and actively steer generation toward structurally reliable pixels. Finally, our model mitigates temporal shock by introducing thinking frames, a transition mechanism that seamlessly reconciles static image conditioning with dynamic video context. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method prevents the corruption of multi-actor dynamics, establishing state-of-the-art spatiotemporal consistency, yielding consistent user preference (39% relative improvement in content preservation and 12% in temporal consistency), and achieving a significant 5% gain in downstream road line segmentation and 7% gain in downstream multi-object detection.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Instruction-guided video generative models offer a scalable solution for stress-testing autonomous driving systems by simulating diverse environmental conditions. However, driving scenes operate within a dense Operational Design Domain (ODD) governed by strict traffic semantics. When applied to global driving-condition edits, general-purpose instruction-guided video editors frequently corrupt these semantics, leading to structural hallucinations and ``temporal shock.'' This work distills and protects traffic semantics during video editing at three levels. First, we construct a hybrid dataset by combining temporally aligned real-world videos with synthetic edits, balancing foundational scene structure with unambiguous appearance signals. Second, during training, we adapt advantage conditioning from offline reinforcement learning to align the model using dense rewards. We extract dense, pixel-level rewards from the hybrid data to serve as a localized signal and actively steer generation toward structurally reliable pixels. Finally, our model mitigates temporal shock by introducing thinking frames, a transition mechanism that seamlessly reconciles static image conditioning with dynamic video context. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method prevents the corruption of multi-actor dynamics, establishing state-of-the-art spatiotemporal consistency, yielding consistent user preference (39% relative improvement in content preservation and 12% in temporal consistency), and achieving a significant 5% gain in downstream road line segmentation and 7% gain in downstream multi-object detection.

</details>

## 55. Complexity-Aware Surgical Video Reasoning Segmentation with Digital Twin and Context Memory

**中文标题**: Complexity-Aware Surgical Video Reasoning Segmentation with Digital Twin and Context Memory

**作者**: Yiqing Shen, Mathias Unberath

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28776v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28776v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28776v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Conventional surgical segmentation relies on closed-set categories or explicit text queries, which cannot resolve implicit queries that require reasoning about workflows, instrument functions, and temporal relationships. To address these limitations, we propose SurgReasonSeg, a complexity-aware method that brings reasoning segmentation over digital twin (DT) representations to surgical video. Specifically, SurgReasonSeg constructs DT representations from surgical foundation models and trains two low-rank adapters (LoRA) of one large language model with reinforcement learning (RL), each specialized for a different query complexity. A lightweight router, also trained with RL, selects between the two adapters to balance latency against accuracy. SurgReasonSeg also sets the temporal context with an adaptive sliding window conditioned on query complexity and workflow phase, and retrieves relevant historical cases from a surgical context memory by workflow similarity and procedural relevance. We further introduce EndoVis-ReasonSeg, a surgical reasoning segmentation benchmark with 1,460 training and 961 test samples across semantic, spatial, and temporal categories. On EndoVis-ReasonSeg, SurgReasonSeg improves J&F over the strongest baseline, ReSurgSAM2, by 0.178, 0.237, and 0.297 on semantic, spatial, and temporal queries, and it also achieves the best results on the Ref-EndoVis17 and Ref-EndoVis18 referring segmentation benchmarks. A backbone-matched attribution protocol separates the contribution of the upstream segmenter from that of the reasoning modules. Our method runs at 18.8 FPS averaged over the query distribution, enabling real-time reasoning segmentation in surgical videos.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Conventional surgical segmentation relies on closed-set categories or explicit text queries, which cannot resolve implicit queries that require reasoning about workflows, instrument functions, and temporal relationships. To address these limitations, we propose SurgReasonSeg, a complexity-aware method that brings reasoning segmentation over digital twin (DT) representations to surgical video. Specifically, SurgReasonSeg constructs DT representations from surgical foundation models and trains two low-rank adapters (LoRA) of one large language model with reinforcement learning (RL), each specialized for a different query complexity. A lightweight router, also trained with RL, selects between the two adapters to balance latency against accuracy. SurgReasonSeg also sets the temporal context with an adaptive sliding window conditioned on query complexity and workflow phase, and retrieves relevant historical cases from a surgical context memory by workflow similarity and procedural relevance. We further introduce EndoVis-ReasonSeg, a surgical reasoning segmentation benchmark with 1,460 training and 961 test samples across semantic, spatial, and temporal categories. On EndoVis-ReasonSeg, SurgReasonSeg improves J&F over the strongest baseline, ReSurgSAM2, by 0.178, 0.237, and 0.297 on semantic, spatial, and temporal queries, and it also achieves the best results on the Ref-EndoVis17 and Ref-EndoVis18 referring segmentation benchmarks. A backbone-matched attribution protocol separates the contribution of the upstream segmenter from that of the reasoning modules. Our method runs at 18.8 FPS averaged over the query distribution, enabling real-time reasoning segmentation in surgical videos.

</details>

## 56. Joints, Camera, Action! Co-Generating 3D Motion and Video for Arbitrary Rigs

**中文标题**: Joints, Camera, Action! Co-Generating 3D Motion and Video for Arbitrary Rigs

**作者**: Noam Rotstein, Yoad Tewel, Chen Tessler, Haggai Maron, Gal Chechik et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28632v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28632v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28632v1)

**项目主页**: https://research.nvidia.com/labs/par/projects/jca/.

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.GR, cs.LG, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video generation models can now depict remarkably realistic motion. Yet it is unclear how to use this motion beyond pixels: to animate a specific 3D asset and replay the animation in its 3D scene. For instance, a video model can show a monkey jumping over a crossbar, but it cannot return that jump as replayable 3D motion placed in the 3D scene. Here we present a model that directly co-generates the video and 3D motion for any supplied rig, along with a camera that registers this motion to the scene. Prior video-motion models either handle only human skeletons, often adding billions of parameters, or triangulate 3D motion from generated 2D pose maps. We instead keep 3D motion in a separate model that supports arbitrary skeletons, and connect it to a pretrained video model through a small set of cross-attention layers. This separation lets the motion model first learn how skeletons move from motion-only data, which is easier to obtain and augment than paired video. Additionally, our model learns to locate each joint in the video frames, which lets us recover the camera and place the 3D motion in the scene. To train across many skeletons, we use a generative video editor to place animated 3D assets in scenes suited to their actions, curating the edits so their known motion remains valid. Together with human and robot video, this yields nearly 200K training pairs. Our evaluations demonstrate that our model animates diverse rigs from text more faithfully than video-first pipelines, and its motion responds to the surrounding scene. It also turns video into rig motion, rig motion into video, and enables keyframe-based control of the 3D motion. Videos and additional results are available on our project page: https://research.nvidia.com/labs/par/projects/jca/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 57. Learning the 4D World through Point Trajectory Forecasting

**中文标题**: Learning the 4D World through Point Trajectory Forecasting

**作者**: Byungwoo Jeon, Sunghwan Hong, Dohyeon Kim, Jonghoon Lee, Jungwoo Park et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28594v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28594v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28594v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reliable multi-view forecasting requires videos from different cameras to describe the same evolving 3D scene. To this end, we introduce Video-Point Model (VPM), a world model that conditions on multi-view video histories and a language instruction to jointly forecast future videos from arbitrary viewpoints and the 3D trajectories of persistent scene points. We extend a pretrained world model with camera-conditioned multi-view processing that integrates complementary observations across cameras, and add a trajectory head that predicts how physical points move through time. Each trajectory tracks a single point across all views, and supervising these trajectories alongside video generation therefore anchors appearance changes in every camera to a shared description of 3D motion. To support training and evaluation, we build VPM-Data, a simulation corpus pairing synchronized multi-view videos with persistent point trajectories, and VP-WorldBench, a forecasting benchmark spanning simulated and real environments. We further extend VPM to robot control by adding robot actions as a token modality in the generator. Action tokens share the point-supervised backbone and are denoised jointly with future videos, thereby grounding the policy in the same 3D-aware representation. On VP-WorldBench and existing 4D generation benchmarks, point supervision improves both video fidelity and 3D motion accuracy over a video-only counterpart, and in simulated and real-robot experiments, it also raises the manipulation success of the action-enabled model.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reliable multi-view forecasting requires videos from different cameras to describe the same evolving 3D scene. To this end, we introduce Video-Point Model (VPM), a world model that conditions on multi-view video histories and a language instruction to jointly forecast future videos from arbitrary viewpoints and the 3D trajectories of persistent scene points. We extend a pretrained world model with camera-conditioned multi-view processing that integrates complementary observations across cameras, and add a trajectory head that predicts how physical points move through time. Each trajectory tracks a single point across all views, and supervising these trajectories alongside video generation therefore anchors appearance changes in every camera to a shared description of 3D motion. To support training and evaluation, we build VPM-Data, a simulation corpus pairing synchronized multi-view videos with persistent point trajectories, and VP-WorldBench, a forecasting benchmark spanning simulated and real environments. We further extend VPM to robot control by adding robot actions as a token modality in the generator. Action tokens share the point-supervised backbone and are denoised jointly with future videos, thereby grounding the policy in the same 3D-aware representation. On VP-WorldBench and existing 4D generation benchmarks, point supervision improves both video fidelity and 3D motion accuracy over a video-only counterpart, and in simulated and real-robot experiments, it also raises the manipulation success of the action-enabled model.

</details>

## 58. Copyright Leakage Detection in AI Generated Videos

**中文标题**: Copyright Leakage Detection in AI Generated Videos

**作者**: Shaif Chowdhury, Moshiur Rahman Tonmoy

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28524v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28524v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28524v1)

**项目主页**: https://zenodo.org/records/21386117

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/Shaif95/video-cp-Leakage.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video generation models can create realistic-looking video footage from images and text prompts. Some of the recent advances have raised concerns about the reproduction of copyrighted visual content. However, copyright leakage detection in generated videos remains largely unexplored.   In this paper, we introduce a new benchmark data set for copyright leakage detection, comprising 50 copyrighted concepts, each with 5 images. For each concept, we have 5 videos created using the image+prompt video generation model and the prompt-only video generation model. Following that, we develop an AI toolset based on a contrastive retrieval framework that formulates copyright leakage detection as a video-to-image retrieval problem by matching aggregated video frame embeddings against a database of copyrighted image embeddings. Our method uses a single similarity threshold and retrieves the most similar copyrighted reference as supporting evidence.   We conducted experiments showing strong retrieval and detection performance, achieving 99.6\% detection accuracy with only a single false negative. These results show the effectiveness of the retrieval-based method for scalable and explainable copyright leakage detection in AI-generated videos. Data can be accessed publicly from https://zenodo.org/records/21386117 and the source code for this project is publicly available at https://github.com/Shaif95/video-cp-Leakage.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Video generation models can create realistic-looking video footage from images and text prompts. Some of the recent advances have raised concerns about the reproduction of copyrighted visual content. However, copyright leakage detection in generated videos remains largely unexplored. </details>

## 59. Latent Bridge: From Video Latents to 3D Geometry via Feature Alignment

**中文标题**: Latent Bridge: From Video Latents to 3D Geometry via Feature Alignment

**作者**: Shuai Zhang, Zhanpeng Ouyang, Zhengqing Chen, Ruohong Mei, Shaoyu Chen et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28521v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28521v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28521v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/hustvl/Latent-Bridge.

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Video generation and feed-forward 3D reconstruction typically use separate representations, requiring RGB decoding and visual re-encoding to recover geometry from generated latents. We propose Latent Bridge, which connects video generative latent spaces with geometric feature spaces to directly predict 3D geometry from video latents. We develop representation-specific bridges for Wan2.1 and V-RAE, enabling feed-forward prediction of cameras, depth, and 3D point clouds without the intermediate RGB pathway. Each bridge is trained on RGB videos through feature distillation, while the video encoder and geometry model remain frozen. Training requires neither ground-truth geometry nor execution of the downstream geometric aggregator or prediction heads. At inference, the same video latents support both RGB decoding and geometry prediction. The same trained bridge can be reused for image-to-video and text-to-video generation within the same video latent space, without bridge retraining. Experiments on Co3Dv2 and WildRGB-D demonstrate competitive geometric reconstruction quality, efficient end-to-end generation and reconstruction, and faster latent-to-geometry decoding, highlighting latent alignment as a practical and reusable interface between video generation and 3D reconstruction. Our code is available at: https://github.com/hustvl/Latent-Bridge.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We propose Latent Bridge, which connects video generative latent spaces with geometric feature spaces to directly predict 3D geometry from video latents. ## 60. Touch-JEPA: Tactile Representation Learning from Hand-Object Video with Limited Tactile Supervision

**中文标题**: Touch-JEPA: Tactile Representation Learning from Hand-Object Video with Limited Tactile Supervision

**作者**: Minseong Kweon, Qiao Feng, Yiming Huang, Lingjie Liu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28519v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28519v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28519v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Understanding contact and tactile magnitude in hand-object interaction is a key step toward physically intelligent systems, yet learning such representations is limited by the cost and restricted coverage of tactile measurements. We present Touch-JEPA, a framework for learning tactile representations from hand-object videos with limited tactile supervision. We first introduce a self-supervised pretraining objective that learns from video alone without tactile supervision. Using video-derived hand geometry, Touch-JEPA predicts masked visual features at corresponding hand-surface locations from spatial and temporal context, encouraging the representation to capture latent contact dynamics. We then extend this formulation to joint representation learning, where masked prediction is applied to all videos while tactile supervision is available only for a labeled subset, grounding the shared representation in measured contact and magnitude. With self-supervised pretraining alone, simple supervised readouts on frozen Touch-JEPA features outperform supervised baselines in contact discrimination and tactile magnitude estimation across three human-hand benchmarks, and transfer to robotic hands without robot tactile training data. Joint learning with limited tactile supervision further improves contact and magnitude estimation on the human-hand benchmarks. Together, these results show that hand-object video can provide scalable self-supervision for tactile representation learning, while limited tactile measurements provide complementary physical grounding.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Understanding contact and tactile magnitude in hand-object interaction is a key step toward physically intelligent systems, yet learning such representations is limited by the cost and restricted coverage of tactile measurements. We present Touch-JEPA, a framework for learning tactile representations from hand-object videos with limited tactile supervision. We first introduce a self-supervised pretraining objective that learns from video alone without tactile supervision. Using video-derived hand geometry, Touch-JEPA predicts masked visual features at corresponding hand-surface locations from spatial and temporal context, encouraging the representation to capture latent contact dynamics. We then extend this formulation to joint representation learning, where masked prediction is applied to all videos while tactile supervision is available only for a labeled subset, grounding the shared representation in measured contact and magnitude. With self-supervised pretraining alone, simple supervised readouts on frozen Touch-JEPA features outperform supervised baselines in contact discrimination and tactile magnitude estimation across three human-hand benchmarks, and transfer to robotic hands without robot tactile training data. Joint learning with limited tactile supervision further improves contact and magnitude estimation on the human-hand benchmarks. Together, these results show that hand-object video can provide scalable self-supervision for tactile representation learning, while limited tactile measurements provide complementary physical grounding.

</details>

## 61. EvaOmni: An Evidence-Grounded Benchmark for Audio-Visual Reasoning

**中文标题**: EvaOmni: An Evidence-Grounded Benchmark for Audio-Visual Reasoning

**作者**: Xiaoxing Hu, Wenxi Yao, Chaoyuan Shi, Ziqi Ye, Xiaorong Guo et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28444v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28444v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28444v2)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Omni LLMs can naturally locate key audio and visual evidence and integrate information across modalities for audiovisual reasoning. Existing audio-visual reasoning benchmarks that mainly rely on multiple-choice questions usually score models solely based on answer correctness, making it difficult to assess whether a model truly forms a reasoning chain from evidence to the correct answer. In addition, textual or unimodal shortcuts may allow models to answer correctly without analyzing the necessary audio-visual evidence, further weakening the reliability of evaluation benchmarks. To address these issues, we introduce EvaOmni, an evidence-grounded benchmark containing 392 videos and 1,027 purely human-annotated question-answer pairs, covering 15 capabilities and three levels: retrieval and localization, basic understanding, and complex reasoning. We design a series of rigorous annotation procedures in which experts annotate audio and visual evidence and construct video-based distractors; humans review whether questions meet strict inspection criteria, and finally models filter textual and unimodal shortcuts. Beyond high-quality question-answer pairs, we require models to simultaneously return the answer and supporting evidence intervals in order to evaluate the complete reasoning chain. We propose the Coverage Grounded Score (C.G.) to measure how much annotated evidence a model identifies, and the Bottleneck Grounded Score (B.G.) to penalize poor temporal localization in either modality; both are incorporated into answer correctness. Across 7 open-weight and 17 proprietary Omni LLMs, Gemini3.7-Flash achieved the highest overall accuracy (79.4%), C.G. (70.8%), and B.G. (35.1%). At Level 3, it reached 79.1% accuracy, but its B.G. was only 12.3%. These results show that even the most advanced models still have room for development in grounded audio-visual reasoning.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Existing audio-visual reasoning benchmarks that mainly rely on multiple-choice questions usually score models solely based on answer correctness, making it difficult to assess whether a model truly forms a reasoning chain from evidence to the correct answer. In addition, textual or unimodal shortcuts may allow models to answer correctly without analyzing the necessary audio-visual evidence, further weakening the reliability of evaluation benchmarks. To address these issues, we introduce EvaOmni, an evidence-grounded benchmark containing 392 videos and 1,027 purely human-annotated question-answer pairs, covering 15 capabilities and three levels: retrieval and localization, basic understanding, and complex reasoning. Beyond high-quality question-answer pairs, we require models to simultaneously return the answer and supporting evidence intervals in order to evaluate the complete reasoning chain. We propose the Coverage Grounded Score (C.G.) to measure how much annotated evidence a model identifies, and the Bottleneck Grounded Score (B.G.) to penalize poor temporal localization in either modality; both are incorporated into answer correctness. Across 7 open-weight and 17 proprietary Omni LLMs, Gemini3.7-Flash achieved the highest overall accuracy (79.4%), C.G. (70.8%), and B.G. (35.1%). At Level 3, it reached 79.1% accuracy, but its B.G. was only 12.3%. These results show that even the most advanced models still have room for development in grounded audio-visual reasoning.

</details>

## 62. Code2Video-Compass: A Comprehensive Benchmark for General-Purpose Code-Based Video Generation across T2V, I2V, and V2V

**中文标题**: Code2Video-Compass: A Comprehensive Benchmark for General-Purpose Code-Based Video Generation across T2V, I2V, and V2V

**作者**: Zhenghan Chen, Zekai Shao, Xin Lin, Leting Cheng, Siming Chen

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28416v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28416v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28416v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Code-based video generation offers fine-grained control, targeted editing, and component reuse compared with direct video generation. This approach requires multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to translate visual and temporal requirements into executable programs that produce complete videos. However, existing code-based benchmarks primarily target specialized tasks, leaving complete video creation across diverse creative categories and input modalities insufficiently evaluated. We introduce Code2Video-Compass, a general-purpose benchmark for code-based video generation across text-to-video (T2V), image-to-video (I2V), and video-to-video (V2V) settings. Code2Video-Compass comprises 2,104 tasks spanning 10 categories, 24 subtypes, and three complexity dimensions covering construction, dynamic animation, and state dependency. It combines tasks curated from textual instructions and visual references with corresponding T2V, I2V, and V2V tasks derived from complete professional and user-created videos. We propose a dual-level evaluation framework combining 12 objective metrics and five subjective metrics. Objective evaluation covers operational performance, video quality, text-video alignment, and reference fidelity, while subjective evaluation uses an MLLM judge to evaluate instruction following and holistic video quality. Extensive evaluation of 15 recent proprietary and open-weight MLLMs from nine families reveals that even leading models face challenges in reliably generating high-quality complete videos aligned with textual and visual requirements, with performance generally declining as construction, dynamic animation, and state-dependency complexity increases. We anticipate that Code2Video-Compass will advance reliable, coherent, and controllable code-based video generation and inspire broader progress in programmatic visual creation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Code-based video generation offers fine-grained control, targeted editing, and component reuse compared with direct video generation. This approach requires multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to translate visual and temporal requirements into executable programs that produce complete videos. However, existing code-based benchmarks primarily target specialized tasks, leaving complete video creation across diverse creative categories and input modalities insufficiently evaluated. We introduce Code2Video-Compass, a general-purpose benchmark for code-based video generation across text-to-video (T2V), image-to-video (I2V), and video-to-video (V2V) settings. Code2Video-Compass comprises 2,104 tasks spanning 10 categories, 24 subtypes, and three complexity dimensions covering construction, dynamic animation, and state dependency. Objective evaluation covers operational performance, video quality, text-video alignment, and reference fidelity, while subjective evaluation uses an MLLM judge to evaluate instruction following and holistic video quality. Extensive evaluation of 15 recent proprietary and open-weight MLLMs from nine families reveals that even leading models face challenges in reliably generating high-quality complete videos aligned with textual and visual requirements, with performance generally declining as construction, dynamic animation, and state-dependency complexity increases. We anticipate that Code2Video-Compass will advance reliable, coherent, and controllable code-based video generation and inspire broader progress in programmatic visual creation.

</details>

## 63. StreamEval: Evaluating Interactive Streaming Video Generation Models

**中文标题**: StreamEval: Evaluating Interactive Streaming Video Generation Models

**作者**:  StreamEval Team

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28402v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28402v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28402v1)

**项目主页**: https://streameval.ai

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

As streaming video generation advances, enabling real-time interaction with generated video is becoming increasingly important. We introduce StreamEval, a unified benchmark for interactive streaming video generation spanning five tasks: avatar dialogue, avatar performance, world generation, instruction editing, and reference editing. StreamEval comprises 300 scripted test cases lasting 15-300s, each specifying initial assets, incrementally arriving multimodal requests, and explicit requirements for state changes and content preservation. Performance is assessed along four evaluation dimensions: task execution, state consistency, visual fidelity, and audiovisual coordination, instantiated through ten task-specific metrics on a 0-100 scale. Evaluating 37 distinct models across 300 test cases, we find that high visual quality and identity retention do not necessarily coincide with reliable request execution or sustained state consistency. In particular, request execution remains challenging: the highest task-specific scores are 36.90 for Following among seven avatar-performance models, 25.93 for Following in text-conditioned world generation, and 15.00 for Edit Success among three reported reference-editing models. These findings identify reliable request execution under timing and preservation constraints as a central challenge for advancing omni-modal real-time interactive systems. The leaderboard is available at https://streameval.ai

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Performance is assessed along four evaluation dimensions: task execution, state consistency, visual fidelity, and audiovisual coordination, instantiated through ten task-specific metrics on a 0-100 scale. Evaluating 37 distinct models across 300 test cases, we find that high visual quality and identity retention do not necessarily coincide with reliable request execution or sustained state consistency. In particular, request execution remains challenging: the highest task-specific scores are 36.90 for Following among seven avatar-performance models, 25.93 for Following in text-conditioned world generation, and 15.00 for Edit Success among three reported reference-editing models. These findings identify reliable request execution under timing and preservation constraints as a central challenge for advancing omni-modal real-time interactive systems. The leaderboard is available at https://streameval.ai

</details>

## 64. RayCo4D: Ray-Conditioned Continuous Motion Model for Monocular 4D Dynamic Reconstruction

**中文标题**: RayCo4D: Ray-Conditioned Continuous Motion Model for Monocular 4D Dynamic Reconstruction

**作者**: Yuanxiang Ni, Xianliang Huang, Ruihan Hou, Feng Xie, Hao Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28351v2](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28351v2)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28351v2)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes from monocular video requires inferring motion from noisy and intermittently visible observations. Long-range tracks provide valuable geometric constraints, but integrating their fragmented temporal coverage into shared, continuous motion remains challenging. We introduce RayCo4D, a ray-conditioned continuous motion model for monocular 4D reconstruction that explicitly separates visual measurements from latent 3D motion states. Each track is associated with a reference-time 3D query constrained to its camera ray, and its image and depth observations constrain shared latent motion nodes through local many-to-many associations. Each node maintains an $\mathrm{SE}(3)$ pose and body-frame velocity at sparse support times. A white-noise-on-acceleration process prior couples neighboring states and defines continuous-time interpolation, allowing observations across visibility intervals to jointly constrain the same latent state sequence. The inferred shared motion is transferred to Gaussian primitives through dual-quaternion blending, while reference-anchored local $\mathrm{SE}(3)$ residuals capture fine-scale non-rigid deformation beyond the shared scaffold. Experiments on diverse monocular dynamic scenes show that RayCo4D achieves competitive novel-view synthesis quality, improves structural and temporal consistency under rapid motion and incomplete tracks, and supports continuous-time rendering.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes from monocular video requires inferring motion from noisy and intermittently visible observations. Long-range tracks provide valuable geometric constraints, but integrating their fragmented temporal coverage into shared, continuous motion remains challenging. We introduce RayCo4D, a ray-conditioned continuous motion model for monocular 4D reconstruction that explicitly separates visual measurements from latent 3D motion states. Each track is associated with a reference-time 3D query constrained to its camera ray, and its image and depth observations constrain shared latent motion nodes through local many-to-many associations. Each node maintains an $\mathrm{SE}(3)$ pose and body-frame velocity at sparse support times. A white-noise-on-acceleration process prior couples neighboring states and defines continuous-time interpolation, allowing observations across visibility intervals to jointly constrain the same latent state sequence. The inferred shared motion is transferred to Gaussian primitives through dual-quaternion blending, while reference-anchored local $\mathrm{SE}(3)$ residuals capture fine-scale non-rigid deformation beyond the shared scaffold. Experiments on diverse monocular dynamic scenes show that RayCo4D achieves competitive novel-view synthesis quality, improves structural and temporal consistency under rapid motion and incomplete tracks, and supports continuous-time rendering.

</details>

## 65. Auto-Director: Leveraging Multi-Agent Collaboration for Autonomous Video Editing

**中文标题**: Auto-Director: Leveraging Multi-Agent Collaboration for Autonomous Video Editing

**作者**: Yuxia Chen, Wenbo Zhu, Yangguang Ji, Zhenxiang Jiang, Teng Zhong et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28346v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28346v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28346v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Autonomous video editing from open-world resources remains a challenging problem in multimedia content creation. Retrieval-based methods are constrained by fixed source material and lack creative flexibility, while generative models struggle with long-horizon coherence and often diverge from user intent. Both paradigms fail to replicate the structured, multi-stage reasoning that underlies professional post-production. To address these limitations, we present Auto-Director, a tool-augmented multi-agent framework that mirrors professional editorial workflows: a Planning Agent decomposes abstract topics into fact-grounded scripts via retrieval-augmented planning; a Material Agent indexes open-world footage through shot segmentation and visual tagging; an Editor Agent assembles semantically aligned clips with MLLM-driven spatial adaptation; and a Critic Agent governs an iterative Reflect-and-Revise loop that checks scene-level alignment and pacing. To rigorously evaluate this new paradigm, we introduce VEdit-Bench, a benchmark of 100 expert-curated topics with human-annotated Golden Scripts and a five-dimensional metric suite covering Narrative Logic, Factual Accuracy, Semantic Alignment, Compositional Quality, and Stylistic Cohesion. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Auto-Director significantly outperforms baseline methods in both automated metrics and human evaluation, effectively bridging the gap between amateurs and professional-grade video production.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Autonomous video editing from open-world resources remains a challenging problem in multimedia content creation. Retrieval-based methods are constrained by fixed source material and lack creative flexibility, while generative models struggle with long-horizon coherence and often diverge from user intent. Both paradigms fail to replicate the structured, multi-stage reasoning that underlies professional post-production. To address these limitations, we present Auto-Director, a tool-augmented multi-agent framework that mirrors professional editorial workflows: a Planning Agent decomposes abstract topics into fact-grounded scripts via retrieval-augmented planning; a Material Agent indexes open-world footage through shot segmentation and visual tagging; an Editor Agent assembles semantically aligned clips with MLLM-driven spatial adaptation; and a Critic Agent governs an iterative Reflect-and-Revise loop that checks scene-level alignment and pacing. To rigorously evaluate this new paradigm, we introduce VEdit-Bench, a benchmark of 100 expert-curated topics with human-annotated Golden Scripts and a five-dimensional metric suite covering Narrative Logic, Factual Accuracy, Semantic Alignment, Compositional Quality, and Stylistic Cohesion. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Auto-Director significantly outperforms baseline methods in both automated metrics and human evaluation, effectively bridging the gap between amateurs and professional-grade video production.

</details>

## 66. PACE: Plan, Attend, Check, and Emit for Training-Free Response Timing in Streaming Video Understanding

**中文标题**: PACE: Plan, Attend, Check, and Emit for Training-Free Response Timing in Streaming Video Understanding

**作者**: Siqi Guo, Yuxia Chen, Wenbo Zhu, Yangguang Ji, Zhenxiang Jiang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28323v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28323v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28323v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Streaming video understanding requires a model to decide not only what to answer but also when to answer. Evidence arrives progressively, and recent benchmarks identify premature responses, issued before the queried event has concluded, as a recurring failure mode. Existing approaches often learn response timing through additional training, while training-free alternatives typically rely on scalar gating signals that do not explicitly represent when an answer becomes final. We organize chunk-wise streaming errors into three patterns: sampling variation can make an answer unstable, a stable answer may lack support in the observed video, or an answer may be supported by the current evidence while the event remains unfinished. We propose PACE (Plan, Attend, Check, and Emit), a training-free framework designed around these three failure modes. Given a question, PACE drafts a plan specifying what to observe and what will signal completion. As the stream unfolds, it invokes an off-the-shelf video LLM at informative moments, evaluates each draft for stability, grounding in its cited frames, and completion, and records verified observations in an event ledger. Experiments on ProactiveVideoQA and OVO-Bench Forward show that PACE improves over the official protocols by up to 5.9 average PAUC points and 6.2 average accuracy points across proprietary and open-source backbones, yielding state-of-the-art performance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Streaming video understanding requires a model to decide not only what to answer but also when to answer. Evidence arrives progressively, and recent benchmarks identify premature responses, issued before the queried event has concluded, as a recurring failure mode. Existing approaches often learn response timing through additional training, while training-free alternatives typically rely on scalar gating signals that do not explicitly represent when an answer becomes final. We organize chunk-wise streaming errors into three patterns: sampling variation can make an answer unstable, a stable answer may lack support in the observed video, or an answer may be supported by the current evidence while the event remains unfinished. We propose PACE (Plan, Attend, Check, and Emit), a training-free framework designed around these three failure modes. Given a question, PACE drafts a plan specifying what to observe and what will signal completion. As the stream unfolds, it invokes an off-the-shelf video LLM at informative moments, evaluates each draft for stability, grounding in its cited frames, and completion, and records verified observations in an event ledger. Experiments on ProactiveVideoQA and OVO-Bench Forward show that PACE improves over the official protocols by up to 5.9 average PAUC points and 6.2 average accuracy points across proprietary and open-source backbones, yielding state-of-the-art performance.

</details>

## 67. SplatFlux: Feed-Forward 4D Gaussian Reconstruction and Generation from Video Latents

**中文标题**: SplatFlux: Feed-Forward 4D Gaussian Reconstruction and Generation from Video Latents

**作者**: Runmin Zhang, Binglin Ren, Zhu Yu, Fudong Wang, Jiangke Lin et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28257v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28257v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28257v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

This work presents SplatFlux, a feed-forward latent-to-4DGS framework for both video-based 4D reconstruction and conditional 4D generation. Our key observation is that observed videos and conditionally generated content can be represented in the same pretrained video latent space, allowing both tasks to be formulated as latent-to-4DGS decoding. To construct compact and temporally coherent 4D scenes, SplatFlux introduces a static-dynamic decoupled 4D Gaussian representation that explicitly predicts dynamic probabilities for Gaussian primitives. This representation separates static scene structures from moving regions, enabling complete 4D scene construction in a feed-forward manner through static aggregation and dynamic motion interpolation. To learn static-dynamic decomposition and dynamic Gaussian motion without costly annotations, we further develop an auxiliary distillation strategy that provides pseudo dynamic masks and optical-flow cues. Extensive experiments across 3D and 4D reconstruction and generation demonstrate that SplatFlux achieves state-of-the-art performance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

This work presents SplatFlux, a feed-forward latent-to-4DGS framework for both video-based 4D reconstruction and conditional 4D generation. Our key observation is that observed videos and conditionally generated content can be represented in the same pretrained video latent space, allowing both tasks to be formulated as latent-to-4DGS decoding. To construct compact and temporally coherent 4D scenes, SplatFlux introduces a static-dynamic decoupled 4D Gaussian representation that explicitly predicts dynamic probabilities for Gaussian primitives. This representation separates static scene structures from moving regions, enabling complete 4D scene construction in a feed-forward manner through static aggregation and dynamic motion interpolation. To learn static-dynamic decomposition and dynamic Gaussian motion without costly annotations, we further develop an auxiliary distillation strategy that provides pseudo dynamic masks and optical-flow cues. Extensive experiments across 3D and 4D reconstruction and generation demonstrate that SplatFlux achieves state-of-the-art performance.

</details>

## 68. Learn to Modify: Self-evolving Multi-agent Refinement Framework for Conversational Text-to-Motion Generation

**中文标题**: Learn to Modify: Self-evolving Multi-agent Refinement Framework for Conversational Text-to-Motion Generation

**作者**: Jianan Li, Jiawei Zhao, Yangtao Zhou, Zhifu Zhao, Shaoxin Wu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28210v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28210v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28210v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Despite rapid progress in text-to-motion generation, existing methods are commonly designed for single-shot generation. When users are dissatisfied with specific motion details, they are often forced to regenerate the entire sequence from scratch, which is computationally inefficient and potentially destroys previously established kinematic context. A practical generation system should instead support iterative user feedback and refine only the affected spatio-temporal regions while preserving the remaining motion. Therefore, we introduce a conversational motion generation paradigm that requires the system to coordinate instruction understanding, motion generation, localized editing, and quality assessment. Recent advances in LLM-driven multi-agent systems provide a promising foundation for such role-specialized coordination. To tackle these challenges, a Self-evolving Multi-Agent RefinemenT Framework (SMART) is proposed for conversational motion generation. Within SMART, to preserve semantic consistency, we design a semantic-guided masked motion editing mechanism, which precisely grounds natural language feedback into spatiotemporal motion regions to enable localized refinements without altering unedited regions. To reduce redundant exploration, we propose a memory-based self-evolving strategy that dynamically accumulates motion editing knowledge from interaction history and reuses past successful experience. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SMART effectively supports multi-turn conversational motion generation and enables efficient motion refinement.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Despite rapid progress in text-to-motion generation, existing methods are commonly designed for single-shot generation. When users are dissatisfied with specific motion details, they are often forced to regenerate the entire sequence from scratch, which is computationally inefficient and potentially destroys previously established kinematic context. A practical generation system should instead support iterative user feedback and refine only the affected spatio-temporal regions while preserving the remaining motion. Therefore, we introduce a conversational motion generation paradigm that requires the system to coordinate instruction understanding, motion generation, localized editing, and quality assessment. Recent advances in LLM-driven multi-agent systems provide a promising foundation for such role-specialized coordination. To tackle these challenges, a Self-evolving Multi-Agent RefinemenT Framework (SMART) is proposed for conversational motion generation. Within SMART, to preserve semantic consistency, we design a semantic-guided masked motion editing mechanism, which precisely grounds natural language feedback into spatiotemporal motion regions to enable localized refinements without altering unedited regions. To reduce redundant exploration, we propose a memory-based self-evolving strategy that dynamically accumulates motion editing knowledge from interaction history and reuses past successful experience. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SMART effectively supports multi-turn conversational motion generation and enables efficient motion refinement.

</details>

---

## 69. Offline Makes Online Better: Calibrated Cache Compression for Video Generation

**中文标题**: Offline Makes Online Better: Calibrated Cache Compression for Video Generation

**作者**: Zhen Wang, Ning Lai, Qingtao Yu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28192v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28192v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28192v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Token merging is an effective approach to reducing KV-cache memory consumption in autoregressive video generation by clustering temporally redundant tokens and retaining only a small set of representatives. Existing methods, however, typically apply a uniform merging ratio across all attention layers and heads. We observe that different layers and heads exhibit substantially different yet jointly correlated sensitivities to token merging, and that these sensitivity patterns remain consistent across prompts. Based on this observation, we introduce CALM, a Calibrated Allocation for Layer-Head Merging that uses offline calibration to improve online cache compression. Exhaustively evaluating all possible layer-head allocations is prohibitively expensive. To enable efficient calibration, we show that attention-output error strongly correlates with end-to-end video quality and can therefore serve as a lightweight proxy for allocation risk assessment. We evaluate different allocation strategies using this proxy and construct a fixed layer-head risk map offline. Given a cache budget at inference time, CALM uses dynamic programming to determine the best allocation of merging ratios. Experiments across three different autoregressive video models demonstrate that CALM consistently outperforms uniform token merging under the same memory budget, by reducing the LPIPS to original videos by around 21% with higher video-quality scores.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Token merging is an effective approach to reducing KV-cache memory consumption in autoregressive video generation by clustering temporally redundant tokens and retaining only a small set of representatives. Existing methods, however, typically apply a uniform merging ratio across all attention layers and heads. We observe that different layers and heads exhibit substantially different yet jointly correlated sensitivities to token merging, and that these sensitivity patterns remain consistent across prompts. Based on this observation, we introduce CALM, a Calibrated Allocation for Layer-Head Merging that uses offline calibration to improve online cache compression. Exhaustively evaluating all possible layer-head allocations is prohibitively expensive. To enable efficient calibration, we show that attention-output error strongly correlates with end-to-end video quality and can therefore serve as a lightweight proxy for allocation risk assessment. We evaluate different allocation strategies using this proxy and construct a fixed layer-head risk map offline. Given a cache budget at inference time, CALM uses dynamic programming to determine the best allocation of merging ratios. Experiments across three different autoregressive video models demonstrate that CALM consistently outperforms uniform token merging under the same memory budget, by reducing the LPIPS to original videos by around 21% with higher video-quality scores.

</details>

## 70. PointForward V2: Point-Aligned Interaction for Feedforward Driving Reconstruction

**中文标题**: PointForward V2: Point-Aligned Interaction for Feedforward Driving Reconstruction

**作者**: Xianqi Wang, Cheng Chi, Yuechuan Pu, Hongcheng Luo, Junda Cheng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28120v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28120v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28120v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Feedforward reconstruction of dynamic driving scenes requires integrating observations across multiple views and time steps into a coherent 3D representation. PointForward established state-of-the-art feedforward driving reconstruction by lifting image observations into sparse 3D queries, demonstrating the effectiveness of point-aligned scene representations. However, these queries are largely processed independently, limiting information exchange across space and time, while direct global attention among tens of thousands of point queries is computationally prohibitive. In this work, we present \textbf{PointForward V2}, which advances point-aligned representation to point-aligned interaction. Our key idea is to mediate query interaction through compact, scene-structured registers. For the static background, frame-wise Static Scene Registers compress point information, communicate across frames, and propagate the aggregated context back to the queries. For each dynamic instance, queries from different frames are aligned in an object-centric canonical space and interact through instance-specific Dynamic Instance Registers. We further employ 4D positional encoding that combines aligned spatial coordinates with original timestamps, enabling spatially aligned yet temporally distinguishable interactions. In this way, PointForward V2 enables scalable spatio-temporal reasoning directly among lifted 3D queries without dense global attention. Experiments on Waymo, nuScenes, and Argoverse 2 demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across large-scale driving benchmarks.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

</details>

## 71. MoDDiT: Simple Token Routing in Diffusion Transformers

**中文标题**: MoDDiT: Simple Token Routing in Diffusion Transformers

**作者**: Jarek Liesen, Matthew T. Jackson, Benjamin Ellis, Shimon Whiteson, Jakob N. Foerster

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28086v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28086v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28086v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Real-time video generation, interactive world models, and on-device image synthesis are almost a reality. Diffusion Transformers (DiT) achieve excellent sample quality, shifting the bottleneck to the throughput of the models themselves. Natural images are inherently sparse, as large portions of an image are often background, contribute little to the scene content, and are easy to denoise. DiTs fail to exploit this sparsity, as they process image tokens statically and uniformly. We propose MoDDiT, a simple architecture modification that learns to route tokens through both MLP and self-attention operations. By routing tokens through both blocks independently, MoDDiT allows for dynamic compute allocation in MLPs that varies across tokens, images, and denoising timesteps. Using MoDDiT to train image generation models from scratch significantly reduces computational costs of training and inference, processing as few as 12.5% of the tokens. By increasing sparsity and model depth, MoDDiT reduces costs and improves generation performance over unrouted baselines. The learned allocation is interpretable, revealing how computation varies with visual content and noise level. We also use MoDDiT to train video generation models, where allocation correlates with motion, and to distill a dense bidirectional video model into a sparse autoregressive student.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Real-time video generation, interactive world models, and on-device image synthesis are almost a reality. DiTs fail to exploit this sparsity, as they process image tokens statically and uniformly. We propose MoDDiT, a simple architecture modification that learns to route tokens through both MLP and self-attention operations. By routing tokens through both blocks independently, MoDDiT allows for dynamic compute allocation in MLPs that varies across tokens, images, and denoising timesteps. Using MoDDiT to train image generation models from scratch significantly reduces computational costs of training and inference, processing as few as 12.5% of the tokens. By increasing sparsity and model depth, MoDDiT reduces costs and improves generation performance over unrouted baselines. The learned allocation is interpretable, revealing how computation varies with visual content and noise level. We also use MoDDiT to train video generation models, where allocation correlates with motion, and to distill a dense bidirectional video model into a sparse autoregressive student.

</details>

## 72. Slot-JEPA: Object-Structured Prediction Interfaces for Latent World Models

**中文标题**: Slot-JEPA: Object-Structured Prediction Interfaces for Latent World Models

**作者**: Peisong Wen, Qianqian Xu, Minye Lei, Siran Dai, Yang Liu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28074v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28074v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28074v1)

**类别**: cs.AI, cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

As a prototypical latent world model, Joint-Embedding Predictive Architectures (JEPAs) provide strong semantic representations. However, dense patch representations expose a redundant interface to downstream predictors, which must implicitly infer spatial organization and temporal correspondence, presenting three challenges. First, object information is distributed across patches, forcing each downstream predictor to redundantly recover object structure that could otherwise be reused across tasks before modeling interactions. Second, the lack of explicit entity correspondence across time makes historical transitions expensive, restricting the predictor's flexibility in utilizing context. Third, without explicit transport structure, existing JEPA models typically use pointwise l1/l2 distances, which are insensitive to spatial displacement when predicted and goal supports are largely disjoint. We propose the Slot-based Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture (Slot-JEPA), which decomposes dense representations into stable semantic slots and time-varying spatial masks. Pretrained once and frozen downstream, this decomposition exposes a reusable object-structured interface. Building on it, our Transition Memory Predictor (TMP) stores visual transitions as compact masks and retrieves useful context using statistics of corresponding slots. Its two branches separate slot-specific dynamics from shared cross-slot dynamics. We further use Unbalanced Optimal Transport (UOT) to supervise mask prediction and evaluate candidate plans by transporting mass between aligned predicted and goal masks, providing a signal better aligned with spatial progress. Across seven visual-understanding benchmarks, the slot-reconstructed features remain transferable. Slot-JEPA also improves goal-conditioned planning across six planar environments and four robotic arm planning tasks on the REALM-JEPA benchmark.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Its two branches separate slot-specific dynamics from shared cross-slot dynamics. Across seven visual-understanding benchmarks, the slot-reconstructed features remain transferable. Slot-JEPA also improves goal-conditioned planning across six planar environments and four robotic arm planning tasks on the REALM-JEPA benchmark.

</details>

## 73. Matrix-Game 4.0: Harnessing Video Models for Real-Time Playable Game Worlds

**中文标题**: Matrix-Game 4.0: Harnessing Video Models for Real-Time Playable Game Worlds

**作者**: Jihai Zhang, Runjia Qian, Kaiqin Kong, Biao Jiang, Puyi Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27999v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27999v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27999v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.GR

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Moving from controllable videos to genuinely playable worlds requires persistent world state, executable game rules, and low-latency interaction. We outline a roadmap for the next generation of Matrix-Game that separates game execution from visual generation through an executable game harness, and introduce Matrix-Game 4.0 as the first system built on this design. A lightweight game engine maintains world state and executes interaction rules, a multimodal coding agent translates player ideas into game code and dynamic scene captions, and a renderer based on autoregressive video model transforms engine-rendered low-poly proxies into detailed visuals, conditioned on dynamic captions and memory references. This framework provides continuous guidance for visual generation and enables natural-language creation and modification of scenes and gameplay. Our renderer is trained on aligned low-poly and high-fidelity video pairs and distilled into a causal generator that generates in four-frame chunks with one or two denoising steps. Together with targeted inference optimizations, Matrix-Game 4.0 enables 480p, 60 FPS gameplay on a single NVIDIA RTX5090 with a key-press-to-display latency of 112ms. Single-step generation achieves 83FPS steady-state throughput with 18.2GiB of peak GPU memory usage. With fixed prompts, steady-state throughput also reaches 67FPS on an RTX5080 and 58FPS on an RTX5070Ti. We demonstrate customizable shooter and driving games with engine-enforced mechanics, and release code, model weights, and low-poly game assets to support research and creation of playable game worlds.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We demonstrate customizable shooter and driving games with engine-enforced mechanics, and release code, model weights, and low-poly game assets to support research and creation of playable game worlds.

</details>

## 74. Recast the Past: Stable Long Video Generation with Full Context Memory

**中文标题**: Recast the Past: Stable Long Video Generation with Full Context Memory

**作者**: Yukuo Ma, Cong Liu, Junke Wang, Junqi Liu, Haibin Huang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27986v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27986v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27986v1)

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Long-horizon video generation requires retaining an ever-expanding history, but conditioning on its growing token sequence is costly. Existing methods restrict or compress historical context, potentially losing information needed for future generation. This work proposes ReCam, which groups local spatiotemporal blocks of historical latents into vectors and projects them directly into the high-dimensional hidden space of a Diffusion Transformer. The model learns to use the resulting memory tokens through native self-attention, reducing token count while retaining block-level access to the full history. To mitigate temporal drift during autoregressive rollout, we further introduce dual-pointer denoising with separate noise schedules for recent context and target frames. Among the evaluated methods, ReCam achieves the highest VBench-Long overall score of 80.1 and competitive static-scene revisitation performance on R2M-Bench. It also leads on all four consistency metrics for off-screen subject reappearance, including clothing consistency of 4.09 versus 3.03 for the best baseline.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Long-horizon video generation requires retaining an ever-expanding history, but conditioning on its growing token sequence is costly. Existing methods restrict or compress historical context, potentially losing information needed for future generation. This work proposes ReCam, which groups local spatiotemporal blocks of historical latents into vectors and projects them directly into the high-dimensional hidden space of a Diffusion Transformer. The model learns to use the resulting memory tokens through native self-attention, reducing token count while retaining block-level access to the full history. To mitigate temporal drift during autoregressive rollout, we further introduce dual-pointer denoising with separate noise schedules for recent context and target frames. Among the evaluated methods, ReCam achieves the highest VBench-Long overall score of 80.1 and competitive static-scene revisitation performance on R2M-Bench. It also leads on all four consistency metrics for off-screen subject reappearance, including clothing consistency of 4.09 versus 3.03 for the best baseline.

</details>
