## 97. Exploring Temporal Context-Aware Physical Grounding in Latent World Models

**中文标题**: Exploring Temporal Context-Aware Physical Grounding in Latent World Models

**作者**: Yilei Man, Yang Chen, Jingcai Guo

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28483v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28483v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28483v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/myl11045/G-JEPA.

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Learning latent dynamics is fundamental to building action-conditioned world models for planning. Among existing families, Joint Embedding Predictive Architectures (JEPAs) offer a natural framework by modeling transitions directly in latent space rather than at the pixel level. However, two issues remain in JEPA-based world models: (i) physical grounding lacks explicit aggregation of temporal context and (ii) action planning always overly relies on generic sampling distributions. To this end, we propose $\mathcal{G}$-JEPA, which incorporates temporal context into physical grounding for latent world models and couples the learned models with prior-guided action planning. Specifically, during learning, we incorporate a shared causal temporal module that aggregates temporal context within observation and prediction sequences under physical state supervision, grounding both in task-relevant physical states. When planning, we further introduce action inference through stepwise conditioning of a generative action distribution on the current and goal latent representations together with their evolving discrepancy, yielding anchored proposals that guide model-based online refinement. Together, physical state supervision guides temporal representation learning, while the action prior guides search through the learned world model. Experiments demonstrate improved planning performance on manipulation tasks, including tasks with increasing temporal separation between initial and goal observations. Code is publicly available at https://github.com/myl11045/G-JEPA.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

However, two issues remain in JEPA-based world models: (i) physical grounding lacks explicit aggregation of temporal context and (ii) action planning always overly relies on generic sampling distributions. To this end, we propose $\mathcal{G}$-JEPA, which incorporates temporal context into physical grounding for latent world models and couples the learned models with prior-guided action planning. Code is publicly available at https://github.com/myl11045/G-JEPA.

</details>

## 98. DIVE: Benchmarking Full-Duplex Interaction with Visual Engagement in Continuous Streams

**中文标题**: DIVE: Benchmarking Full-Duplex Interaction with Visual Engagement in Continuous Streams

**作者**: Yuhang Chen, Guangyue Liu, Shilong Liu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28405v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28405v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28405v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Recent advances in multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have substantially improved spoken interaction and video understanding. Existing online and streaming benchmarks primarily assess causal multimodal perception through timestamped question answering, whereas real-world interaction requires models to continuously identify task-relevant multimodal cues and decide whether, when, and how to respond. This leaves open how well current models can sustain real-time interaction throughout an evolving multimodal stream. To address this gap, we introduce DIVE, a benchmark for evaluating full-duplex interaction with visual engagement under continuous and realistic multimodal streams. It organizes six tasks into three complementary interaction scenarios. (1) Proactive Intervention evaluates whether models can identify emerging visual or semantic cues and intervene at the appropriate moment. (2) Continuous Engagement evaluates sustained participation through continuous visual tracking and visually grounded query interaction. (3) Interaction Coordination evaluates how models coordinate listening and speaking under overlapping and multi-party interactions. DIVE contains 525 audio/video samples with fine-grained human annotations and precise temporal metadata. Its evaluation protocol separates response quality, participation decisions, and response timing. We evaluate representative real-time models using response quality, window response rates, and reaction latency. Experiments show that leading real-time models achieve sub-second average response latency, yet their average window response rates remain at or below 53.77%. Response quality varies substantially across models and tasks, with adapting ongoing responses to newly arriving information remaining particularly challenging. We hope DIVE facilitates progress toward more natural and capable real-time multimodal interactive systems.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We evaluate representative real-time models using response quality, window response rates, and reaction latency. Experiments show that leading real-time models achieve sub-second average response latency, yet their average window response rates remain at or below 53.77%. Response quality varies substantially across models and tasks, with adapting ongoing responses to newly arriving information remaining particularly challenging. We hope DIVE facilitates progress toward more natural and capable real-time multimodal interactive systems.

</details>

## 99. Temporal Evidence-Parameter Co-Alignment for LLM Reasoning over Temporal Knowledge Graphs

**中文标题**: Temporal Evidence-Parameter Co-Alignment for LLM Reasoning over Temporal Knowledge Graphs

**作者**: Jiapu Wang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28176v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28176v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28176v1)

**类别**: cs.CL, cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Large Language Models (LLMs) often struggle with temporally grounded reasoning because their static parametric memory is misaligned with the dynamic evolution of real-world knowledge. Although RAG and Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) provide practical ways to enhance LLMs, they remain insufficient for temporal reasoning. Existing retrieval methods typically prioritize semantic relevance while overlooking temporal validity, event order, and evolutionary dependencies, resulting in evidence that is relevant in isolation but temporally fragmented. Meanwhile, conventional PEFT methods usually employ fixed and input-agnostic low-rank updates, making it difficult to capture time-dependent entity semantics and relation drift. To address these limitations, we propose Temporal Evidence-Parameter Co-Alignment (TEPCA), a framework for LLM reasoning over TKGs. TEPCA aligns LLM reasoning with temporal knowledge at both the evidence and parameter levels. At the evidence level, it introduces time-constrained Dynamic Relevance-Guided Path (DRGP) pruning to preserve globally coherent temporal paths, together with Mutual-Information (MI)-based pruning to retain query-informative local facts under temporal constraints. At the parameter level, it integrates Dynamic-Gated LoRA (DG-LoRA), a timestamp-conditioned low-rank adaptation mechanism that dynamically modulates the effective adaptation subspace according to temporal context. This design enables LLMs to better capture fine-grained entity evolution and time-dependent relation semantics while preserving parameter efficiency. Experimental results on two TKG benchmarks demonstrate that TEPCA achieves state-of-the-art performance. Additional evaluations of DG-LoRA on eight commonsense QA datasets further verify its general input-adaptive capacity, scalability across LLM backbones, and interpretability.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Large Language Models (LLMs) often struggle with temporally grounded reasoning because their static parametric memory is misaligned with the dynamic evolution of real-world knowledge. Although RAG and Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) provide practical ways to enhance LLMs, they remain insufficient for temporal reasoning. To address these limitations, we propose Temporal Evidence-Parameter Co-Alignment (TEPCA), a framework for LLM reasoning over TKGs. TEPCA aligns LLM reasoning with temporal knowledge at both the evidence and parameter levels. At the evidence level, it introduces time-constrained Dynamic Relevance-Guided Path (DRGP) pruning to preserve globally coherent temporal paths, together with Mutual-Information (MI)-based pruning to retain query-informative local facts under temporal constraints. At the parameter level, it integrates Dynamic-Gated LoRA (DG-LoRA), a timestamp-conditioned low-rank adaptation mechanism that dynamically modulates the effective adaptation subspace according to temporal context. This design enables LLMs to better capture fine-grained entity evolution and time-dependent relation semantics while preserving parameter efficiency. Experimental results on two TKG benchmarks demonstrate that TEPCA achieves state-of-the-art performance. Additional evaluations of DG-LoRA on eight commonsense QA datasets further verify its general input-adaptive capacity, scalability across LLM backbones, and interpretability.

</details>

## 100. Robust Representation Learning for Spiking Neural Networks based on Conditional Information Bottleneck

**中文标题**: Robust Representation Learning for Spiking Neural Networks based on Conditional Information Bottleneck

**作者**: Yuli Zha, Wang Fan, Yixiao Dai, Lingjie Wang, Bang Hu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28110v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28110v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28110v1)

**类别**: cs.AI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) have shown great potential for low-power neuromorphic computing due to their event-driven processing and spatiotemporal sparsity, but they remain vulnerable to input perturbations, hardware-intrinsic noise, and adversarial attacks in real-world deployment. We attribute this vulnerability partly to input-specific hidden representations learned by standard SNNs trained with conventional cross-entropy loss, as our information-plane analysis empirically shows that substantial input-related information can still be retained after label-predictive information becomes stable. To address this issue, we propose SCIB, a simple yet effective robust learning objective for SNNs based on the Conditional Information Bottleneck. Different from conventional variational IB methods, SCIB replaces the mutual information compression term $I(X;S)$ with the conditional mutual information $I(X;S|Y)$ and derives a tractable variational upper bound. Moreover, it introduces a learnable class-conditional prior instead of a label-agnostic isotropic Gaussian prior, improving the expressiveness of variational compression and encouraging class-structured representations when combined with the supervised classification objective. Systematic experiments on Fashion-MNIST, CIFAR-10, and CIFAR-100 under external environmental noise, neuromorphic hardware noise, and adversarial attacks show that SCIB improves robustness over standard SNNs and IB-based baselines under most evaluated noise conditions, improves adversarial accuracy under the evaluated attacks without using adversarial training, and reduces the SynOps-based single-sample inference energy consumption by 24.9% relative to an unconstrained SNN baseline.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) have shown great potential for low-power neuromorphic computing due to their event-driven processing and spatiotemporal sparsity, but they remain vulnerable to input perturbations, hardware-intrinsic noise, and adversarial attacks in real-world deployment. We attribute this vulnerability partly to input-specific hidden representations learned by standard SNNs trained with conventional cross-entropy loss, as our information-plane analysis empirically shows that substantial input-related information can still be retained after label-predictive information becomes stable. To address this issue, we propose SCIB, a simple yet effective robust learning objective for SNNs based on the Conditional Information Bottleneck. Different from conventional variational IB methods, SCIB replaces the mutual information compression term $I(X;S)$ with the conditional mutual information $I(X;S|Y)$ and derives a tractable variational upper bound. Moreover, it introduces a learnable class-conditional prior instead of a label-agnostic isotropic Gaussian prior, improving the expressiveness of variational compression and encouraging class-structured representations when combined with the supervised classification objective. Systematic experiments on Fashion-MNIST, CIFAR-10, and CIFAR-100 under external environmental noise, neuromorphic hardware noise, and adversarial attacks show that SCIB improves robustness over standard SNNs and IB-based baselines under most evaluated noise conditions, improves adversarial accuracy under the evaluated attacks without using adversarial training, and reduces the SynOps-based single-sample inference energy consumption by 24.9% relative to an unconstrained SNN baseline.

</details>

## 101. Physical Canvas: A Vision Tactile World Model For Robot Policy Evaluation And Learning

**中文标题**: Physical Canvas: A Vision Tactile World Model For Robot Policy Evaluation And Learning

**作者**: Zebin Xing, Yuhang Zheng, Yupeng Zheng, Yichen Zhang, Zichen Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28613v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28613v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28613v1)

**项目主页**: https://zebinx.github.io/PhysCanvas_HomePage/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Evaluating and post-training robot policies through physical interaction is costly and difficult to scale, motivating action-conditioned world models as interactive simulators. However, visually plausible predictions can misrepresent contact dynamics, providing unreliable feedback for policy evaluation and learning. We introduce PhysCanvas, a state-consistent visuo-tactile world model for contact-rich robot manipulation. PhysCanvas unifies multi-view RGB, tactile images, marker displacement, task state, and actions within a unified vision space. All modalities share a video VAE and pretrained generative backbone, allowing optical touch to reuse visual and temporal priors. Motion images derived through forward kinematics and camera projection provide spatially aligned action conditioning, while an initial RGB anchor and updated reference observations guide sequential generation. Our consistency-aware post-training uses the frozen Scene Reward Model, Modality Reward Model, and State Reward Model to supervise scene preservation, visual-tactile compatibility, and agreement with real task outcomes. The resulting world model supports closed-loop policy rollouts for evaluation and provides imagined interactions and rewards for GRPO policy updates. Across four real-world manipulation tasks, when policies are rolled out in the world model, State Reward Model supervision increases the arithmetic mean of task-wise Pearson correlations between world-model and real-world success rates from 0.602 to 0.885. Policy post-training in the reward-guided world model further increases $π_{0.5}$'s mean real-world success rate from a 59.1% SFT baseline to 70.8%. These results demonstrate the value of unified multimodal modeling and consistency-aware post-training for robot policy evaluation and learning. Project page: https://zebinx.github.io/PhysCanvas_HomePage/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

However, visually plausible predictions can misrepresent contact dynamics, providing unreliable feedback for policy evaluation and learning. The resulting world model supports closed-loop policy rollouts for evaluation and provides imagined interactions and rewards for GRPO policy updates. Across four real-world manipulation tasks, when policies are rolled out in the world model, State Reward Model supervision increases the arithmetic mean of task-wise Pearson correlations between world-model and real-world success rates from 0.602 to 0.885. Policy post-training in the reward-guided world model further increases $π_{0.5}$'s mean real-world success rate from a 59.1% SFT baseline to 70.8%. These results demonstrate the value of unified multimodal modeling and consistency-aware post-training for robot policy evaluation and learning. Project page: https://zebinx.github.io/PhysCanvas_HomePage/.

</details>

## 102. From Predictive Geometry to Action: Learning Complementary Predictive Representations for Embodied Manipulation

**中文标题**: From Predictive Geometry to Action: Learning Complementary Predictive Representations for Embodied Manipulation

**作者**: Xiaoxu Xu, Hao Li, Hongtao Zhang, Yili Tang, Jiafei Cao et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28164v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28164v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28164v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/xuxiaoxxxx/CPR.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Effective manipulation requires a policy to capture not only about the current scene geometry, but also about how that geometry will evolve under interaction. Existing approaches either augment policies with geometric information from current observations or introduce future prediction as auxiliary supervision, leaving open how predictive geometry should be represented for action generation. Therefore, we propose a two-stage framework, which learns complementary predictive representations from multi-view video clips. During pretraining, a frozen 3D foundation model provides dense geometric features that are compressed into predictive geometric representations and optimized to preserve scene structure and its temporal evolution. Action supervision is then applied to geometric transitions to extract a complementary motor representation that emphasizes action-relevant transitions. During downstream post-training, predictive geometry is transferred through geometry queries, while the motor representation guides intermediate features along the action-generation pathway. Future observations are used only as privileged supervision during training and are not required at inference time. Across LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, SimplerEnv, and real-world bimanual and single-arm tasks, our approach consistently improves downstream policies, including an absolute gain of 10.4 percentage points on the WidowX evaluation. We further demonstrate transfer across multiple VLA architectures and WAMs, with improvements in both future-world and action prediction. The code will be available at https://github.com/xuxiaoxxxx/CPR.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Therefore, we propose a two-stage framework, which learns complementary predictive representations from multi-view video clips. During pretraining, a frozen 3D foundation model provides dense geometric features that are compressed into predictive geometric representations and optimized to preserve scene structure and its temporal evolution. Action supervision is then applied to geometric transitions to extract a complementary motor representation that emphasizes action-relevant transitions. During downstream post-training, predictive geometry is transferred through geometry queries, while the motor representation guides intermediate features along the action-generation pathway. Future observations are used only as privileged supervision during training and are not required at inference time. Across LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, SimplerEnv, and real-world bimanual and single-arm tasks, our approach consistently improves downstream policies, including an absolute gain of 10.4 percentage points on the WidowX evaluation. We further demonstrate transfer across multiple VLA architectures and WAMs, with improvements in both future-world and action prediction. The code will be available at https://github.com/xuxiaoxxxx/CPR.

</details>

## 103. Temporal Difference Learning of Probabilistic Reach-Avoid Value Functions

**中文标题**: Temporal Difference Learning of Probabilistic Reach-Avoid Value Functions

**作者**: Elias Fontanari, Pietro Noah Crestaz, Matteo Saveriano, Andrea Del Prete

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28106v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28106v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28106v1)

**类别**: cs.RO, eess.SY

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reach-avoid sets, defining the states from which a task can be completed while avoiding unsafe regions, are fundamental tools for deploying robots in environments subject to disturbances and uncertainty, as well as for runtime task-feasibility monitoring, allowing prediction of whether a state is safe and will lead to successful task completion. Classical Hamilton-Jacobi Reachability (HJR) and its recent neural extensions (e.g., Lipschitz Continuous Reachability Learning, DeepReach) treat disturbances adversarially, computing the reach-avoid set under worst-case disturbances from a bounded set. This guarantees safety but can yield overly conservative sets when disturbances are stochastic rather than adversarial, and additionally requires the disturbance set to be known or specified in advance.   We propose Probabilistic reach-avoid Value Function (PVF), a self-supervised method, applicable to black-box systems, for computing reach-avoid sets as the states with a certain reach-avoid probability under stochastic, potentially unbounded disturbances. We evaluate our approach against HJR-based baselines on systems of increasing complexity, up to a Hopper locomotion task, and show that our probabilistic sets are less conservative and more accurate than adversarial HJR sets when disturbances are non-adversarial.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reach-avoid sets, defining the states from which a task can be completed while avoiding unsafe regions, are fundamental tools for deploying robots in environments subject to disturbances and uncertainty, as well as for runtime task-feasibility monitoring, allowing prediction of whether a state is safe and will lead to successful task completion. Classical Hamilton-Jacobi Reachability (HJR) and its recent neural extensions (e.g., Lipschitz Continuous Reachability Learning, DeepReach) treat disturbances adversarially, computing the reach-avoid set under worst-case disturbances from a bounded set. This guarantees safety but can yield overly conservative sets when disturbances are stochastic rather than adversarial, and additionally requires the disturbance set to be known or specified in advance.   We propose Probabilistic reach-avoid Value Function (PVF), a self-supervised method, applicable to black-box systems, for computing reach-avoid sets as the states with a certain reach-avoid probability under stochastic, potentially unbounded disturbances. We evaluate our approach against HJR-based baselines on systems of increasing complexity, up to a Hopper locomotion task, and show that our probabilistic sets are less conservative and more accurate than adversarial HJR sets when disturbances are non-adversarial.

</details>

## 104. EchoNav: Evidential BEV Completion from Historical Observations for Visual Navigation

**中文标题**: EchoNav: Evidential BEV Completion from Historical Observations for Visual Navigation

**作者**: Diwen Liu, Yiyang Shao, Borui Wang, Chi Zhang, Yunhui Liu

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27616v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27616v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27616v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Visual navigation in cluttered environments requires robots to recover previously observed structure and infer obstacles and free space that remain occluded. We present a framework that combines temporal aggregation and occupancy completion in an evidential bird's-eye-view (BEV) representation predicted from short RGB sequences. A decoder adapts frozen VGGT-Omega features and combines historical residual proposals using cellwise weights and a learned no-update option. The BEV predictor produces a local map in the latest ego frame without depth or camera-pose inputs or explicit geometric warping. The evidential model treats observed free space separately and predicts occupancy with associated confidence across the remaining supported region. We train the predictor on simulated RGB sequences paired with complete and visibility-masked occupancy annotations. Our experiments evaluate navigation in simulation and on a wheeled robot, with additional demonstrations on quadruped and humanoid robots. In simulated sequential two-goal navigation, our system achieves 89.6% success, outperforming the strongest evaluated baseline by 32.5 percentage points. Across 60 wheeled-robot trials in 20 scenes, it achieves 73.3% success, compared with 33.3% for the strongest evaluated baseline.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Visual navigation in cluttered environments requires robots to recover previously observed structure and infer obstacles and free space that remain occluded. We present a framework that combines temporal aggregation and occupancy completion in an evidential bird's-eye-view (BEV) representation predicted from short RGB sequences. A decoder adapts frozen VGGT-Omega features and combines historical residual proposals using cellwise weights and a learned no-update option. The BEV predictor produces a local map in the latest ego frame without depth or camera-pose inputs or explicit geometric warping. The evidential model treats observed free space separately and predicts occupancy with associated confidence across the remaining supported region. We train the predictor on simulated RGB sequences paired with complete and visibility-masked occupancy annotations. Our experiments evaluate navigation in simulation and on a wheeled robot, with additional demonstrations on quadruped and humanoid robots. In simulated sequential two-goal navigation, our system achieves 89.6% success, outperforming the strongest evaluated baseline by 32.5 percentage points. Across 60 wheeled-robot trials in 20 scenes, it achieves 73.3% success, compared with 33.3% for the strongest evaluated baseline.

</details>

## 105. PaPi: Parallel Picard-Duhamel Integration for Fast Neuro-Spectral Architectures

**中文标题**: PaPi: Parallel Picard-Duhamel Integration for Fast Neuro-Spectral Architectures

**作者**: Arthur Bizzi, Márcio Marques, Kleyton Da Costa, Vitor Balestro, Tiago Novello et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28591v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28591v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28591v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Neuro-spectral architectures (NeuSA) are a class of physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) that have proven effective for solving time-dependent PDEs in the frequency domain. However, training and inference often rely on costly numerical integration of the learned vector field, typically using sequential Runge-Kutta time stepping, which limits temporal parallelism. We introduce PaPi, an integration scheme inspired by weakly nonlinear flows that enables time-Parallel inference through exponential integration and a Duhamel-Picard scheme. We show that the Duhamel-Picard iteration converges exponentially for all flows, and provide explicit error bounds that depend on how close the flow is to linear. Experiments show that PaPi accelerates training and inference by up to two orders of magnitude, achieving training times of under one second for low-dimensional PDE learning problems, while maintaining competitive predictive accuracy.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Neuro-spectral architectures (NeuSA) are a class of physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) that have proven effective for solving time-dependent PDEs in the frequency domain. However, training and inference often rely on costly numerical integration of the learned vector field, typically using sequential Runge-Kutta time stepping, which limits temporal parallelism. We introduce PaPi, an integration scheme inspired by weakly nonlinear flows that enables time-Parallel inference through exponential integration and a Duhamel-Picard scheme. We show that the Duhamel-Picard iteration converges exponentially for all flows, and provide explicit error bounds that depend on how close the flow is to linear. Experiments show that PaPi accelerates training and inference by up to two orders of magnitude, achieving training times of under one second for low-dimensional PDE learning problems, while maintaining competitive predictive accuracy.

</details>

## 106. GraphCast-Mamba: Learning Non-Markovian Corrections for Weather Forecasting

**中文标题**: GraphCast-Mamba: Learning Non-Markovian Corrections for Weather Forecasting

**作者**: Ilya Vilkoviskiy, Liang-Hong Mo, Suozhi Huang, Dmitry A. Abanin

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28512v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28512v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28512v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Unresolved atmospheric processes can introduce history dependence beyond the short input windows used by many weather forecasting models. We introduce GraphCast--Mamba, which augments a frozen, pretrained $1^\circ$ GraphCast backbone with a trainable Mamba correction branch combining graph message passing and Mamba layers. The branch retains a temporal memory state across successive six-hour forecast steps, learning corrections informed by temporal context beyond GraphCast's two-frame input. Training is confined to the correction branch, using ERA5 data from 2015--2021. After 2,500 optimizer updates, including warm-up, the selected model achieves substantial improvements relative to the uncorrected GraphCast backbone. It reduces the GraphCast loss (normalized weighted mean-squared error; MSE) by 14.42% when aggregated over the full ten-day forecast and by 21.66% at day 10 (240 h after forecast initialization). At this final forecast lead time, root-mean-square error (RMSE) decreases by 7.09--14.60% across all 11 evaluated variable groups, spanning temperature, wind, pressure, geopotential, specific humidity, and precipitation. These results demonstrate broad improvements in ten-day weather forecasts through a short correction-training schedule while retaining the pretrained backbone.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Unresolved atmospheric processes can introduce history dependence beyond the short input windows used by many weather forecasting models. We introduce GraphCast--Mamba, which augments a frozen, pretrained $1^\circ$ GraphCast backbone with a trainable Mamba correction branch combining graph message passing and Mamba layers. The branch retains a temporal memory state across successive six-hour forecast steps, learning corrections informed by temporal context beyond GraphCast's two-frame input. Training is confined to the correction branch, using ERA5 data from 2015--2021. After 2,500 optimizer updates, including warm-up, the selected model achieves substantial improvements relative to the uncorrected GraphCast backbone. It reduces the GraphCast loss (normalized weighted mean-squared error; MSE) by 14.42% when aggregated over the full ten-day forecast and by 21.66% at day 10 (240 h after forecast initialization). At this final forecast lead time, root-mean-square error (RMSE) decreases by 7.09--14.60% across all 11 evaluated variable groups, spanning temperature, wind, pressure, geopotential, specific humidity, and precipitation. These results demonstrate broad improvements in ten-day weather forecasts through a short correction-training schedule while retaining the pretrained backbone.

</details>

## 107. Geometry-Agnostic Data-Driven Discovery of Extreme Scenarios

**中文标题**: Geometry-Agnostic Data-Driven Discovery of Extreme Scenarios

**作者**: Angeline Hu, Ava K. Tse, Kaitlyn S. Yasumura, Luciana Sarabia, Malcolm Yeung et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28487v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28487v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28487v1)

**类别**: cs.LG, math-ph

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We introduce a two-stage data-driven neural framework for discovering dynamically admissible extreme scenarios. Rather than assuming prior knowledge of the governing equations, the first stage infers the effective dynamical constraints directly from observational data. When the underlying physical system possesses suitable symmetries, the learned dynamics can be represented in a geometry-agnostic form, independent of any fixed grid, coordinate system, dimensionality, and intrinsic curvature. In the second stage, a physics-informed neural-search uses this learned dynamics to uncover rare, high-impact coherent structures that may not have appeared in the original observations. We demonstrate this framework through representative examples of increasing dimensionality and geometric complexity. Starting from a traditionally learned Duffing dynamics, we find a homoclinic pulse as a plausible localized extreme scenario. Next, since the same equation also appears in the stationary nonlinear Schrodinger system, with the coordinate interpreted as space instead of time, this pulse becomes a standing soliton. We then retrain the first stage using a geometry-agnostic neural representation, enabling the learned dynamics to be lifted to higher-dimensional space to obtain higher-dimensional solitary profile, known as Townes soliton. Finally, with a Liouville system, we demonstrate that dynamics learned from data on a (1+1)-dimensional Lorentzian quasi-manifold can be transferred to a (2+0)-dimensional Euclidean manifold to identify an instanton. Our approach can be applied to other coherent spatiotemporal structures, including kinks, rogue waves, compactons, peakons, and potentially even singularities, with applications in risk analysis, nonlinear forecasting, and anomaly detection.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We introduce a two-stage data-driven neural framework for discovering dynamically admissible extreme scenarios. Rather than assuming prior knowledge of the governing equations, the first stage infers the effective dynamical constraints directly from observational data. When the underlying physical system possesses suitable symmetries, the learned dynamics can be represented in a geometry-agnostic form, independent of any fixed grid, coordinate system, dimensionality, and intrinsic curvature. In the second stage, a physics-informed neural-search uses this learned dynamics to uncover rare, high-impact coherent structures that may not have appeared in the original observations. We demonstrate this framework through representative examples of increasing dimensionality and geometric complexity. Starting from a traditionally learned Duffing dynamics, we find a homoclinic pulse as a plausible localized extreme scenario. Next, since the same equation also appears in the stationary nonlinear Schrodinger system, with the coordinate interpreted as space instead of time, this pulse becomes a standing soliton. We then retrain the first stage using a geometry-agnostic neural representation, enabling the learned dynamics to be lifted to higher-dimensional space to obtain higher-dimensional solitary profile, known as Townes soliton. Finally, with a Liouville system, we demonstrate that dynamics learned from data on a (1+1)-dimensional Lorentzian quasi-manifold can be transferred to a (2+0)-dimensional Euclidean manifold to identify an instanton. Our approach can be applied to other coherent spatiotemporal structures, including kinks, rogue waves, compactons, peakons, and potentially even singularities, with applications in risk analysis, nonlinear forecasting, and anomaly detection.

</details>

---

## 108. Region-Specific EEG Classification of Hypnotic Susceptibility Using a Sliding-Window 2D CNN: A Subject-Independent Evaluation

**中文标题**: Region-Specific EEG Classification of Hypnotic Susceptibility Using a Sliding-Window 2D CNN: A Subject-Independent Evaluation

**作者**: Krzysztof Wrobel, Grzegorz M. Wojcik

**发布日期**: 2026-10-20

**arXiv ID**: [2610.28427v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28427v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.28427v1)

**类别**: cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

We investigate whether a 2D Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) trained on minimally preprocessed EEG signals can classify hypnotic susceptibility across distinct brain regions. Sixty-two male participants (32 Control, 30 Guided Imagery / Hipno group) underwent a 20-minute EEG recording using a 256-channel EGI cap. A sliding-window approach evaluates all 10-second windows within the recording for each of seven predefined brain regions (prefrontal, frontal, temporal, central, parietal, occipital, and facial), yielding up to 868 independently trained models. Initial screening with a segment-level 90/10 random split showed that five regions exceeded the 0.67 accuracy threshold, with the occipital region achieving the highest mean accuracy (0.721) across 90.3% of windows. Subject-independent evaluation via Leave-One-Subject-Out (LOSO) cross-validation, however, substantially revised this picture: occipital performance collapsed to near-chance (mean ACC 0.453, AUC 0.429), revealing that high random-split accuracy was driven by subject-specific EEG fingerprints rather than genuine group differences. In contrast, the frontal and prefrontal regions maintained above-chance LOSO accuracy (0.646 and 0.624, respectively), demonstrating genuine generalisability. These results underscore the necessity of subject-independent validation in EEG-based classification studies and highlight anterior cortical regions as more reliable neural markers of hypnotic susceptibility.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

We investigate whether a 2D Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) trained on minimally preprocessed EEG signals can classify hypnotic susceptibility across distinct brain regions. Sixty-two male participants (32 Control, 30 Guided Imagery / Hipno group) underwent a 20-minute EEG recording using a 256-channel EGI cap. A sliding-window approach evaluates all 10-second windows within the recording for each of seven predefined brain regions (prefrontal, frontal, temporal, central, parietal, occipital, and facial), yielding up to 868 independently trained models. Initial screening with a segment-level 90/10 random split showed that five regions exceeded the 0.67 accuracy threshold, with the occipital region achieving the highest mean accuracy (0.721) across 90.3% of windows. Subject-independent evaluation via Leave-One-Subject-Out (LOSO) cross-validation, however, substantially revised this picture: occipital performance collapsed to near-chance (mean ACC 0.453, AUC 0.429), revealing that high random-split accuracy was driven by subject-specific EEG fingerprints rather than genuine group differences. In contrast, the frontal and prefrontal regions maintained above-chance LOSO accuracy (0.646 and 0.624, respectively), demonstrating genuine generalisability. These results underscore the necessity of subject-independent validation in EEG-based classification studies and highlight anterior cortical regions as more reliable neural markers of hypnotic susceptibility.

</details>

## 109. PRIMFLOW: Learning Trajectory Primitives for Few-Step Distillation

**中文标题**: PRIMFLOW: Learning Trajectory Primitives for Few-Step Distillation

**作者**: Hongyi Fang, Benjia Zhou

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27146v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27146v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27146v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Few-step trajectory distillation aims to learn accurate global updates from dense local supervision along a teacher's sampling trajectory. Existing approaches construct within-step dynamics using specific trajectory parameterizations or discrete interval predictions, tying the learned update to a prescribed temporal structure. We propose \method, which directly learns a continuous trajectory primitive representing cumulative displacement along the teacher trajectory. By supervising finite differences of the primitive with local teacher velocities, \method learns the accumulated motion directly, while obtaining global updates through endpoint differences. This formulation avoids prescribing a temporal basis or fixed query grid and instead learns the temporal evolution of cumulative displacement from local supervision. We further establish a local-to-global error bound to characterize how local regression errors affect full-interval displacement accuracy, and introduce endpoint absorption to eliminate the auxiliary primitive branch while preserving one-network-evaluation inference. Experiments on large-scale text-to-image generation show that \method achieves a T-FID of 20.92 and an HPSv2 score of 28.35 at one NFE, improving both metrics over a trajectory-distillation baseline. After endpoint absorption, it retains comparable performance, achieving a T-FID of 20.86 and an HPSv2 score of 28.33.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Few-step trajectory distillation aims to learn accurate global updates from dense local supervision along a teacher's sampling trajectory. Existing approaches construct within-step dynamics using specific trajectory parameterizations or discrete interval predictions, tying the learned update to a prescribed temporal structure. We propose \method, which directly learns a continuous trajectory primitive representing cumulative displacement along the teacher trajectory. By supervising finite differences of the primitive with local teacher velocities, \method learns the accumulated motion directly, while obtaining global updates through endpoint differences. This formulation avoids prescribing a temporal basis or fixed query grid and instead learns the temporal evolution of cumulative displacement from local supervision. We further establish a local-to-global error bound to characterize how local regression errors affect full-interval displacement accuracy, and introduce endpoint absorption to eliminate the auxiliary primitive branch while preserving one-network-evaluation inference. Experiments on large-scale text-to-image generation show that \method achieves a T-FID of 20.92 and an HPSv2 score of 28.35 at one NFE, improving both metrics over a trajectory-distillation baseline. After endpoint absorption, it retains comparable performance, achieving a T-FID of 20.86 and an HPSv2 score of 28.33.

</details>

## 110. LVMem: Image-Augmented Memory for Long-Horizon Question Answering

**中文标题**: LVMem: Image-Augmented Memory for Long-Horizon Question Answering

**作者**: Victoria Khoruzhaya, Maksim Kalmykov, Tuong Do Minh, Tatiana Zemskova, Yixin Zhu et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.27104v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27104v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.27104v1)

**类别**: cs.CV

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reliable language-guided perception is essential for embodied agents, where questions about objects, events, and their locations must be answered from long observation histories. Unlike conventional visual question answering, realistic robotic settings require reasoning over extended temporal horizons and persistent scene memory. We build upon the view-based memory paradigm and propose an efficient framework for long-horizon spatio-temporal question answering. While existing view-based systems typically store either text captions or visual embeddings, we extend the ReMEmbR-style memory with image paths that link each stored description to its original visual content. To make the stored annotations more informative, we replace uniform sampling during memory construction with an optical-flow-based frame selection strategy (PSFR) and store an object-centric descriptive scene representation instead of plain captions. We further equip a VLM-based agent with an adaptive retrieval tool that returns the image associated with the most relevant caption, allowing the agent to decide when visual inspection is needed. On the OC-NaVQA benchmark, LVMem outperforms ReMEmbR on binary and positional metrics with both backbones and matches or exceeds a 4D scene graph DAAAM baseline on binary accuracy, while operating on a lightweight view-based memory that requires no explicit graph construction.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reliable language-guided perception is essential for embodied agents, where questions about objects, events, and their locations must be answered from long observation histories. Unlike conventional visual question answering, realistic robotic settings require reasoning over extended temporal horizons and persistent scene memory. We build upon the view-based memory paradigm and propose an efficient framework for long-horizon spatio-temporal question answering. While existing view-based systems typically store either text captions or visual embeddings, we extend the ReMEmbR-style memory with image paths that link each stored description to its original visual content. To make the stored annotations more informative, we replace uniform sampling during memory construction with an optical-flow-based frame selection strategy (PSFR) and store an object-centric descriptive scene representation instead of plain captions. We further equip a VLM-based agent with an adaptive retrieval tool that returns the image associated with the most relevant caption, allowing the agent to decide when visual inspection is needed. On the OC-NaVQA benchmark, LVMem outperforms ReMEmbR on binary and positional metrics with both backbones and matches or exceeds a 4D scene graph DAAAM baseline on binary accuracy, while operating on a lightweight view-based memory that requires no explicit graph construction.

</details>

## 111. Temporal Dynamics and Security Assessment of SRT Protocol: A 96-Day Longitudinal Analysis of Production Streaming Infrastructure

**中文标题**: Temporal Dynamics and Security Assessment of SRT Protocol: A 96-Day Longitudinal Analysis of Production Streaming Infrastructure

**作者**: Ahmed F. K. Koysha, Ferdi Sönmez

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26402v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26402v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26402v1)

**类别**: cs.CR, cs.MM

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

This study presents a 96-day longitudinal analysis of Secure Reliable Transport (SRT) protocol behavior in a production streaming environment between Iraq and Turkey, examining 262,672 transport metric samples and 5,033,104 system resource measurements collected between October 2025 and January 2026. The research addresses temporal patterns, system-resource relationships, attack vulnerability, and anomaly detection. Results reveal significant temporal variation: Negative Acknowledgment exhibits 7.6-fold hourly variation and 11.5-fold daily variation (p < 0.001), though with small effect sizes (eta^2 < 0.04), indicating that temporal period explains less than 4 percent of total variance. Central Processing Unit (CPU) utilization emerges as the strongest system-level correlate of transport variation (r = 0.617 with NAK rate). Controlled User Datagram Protocol (UDP) flood experiments at 500 packets per second cause CPU increases of 176 to 806 percent, while SRT transport metrics change by only plus or minus 13 percent demonstrating protocol resilience through the Automatic Repeat Request mechanism. An unexpected anomaly reaching 97.7 percent CPU enabled signature-based differentiation between attack types. A Z-score anomaly detection method outperforms fixed thresholds, detecting all 7 security events. This study contributes the longest temporal characterization of SRT metrics reported to date, the first system-SRT correlation analysis, and the first controlled security assessment of SRT infrastructure. These findings provide actionable guidance for capacity planning, security hardening, and multi-metric monitoring of production SRT deployments.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

This study presents a 96-day longitudinal analysis of Secure Reliable Transport (SRT) protocol behavior in a production streaming environment between Iraq and Turkey, examining 262,672 transport metric samples and 5,033,104 system resource measurements collected between October 2025 and January 2026. The research addresses temporal patterns, system-resource relationships, attack vulnerability, and anomaly detection. Results reveal significant temporal variation: Negative Acknowledgment exhibits 7.6-fold hourly variation and 11.5-fold daily variation (p < 0.001), though with small effect sizes (eta^2 < 0.04), indicating that temporal period explains less than 4 percent of total variance. Central Processing Unit (CPU) utilization emerges as the strongest system-level correlate of transport variation (r = 0.617 with NAK rate). Controlled User Datagram Protocol (UDP) flood experiments at 500 packets per second cause CPU increases of 176 to 806 percent, while SRT transport metrics change by only plus or minus 13 percent demonstrating protocol resilience through the Automatic Repeat Request mechanism. An unexpected anomaly reaching 97.7 percent CPU enabled signature-based differentiation between attack types. A Z-score anomaly detection method outperforms fixed thresholds, detecting all 7 security events. This study contributes the longest temporal characterization of SRT metrics reported to date, the first system-SRT correlation analysis, and the first controlled security assessment of SRT infrastructure. These findings provide actionable guidance for capacity planning, security hardening, and multi-metric monitoring of production SRT deployments.

</details>

## 112. Applicability and Generalization of Audio Fingerprinting Methods to Speech

**中文标题**: Applicability and Generalization of Audio Fingerprinting Methods to Speech

**作者**: Kemal Altwlkany, Elmedin Selmanovic, Sead Delalić, Emanuel Lacic

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26112v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26112v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26112v1)

**类别**: cs.SD, cs.MM, eess.AS

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Audio fingerprinting methods identify audio based on its content, even when the content has been possibly corrupted. Despite their applicability and usage in the speech domain, research and advancements in audio fingerprinting traditionally focus on music and are almost exclusively evaluated on music. We provide insights into speech-oriented audio fingerprinting by benchmarking the best performing audio fingerprinting models on speech data while taking into consideration the effects of noise, reverberation, temporal changes, pitch modifications, and lossy compression. Our findings indicate that audio fingerprinting methods based on self-supervised learning outperform classical approaches on speech data, while the performance of these models on speech is inferior compared to the performance that they achieve on music. Results also include an analysis of false positives, showing that fingerprinting methods are more likely to match with an audio of the same speaker rather than an audio of the same text, when mismatches occur.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Audio fingerprinting methods identify audio based on its content, even when the content has been possibly corrupted. Despite their applicability and usage in the speech domain, research and advancements in audio fingerprinting traditionally focus on music and are almost exclusively evaluated on music. We provide insights into speech-oriented audio fingerprinting by benchmarking the best performing audio fingerprinting models on speech data while taking into consideration the effects of noise, reverberation, temporal changes, pitch modifications, and lossy compression. Our findings indicate that audio fingerprinting methods based on self-supervised learning outperform classical approaches on speech data, while the performance of these models on speech is inferior compared to the performance that they achieve on music. Results also include an analysis of false positives, showing that fingerprinting methods are more likely to match with an audio of the same speaker rather than an audio of the same text, when mismatches occur.

</details>

## 113. Physical Words: A Vocabulary for Structuring Latent Reasoning in Robot Manipulation

**中文标题**: Physical Words: A Vocabulary for Structuring Latent Reasoning in Robot Manipulation

**作者**: Jiaming Liu, Xinghao Zhu, Yiming Zhang, Shanghang Zhang, Yinxi Wang et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26854v3](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26854v3)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26854v3)

**项目主页**: https://nvidia-isaac.github.io/video_to_data/physicalword/

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Multimodal reasoning provides robotic foundation models with predictive context needed for reliable action generation. However, language-based reasoning captures task semantics but struggles to express fine-grained physical dynamics, while latent reasoning models future state evolution but offers limited structure for organizing these dynamics into reusable reasoning primitives. To bridge this gap, we introduce PhysWord, a reasoning-before-acting foundation model built on Physical Words, which use atomic action verbs as shared semantic anchors for continuous representations of physical dynamics. Specifically, PhysWord predicts upcoming atomic action words and directly aligns their contextual hidden states with latent targets derived from the corresponding future observations. Building on these primitives, we construct a temporally structured PhysWord space with visual and optional tactile variants of each action word. Autoregressively predicting Physical Words across future timesteps captures transitions between execution stages, providing structured multimodal context for action generation. PhysWord achieves state-of-the-art success rates of 90.3\% on visuotactile UniVTAC and 99.6\% on vision-based LIBERO-Long, and outperforms competing methods on real-world tasks using bimanual grippers and dexterous hands. Further evaluations demonstrate that our structured latent reasoning framework generalizes to unseen scenarios and enables long-horizon manipulation. Project page: https://nvidia-isaac.github.io/video_to_data/physicalword/

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

However, language-based reasoning captures task semantics but struggles to express fine-grained physical dynamics, while latent reasoning models future state evolution but offers limited structure for organizing these dynamics into reusable reasoning primitives. Specifically, PhysWord predicts upcoming atomic action words and directly aligns their contextual hidden states with latent targets derived from the corresponding future observations. Building on these primitives, we construct a temporally structured PhysWord space with visual and optional tactile variants of each action word. Further evaluations demonstrate that our structured latent reasoning framework generalizes to unseen scenarios and enables long-horizon manipulation. Project page: https://nvidia-isaac.github.io/video_to_data/physicalword/

</details>

## 114. CoopUQ: Robust Multi-Robot Social Navigation via Uncertainty-Aware Cooperative Forecasting

**中文标题**: CoopUQ: Robust Multi-Robot Social Navigation via Uncertainty-Aware Cooperative Forecasting

**作者**: Zehao Wang, Jianpeng Yao, Yuping Wang, Yiping Li, Jiaqi Li et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26852v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26852v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26852v1)

**项目主页**: https://coopuq.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Reinforcement learning enables mobile robots to make fast and scalable navigation decisions in constrained human-shared environments. However, directly extending single-robot approaches to multi-robot settings often fails to ensure safety and efficiency, especially under occlusions and out-of-distribution (OOD) scenarios, where individual robots may overlook potential risks or make inaccurate predictions. Leveraging cooperation effectively for both prediction and navigation remains a major challenge. We propose CoopUQ, a multi-robot social navigation framework that bridges uncertainty-aware cooperative occupancy forecasting and robust decision making. CoopUQ predicts future occupancy grid maps through cross-robot spatiotemporal feature fusion and further leverages forecasts and their uncertainty to guide policy via constrained multi-agent reinforcement learning, where the cost penalizes navigation through regions predicted to be occupied or highly uncertain. This design enables the policy to adapt its risk sensitivity under distribution shifts. Experimental results across multiple Unity3D-based simulation environments with both in-distribution and OOD settings, including diverse layouts, pedestrian densities, and rushing behavior, demonstrate improved prediction performance and safety over representative baselines. Real-robot experiments further demonstrate its robustness across various scenes. Our code and videos are available at https://coopuq.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Reinforcement learning enables mobile robots to make fast and scalable navigation decisions in constrained human-shared environments. However, directly extending single-robot approaches to multi-robot settings often fails to ensure safety and efficiency, especially under occlusions and out-of-distribution (OOD) scenarios, where individual robots may overlook potential risks or make inaccurate predictions. Leveraging cooperation effectively for both prediction and navigation remains a major challenge. We propose CoopUQ, a multi-robot social navigation framework that bridges uncertainty-aware cooperative occupancy forecasting and robust decision making. CoopUQ predicts future occupancy grid maps through cross-robot spatiotemporal feature fusion and further leverages forecasts and their uncertainty to guide policy via constrained multi-agent reinforcement learning, where the cost penalizes navigation through regions predicted to be occupied or highly uncertain. This design enables the policy to adapt its risk sensitivity under distribution shifts. Experimental results across multiple Unity3D-based simulation environments with both in-distribution and OOD settings, including diverse layouts, pedestrian densities, and rushing behavior, demonstrate improved prediction performance and safety over representative baselines. Real-robot experiments further demonstrate its robustness across various scenes. Our code and videos are available at https://coopuq.github.io/.

</details>

## 115. PICA-WAM: Visual Perturbation Invariance via Cross-Appearance Self-Alignment in World Action Models

**中文标题**: PICA-WAM: Visual Perturbation Invariance via Cross-Appearance Self-Alignment in World Action Models

**作者**: Yude Zou, Yifei Yao, Guanbo Jiao, Jianming Ma, Guanjie Zheng et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26813v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26813v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26813v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/YudeZou/PICA-WAM-release

**类别**: cs.CV, cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

World Action Models (WAMs) jointly predict future video and generate actions, yet remain sensitive to task-irrelevant visual perturbations, i.e., changes in visual appearance that should not affect robot behavior. We identify a gap in conventional WAM training: video prediction encourages representations to preserve appearance information, while action generation does not explicitly enforce invariance to task-irrelevant appearance. Data augmentation exposes WAMs to such perturbations but does not require perturbed and nominal observations to produce consistent action representations, and alignment with external pretrained features does not guarantee the desired invariance. To address this gap, we propose PICA-WAM, a self-supervised training framework that casts perturbation invariance as an auxiliary cross-appearance self-alignment task for WAMs. An exponential-moving-average (EMA) teacher processes the nominal observation to provide a deeper-layer target, while the online WAM processes a perturbed observation and aligns its shallower-layer action representation to this target through a lightweight projection head. Across four strong WAMs evaluated on RoboTwin 2.0, LIBERO-Plus, EBench, and a real robot, PICA-WAM consistently improves robustness under visual perturbations, preserves nominal performance in simulation, and raises the real robot success rate. PICA-WAM further reduces the sensitivity of video features to task-irrelevant appearance and its propagation into actions, as shown by paired layer-wise diagnostics. Code: https://github.com/YudeZou/PICA-WAM-release

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

World Action Models (WAMs) jointly predict future video and generate actions, yet remain sensitive to task-irrelevant visual perturbations, i.e., changes in visual appearance that should not affect robot behavior. We identify a gap in conventional WAM training: video prediction encourages representations to preserve appearance information, while action generation does not explicitly enforce invariance to task-irrelevant appearance. Data augmentation exposes WAMs to such perturbations but does not require perturbed and nominal observations to produce consistent action representations, and alignment with external pretrained features does not guarantee the desired invariance. Across four strong WAMs evaluated on RoboTwin 2.0, LIBERO-Plus, EBench, and a real robot, PICA-WAM consistently improves robustness under visual perturbations, preserves nominal performance in simulation, and raises the real robot success rate. PICA-WAM further reduces the sensitivity of video features to task-irrelevant appearance and its propagation into actions, as shown by paired layer-wise diagnostics. Code: https://github.com/YudeZou/PICA-WAM-release

</details>

## 116. RoboValue: A Fine-Grained Sim-and-Real Benchmark for Unified Evaluation of Robotic Value Models

**中文标题**: RoboValue: A Fine-Grained Sim-and-Real Benchmark for Unified Evaluation of Robotic Value Models

**作者**: Shengbang Liu, Zhengye Du, Zhilong Wan, Honghao Su, Chenxiang Xia et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26663v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26663v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26663v1)

**项目主页**: https://robovalue-benchmark.github.io/.

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robotic value models assess task execution from visual observations and instructions, providing feedback for policy learning and execution monitoring. However, existing evaluations cover only selected aspects of execution understanding, leaving the reliability of value feedback insufficiently tested. We introduce RoboValue, a fine-grained sim-and-real benchmark for unified evaluation of robotic value models. Its framework evaluates four complementary capability dimensions through shared interfaces: task-state understanding, temporal progress monitoring, failure and recovery reasoning, and value consistency. Diagnostic trajectories and instruction variations test whether value judgments accurately reflect task requirements and changes throughout execution. RoboValue-Dataset covers 15 simulation and 20 real-world manipulation tasks, providing expert demonstrations for training and annotated trajectories for fine-grained evaluation. We evaluate representative robotic value models in zero-shot and one-shot settings under standard conditions and separate shifts in robot embodiment and environment. Our results reveal errors in fine-grained instruction grounding, history-dependent progress tracking, and recovery assessment, even in models with strong outcome and forward-progress scores. The same semantic subtask can also receive substantially different value gains across valid execution orders. These findings identify concrete targets for value supervision, while the dataset and public leaderboard support continued assessment of feedback reliability. The website is available at https://robovalue-benchmark.github.io/.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

## 117. CAOS-SLAM: Class-Agnostic Object-Aware Real-Time vSLAM for Dynamic Environments

**中文标题**: CAOS-SLAM: Class-Agnostic Object-Aware Real-Time vSLAM for Dynamic Environments

**作者**: Gaia Borghi, João Carlos Virgolino Soares, Angelo Bratta, Shahab Heshmati-Alamdari, Claudio Semini

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26633v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26633v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26633v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Robust visual Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (vSLAM) in dynamic environments faces challenges due to the presence of moving objects that may introduce erroneous observations, corrupt the map reconstruction and overall degrade localization accuracy. Existing vSLAM approaches often rely on predefined semantic classes or costly pixel-wise classification, limiting their robustness or applicability in real-world scenarios. This paper presents CAOS-SLAM, a dynamic vSLAM framework that extends ORB-SLAM3 with a hybrid perception pipeline to detect, track, and filter both semantically known and previously unseen moving objects in real-time. On-demand panoptic segmentation is performed for object discovery, with candidate unknown objects instantiated through depth-based clustering of unclassified regions. Between panoptic updates, persistent object tracks are updated using lightweight detection when available or temporal mask propagation based on world-frame Kalman prediction, depth consistency, and appearance similarity. The estimated object motion is then used to reject dynamic features from the SLAM pipeline. Experimental results on multiple dynamic RGB-D datasets and real-world experiments demonstrate improved localization accuracy over state-of-the-art dynamic vSLAM approaches, achieving up to 59% lower localization error in scenes characterized by unknown dynamic objects and up to 88% lower error in heavily occluded scenes, while achieving a median processing rate of 30 FPS on a laptop. Furthermore, the tracking module provides estimates of object trajectories and velocities, supporting potential future applications in perception, navigation, and motion-planning tasks in dynamic environments. Code and dataset will be released upon acceptance.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Robust visual Simultaneous Localization and Mapping (vSLAM) in dynamic environments faces challenges due to the presence of moving objects that may introduce erroneous observations, corrupt the map reconstruction and overall degrade localization accuracy. Existing vSLAM approaches often rely on predefined semantic classes or costly pixel-wise classification, limiting their robustness or applicability in real-world scenarios. This paper presents CAOS-SLAM, a dynamic vSLAM framework that extends ORB-SLAM3 with a hybrid perception pipeline to detect, track, and filter both semantically known and previously unseen moving objects in real-time. On-demand panoptic segmentation is performed for object discovery, with candidate unknown objects instantiated through depth-based clustering of unclassified regions. Between panoptic updates, persistent object tracks are updated using lightweight detection when available or temporal mask propagation based on world-frame Kalman prediction, depth consistency, and appearance similarity. The estimated object motion is then used to reject dynamic features from the SLAM pipeline. Experimental results on multiple dynamic RGB-D datasets and real-world experiments demonstrate improved localization accuracy over state-of-the-art dynamic vSLAM approaches, achieving up to 59% lower localization error in scenes characterized by unknown dynamic objects and up to 88% lower error in heavily occluded scenes, while achieving a median processing rate of 30 FPS on a laptop. Furthermore, the tracking module provides estimates of object trajectories and velocities, supporting potential future applications in perception, navigation, and motion-planning tasks in dynamic environments. Code and dataset will be released upon acceptance.

</details>

---

## 118. ReInsVLN: Revisiting Historical Evidence for Training-Free Vision-and-Language Navigation

**中文标题**: ReInsVLN: Revisiting Historical Evidence for Training-Free Vision-and-Language Navigation

**作者**: Siwei Cheng, Hao Wu, Zitong Qi, Xinming Zhang, Haoyu Xi et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26545v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26545v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26545v1)

**类别**: cs.RO

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Vision-and-language navigation (VLN) relies on retaining and using relevant observations. Yet growing histories introduce redundancy, and retained information may remain underused. We introduce ReInsBench, a diagnostic benchmark that examines history utilization through controlled continuation trials. Its results show that history-review reinstruction can recover navigation failures using the existing task context. Motivated by this finding, we propose ReInsVLN, a training-free framework supporting memory construction and utilization in frozen navigators. Coverage- and Instruction-Aware Compression constructs compact visual context through temporal redundancy reduction, coverage-aware protection, and instruction-guided token selection. Forgetting-Inspired History Reminders guide navigators to revisit retained observations according to a predefined schedule, without additional model calls. On R2R Val-Unseen, ReInsVLN improves success rates by 8.48 and 3.37 percentage points over the native StreamVLN and InternVLA-N1, reaching 64.27% and 64.44%, respectively. Visual compression largely preserves navigation performance while reducing prefill latency and KV cache memory by up to 21.77% and 32.25%, respectively, on matched observation workloads. Real-world trials on humanoid and quadruped robots further support the applicability of ReInsVLN to sequential instruction following and ordinal navigation.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Real-world trials on humanoid and quadruped robots further support the applicability of ReInsVLN to sequential instruction following and ordinal navigation.

</details>

## 119. Learn the Task, Not the Schedule: Reducing Spurious Cross-Arm Coupling in Bimanual Imitation Learning

**中文标题**: Learn the Task, Not the Schedule: Reducing Spurious Cross-Arm Coupling in Bimanual Imitation Learning

**作者**: Shuyuan Hu, Deng-Ke Wu, Yifan Jin, Qianhang Luo, Mingchen Ju et al.

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26507v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26507v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26507v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/MINT-SJTU/CTR.

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.AI, cs.LG

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

A bimanual demonstration specifies both what the robot should do and one particular schedule for when each arm should act. Standard imitation learning does not distinguish task-required cross-arm constraints from incidental scheduling choices, and may therefore exploit opposite-arm progress as a shortcut cue, creating spurious cross-arm coupling even where relative timing is irrelevant for the current task phase. We introduce Constraint-preserving Temporal Recombination (CTR), a training-time data intervention that preserves recorded arm-local sensorimotor content while retiming the corresponding segments to realize admissible schedules that preserve task-required cross-arm constraints. We use CTR to train Diffusion Policy, Action Chunking with Transformers, and $π_{0.5}$, and evaluate them on four simulated and four real-robot tasks. Across six real-robot bimanual progress-state initializations, CTR raises worst-state success from 56% for the strongest non-CTR schedule mixture to 76%. With $π_{0.5}$ on an Independent task, a 4 s pause transfers 2.56 s of delay to the opposite arm under the Concurrent schedule distribution, but only 0.25 s with CTR. On real robots, CTR violates task constraints in only 0--4% of trials, versus 36--98% for narrow-schedule baselines. Code will be released at https://github.com/MINT-SJTU/CTR.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Standard imitation learning does not distinguish task-required cross-arm constraints from incidental scheduling choices, and may therefore exploit opposite-arm progress as a shortcut cue, creating spurious cross-arm coupling even where relative timing is irrelevant for the current task phase. We introduce Constraint-preserving Temporal Recombination (CTR), a training-time data intervention that preserves recorded arm-local sensorimotor content while retiming the corresponding segments to realize admissible schedules that preserve task-required cross-arm constraints. We use CTR to train Diffusion Policy, Action Chunking with Transformers, and $π_{0.5}$, and evaluate them on four simulated and four real-robot tasks. Across six real-robot bimanual progress-state initializations, CTR raises worst-state success from 56% for the strongest non-CTR schedule mixture to 76%. With $π_{0.5}$ on an Independent task, a 4 s pause transfers 2.56 s of delay to the opposite arm under the Concurrent schedule distribution, but only 0.25 s with CTR. On real robots, CTR violates task constraints in only 0--4% of trials, versus 36--98% for narrow-schedule baselines. Code will be released at https://github.com/MINT-SJTU/CTR.

</details>

## 120. K-AoI: A Spatio-Temporal Synchronization Metric for Digital Twin-based Teleoperation in Dynamic Network Environments

**中文标题**: K-AoI: A Spatio-Temporal Synchronization Metric for Digital Twin-based Teleoperation in Dynamic Network Environments

**作者**: Lu Tian, Zhenhui Yuan, Zexin Deng, Wenjuan Zhang

**发布日期**: 2026-10-19

**arXiv ID**: [2610.26201v1](http://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26201v1)

**PDF**: [下载链接](http://arxiv.org/pdf/2610.26201v1)

**代码仓库**: https://github.com/luziyaotian/kaoi-testbed.

**类别**: cs.RO, cs.NI

<details>
<summary><b>摘要 (Abstract)</b></summary>

Synchronization between a digital twin (DT) and its physical robot is critical for effective teleoperation, as mismatches directly degrade operator awareness and task performance. Existing synchronization metrics are predominantly time-based and rarely capture the spatial consequences of delayed information during motion. In this paper, we introduce Kinematic Age of Information (K-AoI), a spatio-temporal metric that weights AoI by instantaneous Cartesian velocity, translating temporal staleness into physically meaningful spatial divergence. We implement and evaluate K-AoI on a DT teleoperation testbed comprising a six-degrees-of-freedom (6-DOF) manipulator, a Unity-based twin, and controlled network impairments using tc netem across seven network conditions. Per-sample analysis shows that K-AoI exhibits rank-correlations with AoI at $ρ\leq 0.65$ across all networks, dropping to $ρ= 0.22$ under satellite connections. This shows that K-AoI is not a simple transformation of AoI, but instead captures velocity-dependent degradation effects that time-only metrics cannot resolve. We further analyze AoI tail behavior using Ultra-Low AoI (ULAoI) and find that the commonly assumed Generalized Pareto Distribution (GPD) model is rejected. This reflects the impact of deterministic forwarding and bounded jitter, which violate standard extreme-value assumptions. Overall, K-AoI provides a complementary and physically grounded synchronization metric, linking network delay to task-relevant spatial error in digital-twin teleoperation systems. The dataset and testbed software are publicly available at: https://github.com/luziyaotian/kaoi-testbed.

</details>

<details>
<summary><b>中文摘要</b></summary>

Synchronization between a digital twin (DT) and its physical robot is critical for effective teleoperation, as mismatches directly degrade operator awareness and task performance. Existing synchronization metrics are predominantly time-based and rarely capture the spatial consequences of delayed information during motion. In this paper, we introduce Kinematic Age of Information (K-AoI), a spatio-temporal metric that weights AoI by instantaneous Cartesian velocity, translating temporal staleness into physically meaningful spatial divergence. We implement and evaluate K-AoI on a DT teleoperation testbed comprising a six-degrees-of-freedom (6-DOF) manipulator, a Unity-based twin, and controlled network impairments using tc netem across seven network conditions. Per-sample analysis shows that K-AoI exhibits rank-correlations with AoI at $ρ\leq 0.65$ across all networks, dropping to $ρ= 0.22$ under satellite connections. This shows that K-AoI is not a simple transformation of AoI, but instead captures velocity-dependent degradation effects that time-only metrics cannot resolve. We further analyze AoI tail behavior using Ultra-Low AoI (ULAoI) and find that the commonly assumed Generalized Pareto Distribution (GPD) model is rejected. This reflects the impact of deterministic forwarding and bounded jitter, which violate standard extreme-value assumptions. Overall, K-AoI provides a complementary and physically grounded synchronization metric, linking network delay to task-relevant spatial error in digital-twin teleoperation systems. The dataset and testbed software are publicly available at: https://github.com/luziyaotian/kaoi-testbed.

</details>
